265 — Source Map Generator

Project Number: 265

Project Name: Source Map Generator

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal & Scope

A from-scratch, production-grade Source Map v3 toolkit in Rust — the codec + model + builder + consumer + composer + index + editor + smap CLI — that build tools use to emit maps and that browsers/error-reporters use to consume them. In one definition: a Base64 VLQ codec; the source-map data model and mappings relative-delta grammar/state machine; full v3 JSON (de)serialization incl. sourcesContent/sourceRoot/ignoreList, the //# sourceMappingURL= comment and inline data: URLs; a generator/builder API; a consumer/query API (original_position_for via binary search with bias); correct map composition/chaining (original → intermediate → generated); index (sectioned) maps; and a MagicString-style source editor. Validated against the Source Map v3 spec and the reference source-map / @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec corpora; wired into the #264 bundler. It generates, decodes, composes, and queries source maps; it does not parse or transform JavaScript (that is #264).

1.1 What is built (precise definition)

A Cargo workspace of ten smap-* crates delivering the full Source Map v3 lifecycle:

Capability Owner crate Definition
Base64 VLQ integer codec smap-vlq encode_vlq/decode_vlq; 5-bit groups, continuation bit (32), sign bit (LSB of value); 64-char alphabet + reverse table
v3 data model smap-model SourceMap (JSON shape) + DecodedMap (Vec<Vec<Segment>>, outer=generated line); Segment/Position/OriginalLocation; source/name index newtypes
mappings string codec smap-codec encode_mappings/decode_mappings via running-delta EncoderState/DecoderState; field-count {1,4,5} validation; gen_column reset per ;
v3 JSON (de)serialization smap-json to_json/from_json; XSSI )]}' strip; //# sourceMappingURL= parse/emit; inline data:application/json;base64,; sourcesContent/sourceRoot/ignoreList
Generator/builder API smap-builder SourceMapBuilder: add_source/add_name interning, set_source_content, add_mapping; sort by (gen_line, gen_column); dedup; into_sourcemap
Consumer/query API smap-consumer SourceMapConsumer: original_position_for/generated_position_for via sorted indexes + binary search with Bias (GLB default / LUB); source_content_for
Composition / chaining smap-compose compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen); remap(chain) folds compose; null-segment propagation; names + sourcesContent preservation
Index (sectioned) maps smap-index IndexedSourceMap{ sections:[Section{offset,map}] }; build from concatenated chunk maps; flatten() with offset arithmetic
MagicString editor smap-edit MagicString: overwrite/append/prepend/remove/move/indent → transformed code + correct map, no re-parse
CLI smap-cli smap binary: encode, decode, validate, compose, flatten, query, inspect, extract-url

Net: this project produces, decodes, composes, and queries source maps. It is the codec + model + tooling layer — consumed by transpilers/minifiers/bundlers/TS compiler and by browsers/error-reporters/devtools.

1.2 System boundaries

upstream (EMIT)                          this project                         downstream (CONSUME)
transpiler / minifier / #264 bundler --> smap-builder / smap-edit ---------> SourceMap (smap-model)
TS compiler: code + (gen,orig) pos       smap-codec <-> "mappings" string     ^   browser stack frame
                                         smap-json <-> v3 JSON / URL / data:  |   error reporter -> orig
                                         smap-compose / smap-index            +-- smap-consumer (Bias, bsearch)
  • In scope: everything that turns (generated,original) position pairs into a valid v3 map and back, plus composition, sectioning, in-place editing, and CLI tooling.
  • Boundary — column units: internally 0-based, byte/char column convention is fixed at the model edge (§14); UTF-16-vs-byte translation is performed only at the API/CLI boundary, never inside smap-codec.
  • Boundary — 0- vs 1-based: map is 0-based; 1-based line presentation happens only at API/CLI boundary.
  • Boundary — trust: all decode paths (smap-vlq, smap-codec, smap-json) treat input as untrusted/hostile → never panic; return typed errors.

1.3 NON-goals (explicit)

Excluded Why / where it lives
Parsing/transforming JavaScript, AST work → project #264 Bundler/Transpiler; we only accept emitted (gen,orig) positions
Minification / code generation → #264; we map, we do not rewrite semantics
Debugger / devtools UI, symbolication UX out of scope; consumer API is the contract a UI would build on
WebAssembly DWARF / wasm source-map extension PhaseDeferred("§1.6")
Hosted symbolication service, network fetch of sourceMappingURL PhaseDeferred; extract_source_mapping_url returns the ref, does not fetch

1.4 Deployment model

  • Embeddable Rust library set: core crates (smap-vlq, smap-model, smap-codec, smap-builder, smap-consumer, smap-compose, smap-index) are no_std + alloc → linkable into transpilers, WASM targets, embedded error agents. smap-json, smap-edit, smap-cli are std (I/O).
  • Dependency-first: a build tool adds smap-builder to emit maps; an observability agent adds smap-consumer to map stack frames; neither pulls the CLI.
  • smap binary: standalone tooling for CI validation, inspection, manual compose/flatten/query.

1.5 Target users

  • Build-tool / compiler engineers — emit composed, minified-output maps (the SourceMapGenerator contract); model on @jridgewell/gen-mapping, SWC/OXC emitters.
  • Observability / stack-trace tooling teams — resolve production stack frames to original positions (the SourceMapConsumer / trace-mapping contract); model on Sentry's Rust sourcemap crate + Chrome DevTools/V8 consumer behavior.
  • Bundler authors (#264) — chain map composition + index maps across concatenated chunks.

1.6 Phase-0 deliverable — the ten crates

  1. smap-vlq — Base64 VLQ primitives.
  2. smap-model — SourceMap/DecodedMap/Segment/Position + error enums.
  3. smap-codec — DecodedMap ⇄ mappings string.
  4. smap-json — v3 JSON, XSSI, sourceMappingURL, data: inline.
  5. smap-builder — generator API.
  6. smap-consumer — query API.
  7. smap-compose — composition/chaining.
  8. smap-index — sectioned maps + flatten.
  9. smap-edit — MagicString editor.
  10. smap-cli — smap binary.

Phase-0 exit: workspace builds, model types + error enums compiled, CLI scaffold, CI/Makefile/Dockerfile, and the "encode one segment, decode it back to the same Segment" smoke test green.

1.7 What later phases unlock

Phases Unlocks
1–2 (§13–14) VLQ round-trip + the mappings grammar/delta semantics → correct primitive layer
3–4 (§15–16) full codec + v3 JSON/URL/data: → read & write real-world maps
5–6 (§17–18) emit maps (builder) + resolve positions (consumer) → end-to-end usable
7–8 (§19–20) compose chains + sectioned/flatten → bundler-grade output
9–10 (§21–22) MagicString editing + #264 integration (sourcesContent/sourceRoot/ignoreList policy)
11–12 (§23–24) spec conformance + fuzzing + millions-of-segments performance

1.8 Deferred features — PhaseDeferred

  • PhaseDeferred("§1.6") — WASM/DWARF source-map extension.
  • PhaseDeferred("§1.6") — debug-id / build-id extensions (Sentry-style stable map identity).
  • PhaseDeferred("§1.6") — hosted symbolication service (upload, store, server-side query).

Each is gated: a typed PhaseDeferred marker, not a silent stub; CI asserts no deferred path is reachable in shipped phases.

1.9 Timeline (estimate)

Milestone Estimate
Phase 0 (skeleton + smoke test) 6–14 weeks (1 engineer; spans codec + model + CLI scaffold)
Phases 1–6 (codec, JSON, builder, consumer usable) + ~2–3 months
Phases 7–12 (compose, index, edit, integration, conformance, perf) + ~2–4 months
Full toolkit, production bar ~6–9 months total

1.10 Recommended language — Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

Rationale (expanded from spine):

  • Hot integer/allocation paths: VLQ codec + per-line segment arrays are tight loops over millions of integers; Rust gives bounds-checked, zero-cost, branch-predictable code with no GC pause.
  • Layout + allocation control: maps may hold millions of segments; Vec<Vec<Segment>> + arena/interner control + zero-copy JSON parsing keep memory ceilings hittable (§24).
  • Untrusted-input safety: map JSON/mappings/data: URLs come from the network; Rust's memory safety + typed errors → "never panic" is enforceable, not aspirational.
  • no_std + alloc core: core crates link into WASM / embedded agents.
  • Reference parity: Sentry's Rust sourcemap, SWC/OXC emitters, @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec map cleanly onto this design.

Rejected alternatives:

Lang Rejection
JavaScript (historical source-map lib) GC + allocation overhead on large maps; no layout control
C / C++ memory-unsafe parsing of untrusted map JSON
Go weaker codec ergonomics; GC pauses on huge decodes

1.11 Required prerequisites (reader/implementer)

  • Source Map v3 spec (Source Map Revision 3 Proposal / TC39 source-map spec).
  • VLQ + Base64 (sign-bit + magnitude, not two's complement; continuation bit).
  • Delta / relative encoding (per-field reset-vs-carry rules — the #1 bug source).
  • Binary search with bias (greatest-lower-bound / least-upper-bound).
  • JSON (parse/emit, zero-copy considerations).
  • Column conventions: UTF-16 code units vs byte offsets vs char offsets, and where the boundary conversion lives.

2. Why Build This

Source maps are the debuggability contract of every compiled-to-JS toolchain: the only artifact that lets a runtime stack frame in shipped output point back to the line/column the author wrote. This project builds the codec + model + tooling that produces, decodes, composes, and queries that contract from scratch. Not a tutorial; a reference-grade implementation forcing mastery of five distinct disciplines that recur across the portfolio.

2.1 Unique knowledge it teaches

Discipline What it forces you to learn Where else it shows up
Variable-length integer codec Base64 VLQ: 5-bit groups, continuation bit (32), sign-bit + magnitude (NOT two's complement), overflow guards, alphabet + reverse table #178 Protobuf/Avro varint, #62 Lexer numeric scanning
Relative-state decoder A running-delta state machine where some fields reset per ; (gen_column) and others carry across lines (source/orig_line/orig_column/name) — the #1 bug source any stream-delta codec, RLE, DWARF line programs
Position-mapping + query structures sorted-by-generated + sorted-by-original indexes, binary search with bias (GLB default / LUB), interval lookup over millions of segments #74 Debugger, #126 JS Engine, #75 Profiler
Composition of transformations folding orig->mid->gen correctly, null-segment propagation, name "deepest mapping wins", associativity #264 Bundler/Transpiler pipeline
Format-evolution + tolerant parsing real-world maps violate the spec; ignoreList/x_google_ignoreList, XSSI prefix, inline data: URLs, missing fields, 1-field segments every wire-format parser facing untrusted input
  • VLQ is deceptively hard. Sign lives in the LSB of the value before grouping, not in any group; continuation is bit 5 of each group. Most naive implementations get sign + overflow wrong and only fail on large/negative deltas in real maps.
  • The relative-delta reset table is the whole ballgame. Encode/decode symmetry breaks the instant gen_column is carried across a ; or source is reset per line. Teaches: state machines whose invariants are which variable resets when, validated by round-trip.
  • 0-based inside, 1-based at the boundary. Lines/columns are 0-based everywhere in smap-codec/smap-model; the 1-based presentation conversion happens ONLY at the API/CLI edge. Teaches discipline of one canonical internal representation + a single conversion seam.
  • Query is a search problem, not a hash lookup. A generated position rarely hits a segment exactly; you want the greatest mapping <= query (GLB) or least >= (LUB). Teaches bias semantics + the cost of choosing the wrong sort key.
  • Composition is the non-obvious crown jewel. For each mid->gen segment, resolve its mid position through orig->mid; emit a 1-field generated-only segment when the mid position is unmapped. Teaches that a transform chain is a fold of partial functions, and that "lost the original line number" is a composition bug, not a codec bug.

2.2 Why source maps matter — historically + architecturally

  • Historical. v1 (line-only) -> v2 -> v3 (the current Base64-VLQ mappings string, column-precise, the spec everyone ships). v3 added compactness (delta + VLQ) so maps for multi-MB bundles stay tractable.
  • Architectural — the contract. Authoring code is unrecognizable after TS->JS, bundling, and minification. The source map is the inverse function the browser/error reporter applies to recover the original site of an exception or breakpoint.
  • Composition is load-bearing. A TS -> JS -> bundle -> minify pipeline produces a chain of maps. Without compose/remap, each stage's map only points one hop back; the final stack trace resolves to minified JS, not the author's TS. The whole value proposition collapses without correct chaining.
author .ts ──tsc──> .js ──bundle──> chunk.js ──minify──> out.min.js
   ^  m1: ts<-js        ^ m2: js<-chunk     ^ m3: chunk<-min
   └──────────── remap([m1,m2,m3]) : out.min.js position ──> author .ts position

2.3 Canonical prior art (modeled on, cited consistently)

Reference Role What we take
Source Map v3 spec (TC39 / "Revision 3 Proposal") the format itself envelope fields, mappings grammar, segment field counts
Mozilla source-map (SourceMapGenerator / SourceMapConsumer) the original mainstream lib the generator/consumer API shape (mirrored in smap-builder/smap-consumer)
@jridgewell/sourcemap-codec + gen-mapping + trace-mapping modern fast split the codec / generate / trace separation = our crate boundaries
Rich Harris magic-string edit-without-reparse smap-edit's interval bookkeeping + column tracking
Sentry Rust sourcemap crate production Rust prior art no_std-friendly layout, tolerant decode, index maps
Chrome DevTools + V8 consumer the consumer that must agree bias behavior, real-world quirks our differential corpus checks against

2.4 Engineering muscles built

  • Bit-level codec design: continuation/sign bits, group packing, table-driven Base64, byte-at-a-time decode, never-panic parsing of untrusted input.
  • State-machine discipline: explicit EncoderState/DecoderState reset rules verified by round-trip + differential testing.
  • Search-structure design: dual sorted indexes, binary search with bias, query latency under millions of segments.
  • Transformation algebra: associative compose, fold-based remap, null propagation, name preservation.
  • Format robustness: XSSI prefix stripping, inline data: decode, optional/missing fields, fuzz-hardened JSON + VLQ + mappings.
  • Performance at scale: zero-copy JSON, pre-sized buffers, memory ceilings, streaming, criterion benchmarks vs explicit targets.

2.5 Cross-links to the portfolio

Project Relationship
#264 Bundler/Transpiler the primary producer; consumes smap-builder/smap-compose/smap-index to emit composed maps for concatenated + minified output (Phase 10)
#126 JS Engine a consumer: maps runtime line/column back to original positions for stack frames
#74 Debugger a consumer: breakpoint + step resolution via original_position_for/generated_position_for
#75 Profiler/Tracer symbolicates sampled frames in minified code back to author source
#132 Distributed Tracing same symbolication need for client-side spans/errors across services
#178 Protobuf/Avro shares the varint/zig-zag-vs-sign-magnitude integer-encoding lessons (contrast: VLQ is sign-magnitude, protobuf zig-zag)
#62 Lexer shares byte-at-a-time scanning, position/column tracking, tolerant tokenization

2.6 Non-goals (so the scope stays a codec, not a compiler)

  • No JS parsing/transforming — that is #264; this project only records and replays positions.
  • No minification, no AST work, no devtools UI.
  • No WebAssembly DWARF / wasm source-map extension — gated behind PhaseDeferred.

2.7 Calibration value

A finished smap toolkit is a compact, high-signal calibration of staff-level skill: a complete wire format (codec + model + JSON), two non-trivial algorithmic cores (relative-delta machine + biased binary search), one genuinely subtle systems result (correct composition/flatten), and production hardening (fuzz, differential corpora, million-segment benchmarks) — all under ~10 small crates with crisp boundaries. It is the smallest project that exercises codec design, search structures, transformation algebra, and tolerant parsing simultaneously.

2.8 What mastery looks like

After building this, the reader can:

  • Design a variable-length integer codec from the bit layout up and prove it total against hostile bytes — the sign-magnitude VLQ machine of Phase 1 (§13), defensible vs protobuf zig-zag.
  • Reason about a relative-delta state machine by which variable resets when, and localize an "off-by-everything" multi-line bug with a delta-dump — Phase 3 (§15) + Failure Mode F1.
  • Choose a query structure and bias for nearest-match semantics and explain why a hashmap is the wrong tool — Phase 6 (§18).
  • Treat a transform chain as a fold of partial functions, emit null segments for unmapped hops, and prove associativity — Phase 7 (§19), the crown-jewel result.
  • Apply section-offset arithmetic losslessly (column only on the first line) and articulate index-map vs compose tradeoffs — Phase 8 (§20).
  • Edit a buffer and emit a correct map without re-parsing, tracking UTF-16 columns over UTF-8 bytes — Phase 9 (§21) + Failure Mode F6.
  • Harden a parser of untrusted input to never panic, never OOM, never overflow under fuzzing and differential corpora — Phase 11 (§23).
  • Hit million-segment throughput/memory targets via table-driven decode, zero-copy JSON, and streaming — Phase 12 (§24).

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 System Diagram

   PRODUCERS (untrusted-input not implied; we emit)                CONSUMERS (untrusted-input IMPLIED)
   transpilers · minifiers · bundler (#264) · TS compiler          browsers · error reporters · devtools · V8
        emit code + (gen,orig) positions                                 consume: stack frame -> original
                  |                                                                  ^
                  v                                                                  |
        smap-builder  /  smap-edit  ---build--->  SourceMap (smap-model)  <--query-- smap-consumer
                  |                                   ^      |                            ^ (Bias, binary search)
                  | raw segments (sorted)             |      | JSON envelope               | DecodedMap
                  v                                   |      v                             |
        smap-codec  <---encode / decode--->  "mappings" string      smap-json (v3 JSON · URL · data: inline)
                  |                                                          |
                  v                                                          | <==== TRUST BOUNDARY ====
        smap-vlq (Base64 VLQ: continuation bit + sign bit)                  (from disk / HTTP / inline code)

        smap-compose (orig->mid->gen)     smap-index (sectioned maps · flatten)     smap-cli ("smap" binary)

Dataflow: producers push (gen, orig, name) positions into smap-builder (or edit a buffer via smap-edit); into_sourcemap sorts + hands DecodedMap to smap-codec -> mappings string -> smap-vlq per integer; smap-json wraps the SourceMap envelope as v3 JSON (disk / inline data: / sourceMappingURL). Consume reverses: smap-json parse -> SourceMap -> smap-consumer (SourceMapConsumer::new decodes via smap-codec, builds sorted indexes) -> binary-searched original_position_for / generated_position_for. smap-compose + smap-index operate above the model.

3.2 Boundaries

Boundary Where Rule
Trust smap-json::from_json / decode_mappings / extract_source_mapping_url Inbound maps are UNTRUSTED (web fetch, build artifact, attacker-authored). Codec + JSON layers MUST NOT panic, MUST bound allocation, MUST return typed errors (JsonError/CodecError/VlqError). Producers (builder/edit) emit our own data -> trusted.
Concurrency none SourceMap/DecodedMap/SourceMapConsumer are immutable values post-construction; &self query methods. No shared mutable state, no locks. Per-chunk build + per-map decode parallelize trivially (caller-side rayon); cores scale linearly.
Persistence smap-json only Maps persist as JSON: a sibling .map file on disk, an inline data:application/json;base64, URL in code, or fetched over HTTP via //# sourceMappingURL=. Optional XSSI )]}'prefix. No DB; the artifact IS the map.

The trust boundary is the load-bearing safety contract: every decode path (Phase 1 VLQ, Phase 3 mappings, Phase 4 JSON) is fuzzed in Phase 11 against malformed/adversarial input with a "never panics, always typed error" invariant.

3.3 Crate Responsibilities (one per box)

smap-vlq (no_std+alloc). Lowest layer: Base64 VLQ integer codec. Owns B64: [u8;64] (A-Za-z0-9+/) + the reverse lookup table, encode_vlq(value, &mut String), decode_vlq(&[u8], &mut usize) -> Result<i64, VlqError>. Encodes via 5-bit groups low-to-high, bit 5 (=32) continuation, value-LSB sign bit (sign+magnitude, NOT two's complement). In: i64 (encode) / byte slice + cursor (decode); out: appended Base64 chars / decoded i64 + advanced cursor. Invariant: decode_vlq(encode_vlq(v))==v for all i64 in range; never panics — overflow -> VlqError::Overflow, bad char -> InvalidBase64Char, truncation -> UnexpectedEof. Foundation for Phase 1; every mappings field in Phase 3 routes through it; Phase 12 table-driven Base64 + byte-at-a-time decode optimize it.

smap-model (no_std+alloc). Pure data, zero logic. Defines Position{line,column:u32} (0-based), Segment{gen_column, source:Option<u32>, orig_line, orig_column, name:Option<u32>}, DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>) (outer = generated line), the v3 SourceMap envelope struct (version:u8==3, file, source_root, sources:Vec<Option<String>>, sources_content, names, mappings:String, ignore_list), OriginalLocation, and source/name index newtypes. In/out: none (definitions). Invariant: 0-based lines+columns internally — the 0↔1 conversion lives ONLY at the API/CLI boundary, never inside the model. Referenced by all 9 other crates; the shared vocabulary fixed in Phase 0/2.

smap-codec (no_std+alloc, dep: smap-vlq+smap-model). The relative-delta state machine. encode_mappings(&DecodedMap)->String and decode_mappings(&str)->Result<DecodedMap,CodecError>. Owns EncoderState/DecoderState carrying running deltas: prev_gen_col RESET per ;, prev_source/prev_orig_line/prev_orig_column/prev_name CARRIED ACROSS lines. In: DecodedMap / mappings str; out: mappings str / DecodedMap. Invariants: field count ∈ {1,4,5} (else BadFieldCount); ;=new generated line, ,=next segment; gen_column reset is the #1 bug surface so it is asserted. Tolerant decode of real-world maps (trailing separators, empty groups). Built in Phase 3; consumed by builder (Phase 5), consumer (Phase 6), compose/index/edit, cli.

smap-json (std, dep: smap-model). The serialization/envelope layer + only place touching I/O-ish formats. to_json(&SourceMap)->String (emits version:3), from_json(&str)->Result<SourceMap,JsonError> (strips optional XSSI )]}'), extract_source_mapping_url(code)->Option<SourceMapRef> (parses //# sourceMappingURL=, decodes inline data:application/json;base64, -> SourceMapRef::Inline, else Url). Handles sourcesContent/sourceRoot/ignoreList (x_google_ignoreList). In: JSON text / source code text; out: SourceMap / JSON text / SourceMapRef. Invariant: untrusted input -> typed JsonError (Parse/BadVersion/MissingField), never panic; version!=3 rejected. Phase 4; Phase 12 zero-copy parsing.

smap-builder (no_std+alloc, dep: smap-model+smap-codec). The SourceMapGenerator contract — what producers call while emitting. Holds sources+names interners + per-line segment buffers. add_source(&str)->u32, add_name(&str)->u32 (dedup-interned), set_source_content(source,&str), add_mapping(gen:Position, orig:Option<(u32,Position)>, name:Option<u32>), into_sourcemap(self)->SourceMap (sorts by (gen_line,gen_column), dedups, encodes via smap-codec). In: incremental positions; out: a complete SourceMap. Invariant: output segments sorted + dedup'd, every source/name index in range of sources/names. Phase 5; consumed by compose (Phase 7), index (Phase 8), edit (Phase 9), the #264 integration (Phase 10).

smap-consumer (no_std+alloc, dep: smap-model+smap-codec). The query/decoder side. SourceMapConsumer::new(SourceMap)->Result<Self,CodecError> decodes mappings and builds two sorted indexes: by-generated (line,column) and by-original (source,line,column). original_position_for(gen, Bias)->Option<OriginalLocation> binary-searches greatest gen ≤ query (Bias::GreatestLowerBound, default) or least ≥ (LeastUpperBound); generated_position_for(source,orig,Bias)->Option<Position> over the by-original index; source_content_for(source)->Option<&str>; all_generated_positions_for. In: a SourceMap; out: positions. Invariant: indexes total-ordered, query O(log n). Phase 6; basis of compose lookups (Phase 7), cli query, devtools-equivalent behavior.

smap-compose (no_std+alloc, dep: smap-consumer+smap-builder). Composition/chaining: compose(orig_to_mid:&SourceMapConsumer, mid_to_gen:&DecodedMap, mid_names:&[String])->SourceMap and remap(chain:&[SourceMap])->Result<SourceMap,CodecError> (fold compose). For each mid_to_gen segment, look up its mid (original) position in orig_to_mid; if mapped, emit gen -> deepest source/line/col(+name); if unmapped, emit a 1-field generated-only segment (null-segment propagation). Names follow the deepest mapping; sourcesContent preserved. In: a consumer + a DecodedMap (or a chain); out: a flattened SourceMap. Invariant: compose associative -> remap order-independent in result; round-trip-through-chain property. Phase 7.

smap-index (no_std+alloc, dep: smap-model+smap-builder). Sectioned (index) maps: Section{offset:Position, map:SourceMap}, IndexedSourceMap{version, file, sections:Vec<Section>}, flatten()->Result<SourceMap,CodecError>. Builds from concatenated chunk maps (each chunk's map + its byte offset in the combined output); flatten shifts each section's generated positions by offset (offset.line added to every gen line; offset.column added ONLY to the first line of the section). In: per-chunk SourceMaps + offsets; out: one flat SourceMap. Invariant: sections sorted-by-offset + non-overlapping. Phase 8; used by #264 for concatenated bundles (Phase 10).

smap-edit (std, dep: smap-builder). MagicString-style source editor: emit transformed code + a correct map WITHOUT re-parsing. MagicString over an original buffer with ordered edits — overwrite(start,end,&str), append, prepend, remove(start,end), plus move/indent — to_string() materializes output, generate_map(&MapOptions)->SourceMap derives mappings from interval bookkeeping + column tracking against the original byte ranges. In: original buffer + edit ops; out: edited string + SourceMap. Invariant: untouched intervals map 1:1 to original positions; removed ranges produce no segments; map stays consistent with to_string() output. Phase 9.

smap-cli (std, dep: all). The smap binary surfacing every capability: encode, decode, validate, compose, flatten, query (original-for / generated-for), inspect, extract-url. Translates the 0-based internal model to 1-based user-facing lines at the CLI boundary (per §3.2). In: files/stdin + flags; out: JSON/text + exit codes. Invariant: the ONLY place that does 0↔1 conversion and human formatting; pure-library crates stay 0-based. Phase 0 scaffold, fully wired by Phase 12.

4. System Constraints

Non-negotiable performance, memory, determinism, safety, and sizing contracts. Every phase's Acceptance and Performance subsections inherit these numbers. Targets are per single core, release build, on a 2020-era x86-64 / aarch64 reference machine; CI gates the criterion benchmarks (Phase 12) against the table below with a +15% regression tolerance.

4.1 Quantified targets

Class Metric Target Boundary / notes
Throughput decode_mappings (text scan) > 50 MB/s of "mappings" string byte-at-a-time, no UTF-8 validation on ASCII Base64
Throughput encode 1M-segment map -> mappings < 200 ms pre-sized String, ~10 bytes/segment avg
Throughput VLQ decode (decode_vlq) > 200M ints/s/core table-driven reverse-Base64, branch-light
Throughput VLQ encode (encode_vlq) > 150M ints/s/core 5-bit group loop, no allocation per int
Throughput from_json parse, 50 MB envelope > 80 MB/s zero-copy borrow of sources/names/mappings
Query original_position_for, p50 < 1 us after index build; binary search on by-gen index
Query original_position_for, p99 < 5 us includes Bias resolution + Option construction
Query generated_position_for, p99 < 8 us by-original index, secondary sort key
Build SourceMapConsumer::new, 1M segments < 100 ms decode + build both sorted indexes
Build into_sourcemap, 1M segments < 250 ms sort by (gen_line, gen_column) + dedup + encode
Memory DecodedMap resident ~ 20 bytes/segment Segment = 5 x u32 + Option niche packing
Memory 1M-segment map total resident < 64 MB DecodedMap + interners + index, excl. sourcesContent
Memory from_json transient overhead < 1.2x input size borrow, don't copy, source/name strings
Startup process/lib init 0 global init, 0 lazy_static tables are const; no first-call warmup

Numbers above are SLOs, not best-effort: a benchmark below target is a CI failure, not a warning. sourcesContent (original file bodies) is excluded from the 64 MB ceiling — it is unbounded user payload, accounted separately and may be dropped via builder policy (Phase 10).

4.2 Determinism

  • encode(decode(s)) == s byte-stable for any canonical input map (segments already sorted, minimal deltas). Non-canonical input is re-canonicalized, so the round-trip is idempotent after one pass, not necessarily identical on pass 1. State this distinction in conformance fixtures.
  • SourceMapBuilder output is a pure function of the add-order: same add_source / add_name / add_mapping sequence -> byte-identical mappings string + identical sources/names arrays. Interner assigns indexes in first-seen order; no hashing-order nondeterminism leaks into output.
  • to_json field order is fixed (version, file, sourceRoot, sources, sourcesContent, names, mappings, ignoreList); no map/set iteration in serialization. Reproducible builds depend on this.
  • No clocks, RNG, thread scheduling, or environment reads affect any output byte.

4.3 Size assumptions + hard caps (why: DoS + overflow safety)

Limit Cap Rationale
VLQ value range signed 32-bit (i32/u32 fields) matches spec field types; reject > 32-bit -> VlqError::Overflow before shift overflow
VLQ groups per field <= 7 groups (35 bits raw) 5 bits x 7 covers 32-bit value + sign; 8th group is malformed -> error
max sources per map 2^24 (16,777,216) bounds source-index interner + index arrays; over -> typed error
max names per map 2^24 same
max segments per map 2^28 (~268M) bounds total alloc (~5 GB at 20 B/seg is the absolute ceiling, well past 64 MB SLO regime)
max generated lines 2^28 one Vec<Segment> per line; guards DecodedMap outer Vec
max mappings string 1 GiB hard parse cap; over -> error, not OOM
max JSON envelope 2 GiB std I/O / 32-bit-offset safety

All caps are checked before allocation or arithmetic that could overflow. Exceeding a cap returns a typed error (CodecError / JsonError / VlqError), never a panic, abort, or silent truncation. Caps exist because map JSON is untrusted network input (browsers, error reporters, CI ingesting third-party maps): an attacker-controlled mappings of repeated continuation bits or a 10 GB sourcesContent must be rejected, not allow unbounded allocation (memory-exhaustion DoS) or integer overflow (logic corruption -> wrong original positions).

4.4 Recovery + framing (safety invariants)

  • Never panic on input. Parsing, decoding, or querying a malformed/hostile map returns Result<_, E> with a typed error variant; the codec and consumer contain zero unwrap/expect/panic!/indexing-without-bounds on input-derived data. Enforced by #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::panic, clippy::indexing_slicing)] in smap-vlq/smap-codec/smap-consumer + Phase 11 fuzzing (libfuzzer/AFL) asserting no panic across VLQ, JSON, and mappings corpora.
  • A truncated VLQ -> VlqError::UnexpectedEof; a non-Base64 byte -> InvalidBase64Char; a wrong field count -> CodecError::BadFieldCount; a negative resolved column -> NegativeColumn; an out-of-range source index -> SourceIndexOob. Each error names the offending byte offset for diagnostics.
  • Hostile-map framing: a corrupt or adversarial map from the network can never crash, hang, or OOM the consumer. Worst case is bounded work (capped by 4.3) returning either a typed error or a partially-decoded map under tolerant-decode policy (Phase 3) — chosen so a browser/devtools consumer degrades to "no mapping" rather than dying on one bad segment.
  • Tolerant decode is opt-in and explicit: strict mode rejects any anomaly; tolerant mode skips malformed segments and records a recoverable-warning list. The mode is a parameter, never a global flag.
  • arithmetic_overflow is impossible: all delta accumulation uses checked/saturating arithmetic on capped i32/u32, with overflow mapped to a typed error.

4.5 Platform + build constraints

  • smap-vlq, smap-model, smap-codec, smap-builder, smap-consumer, smap-compose, smap-index are #![no_std] + alloc — usable from WASM consumers and embedded error reporters with no std dependency. smap-json, smap-edit, smap-cli use std (I/O, fs).
  • No unsafe in any crate except behind an audited, feature-gated SIMD path in smap-vlq (off by default; the safe path meets the 200M ints/s target). #![forbid(unsafe_code)] elsewhere.
  • Zero non-vendored runtime dependencies in the core codec path; serialization may use a minimal hand-rolled or vendored JSON reader to preserve the zero-copy borrow guarantee (4.1) — no serde-derived intermediate allocation on the hot decode path.
  • MSRV 1.78, edition 2021; the whole workspace builds with --no-default-features for the no_std core.

5. Required Reading

Curated, scoped to this project's seams: VLQ/delta coding, source-position modeling, the v3 envelope, the codec hot loop, composition, and the column-encoding gotcha. Read for what to extract / what NOT to copy, not cover-to-cover.
