6. Design Principles

Twelve to fifteen non-negotiable principles. Each is law for every smap-* crate; phases reference them by name. Format: Title — rule; tradeoff; operational implication; failure-on-violation.

6.1 Spec is the oracle

  • Rule — the Source Map v3 specification (TC39 source-map / "Revision 3 Proposal") is the sole authority for envelope shape, VLQ encoding, and mappings grammar. Where the spec is silent or ambiguous, the tie-breaker is differential behavior against source-map (Mozilla) and @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec/gen-mapping/trace-mapping. We never invent semantics.
  • Tradeoff — locks us to legacy quirks (sign-bit VLQ, 0-based, XSSI prefix) we cannot "improve."
  • Operational — Phase 11 (## 23) runs differential decode/encode/query against the reference corpora; any divergence is a bug in smap-*, never in the corpus.
  • Violation — invent a "cleaner" delta rule → maps load in smap-consumer but produce wrong frames in Chrome DevTools / V8; silent miscorrelation in production stack traces.

6.2 Decode never panics

  • Rule — every decode entry point (decode_vlq, decode_mappings, from_json, extract_source_mapping_url, SourceMapConsumer::new) treats input as untrusted and returns Result<_, {VlqError|CodecError|JsonError}>. No panic!, unwrap, expect, array index that can trap, integer op that can overflow-panic, or unbounded recursion on hostile input.
  • Tradeoff — every hot path threads Result; no [] indexing in decode loops (use get + typed error).
  • Operational — Phase 11 fuzzes VLQ + JSON + mappings with cargo-fuzz; the property "never panics / never aborts / terminates" is a release gate.
  • Violation — one unwrap on a truncated VLQ group → an error-reporting service ingesting attacker-supplied data: maps crashes the worker; map upload becomes a DoS.

6.3 Relative-delta discipline (the reset rules are sacred)

  • Rule — segment fields are relative deltas with exactly these reset semantics, enforced identically in EncoderState/DecoderState (smap-codec):
field meaning relative to reset on ;
1 generated col prev segment, same generated line yes → 0
2 source index prev segment (any line) no (carry)
3 original line prev segment (any line) no (carry)
4 original col prev segment (any line) no (carry)
5 name index prev segment (any line) no (carry)
  • Tradeoff — encoder and decoder must hold mirror state; no field may be deltad independently of the others.
  • Operational — Phase 1/3 round-trip property decode(encode(d)) == d; cross-line carry covered by fixtures with empty generated lines (;;).
  • Violation — reset prev_source/prev_orig_line on ; (a classic bug) → every line after the first points at the wrong source/line; off-by-N grows with line count.

6.4 0-based inside, convert only at the boundary

  • Rule — all lines and columns are 0-based everywhere inside smap-vlq/-model/-codec/-builder/-consumer/-compose/-index. The 0↔1 conversion (tools present 1-based lines) happens only at the smap-cli / public-API boundary, in one place, documented per entry point.
  • Tradeoff — internal code must resist the urge to "+1 for readability"; CLI must remember to translate.
  • Operational — Position/Segment/OriginalLocation are documented 0-based; CLI query flags state 1-based input/output explicitly.
  • Violation — a stray +1 inside the codec → every queried frame is one line high; the off-by-one is invisible in unit tests that use 0-based fixtures and surfaces only in user-facing CLI output.

6.5 Composition must be exact

  • Rule — compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen) and remap(chain) introduce zero positional drift. For each mid_to_gen segment, look up its mid (original-of-mid) position in orig_to_mid and map gen → deepest original; bias choice in the inner lookup is fixed and documented (GLB), never ad hoc.
  • Tradeoff — exactness over speed; composition does O(segments) consumer queries, each binary-search.
  • Operational — Phase 7 (## 19) property: query(compose(A,B), p) == query(A, query(B, p)) for all mapped p; compose is associative, so remap folds left or right identically.
  • Violation — one off-by-one at a chain hop (e.g. wrong bias mid-chain) → a 3-stage TS→bundle→minify pipeline mislocates every frame; the error compounds, not cancels.

6.6 Null-segment propagation

  • Rule — when an intermediate (mid) position is unmapped in orig_to_mid, the composed output emits a 1-field (generated-column-only) segment, not a guess and not a dropped mapping. Holes propagate as holes.
  • Tradeoff — composed maps retain "generated-only" runs rather than fabricating plausible origins.
  • Operational — Phase 7 fixtures include intermediate gaps; flatten/compose preserve 1-field segments byte-faithfully through encode.
  • Violation — snap an unmapped position to the nearest mapped one → a debugger jumps to an unrelated source line with false confidence.

6.7 Tolerant decode, strict encode (Postel)

  • Rule — from_json/decode_mappings accept real-world-messy maps: an XSSI )]}' prefix, trailing ;, empty groups, missing optional fields, null entries in sources, absent sourcesContent, x_google_ignoreList/ignoreList aliasing, BOM. to_json/encode_mappings emit one canonical form: version:3, minimal deltas, no spurious separators, stable key order.
  • Tradeoff — decoder carries branchy leniency; bytes-in ≠ bytes-out (re-encode normalizes), so we do not promise byte-preservation, only semantic-preservation.
  • Operational — Phase 3/4 conformance; round-trip is asserted at the DecodedMap/SourceMap level, not the string level.
  • Violation — reject a trailing-semicolon map (esbuild emits these) → tool falls over on legitimate third-party output.

6.8 Bias is explicit

  • Rule — every consumer query takes an explicit Bias (GreatestLowerBound default, LeastUpperBound); the codec/consumer never silently picks one. Default is documented as GLB to match source-map/V8.
  • Tradeoff — callers must think about boundary behavior; no "just works" magic at segment edges.
  • Operational — Phase 6 (## 18) tests both biases at exact hits, between-segment gaps, before-first, after-last; CLI exposes --bias.
  • Violation — hard-code LUB inside original_position_for → queries land on the next mapping at boundaries; error reporters attribute a line to the following statement.

6.9 Zero-copy where safe

  • Rule — borrow from the input buffer wherever lifetimes permit: source_content_for returns &str; from_json slices string fields against the source text instead of eager-cloning; decode reads &[u8] byte-at-a-time without intermediate Strings.
  • Tradeoff — borrowing ties SourceMapConsumer/parsed views to the owning buffer's lifetime; owned/Cow variants exist only where the API must outlive the input.
  • Operational — Phase 12 (## 24) target: parse a 50 MB map with allocations bounded near segment count, not byte count; benchmarked with criterion.
  • Violation — clone every sourcesContent string on parse → memory blows past the ceiling on million-segment maps; GC-library-grade overhead we explicitly rejected.

6.10 sourcesContent is opt-in

  • Rule — sourcesContent (and sourceRoot) are emitted only when the builder/edit caller opts in (set_source_content, MapOptions). Default omits original source bodies.
  • Tradeoff — privacy + size by default vs. self-contained debuggability on request.
  • Operational — Phase 10 (## 22) defines embedding policy; ignoreList marks vendored sources; CLI inspect reports whether content is embedded.
  • Violation — embed sources by default → proprietary/source code leaks in a public web bundle; map size multiplies.

6.11 Determinism is a contract

  • Rule — given identical inputs, to_json/encode_mappings/into_sourcemap produce byte-identical output: stable sort (gen_line, then gen_column), deterministic intern-index assignment (insertion order), fixed JSON key order, no map/hash iteration leaking into output.
  • Tradeoff — must use ordered structures or sort before emit; cannot rely on HashMap iteration for sources/names ordering.
  • Operational — enables content-addressed caching, reproducible builds, and meaningful map diffs; Phase 5/11 assert encode(d) == encode(d) across runs and platforms.
  • Violation — order names by hash iteration → identical builds produce different maps → cache misses, noisy diffs, broken reproducible-build attestation.

6.12 Bounded resource usage

  • Rule — all decode paths are overflow-checked (decode_vlq returns VlqError::Overflow past i64/u32 range; column/line accumulation is checked) and bounded: configurable caps on total segments, sources, names, and mappings length; recursion-free parsing.
  • Tradeoff — caps can reject a pathological-but-legal giant map; tune limits per deployment.
  • Operational — Phase 11 fuzzing + Phase 12 ceilings; default caps documented; CLI surfaces a clear "limit exceeded" error, not OOM.
  • Violation — unchecked delta accumulation → a crafted map with huge deltas overflows u32 column (wraps or panics), or unbounded sources exhausts memory in a shared build service.

6.13 Names are best-effort

  • Rule — the optional name index (field 5) is preserved whenever present — through add_mapping, encode/decode, and composition (names follow the deepest available mapping). When a hop lacks a name, the composed segment simply omits it; we never fabricate names.
  • Tradeoff — composed maps may lose names that existed only at an intermediate stage; we do not back-fill.
  • Operational — Phase 7 preserves names + interns them in the output; property: a name present end-to-end survives remap.
  • Violation — drop names on every compose → minified identifiers can't be un-minified in DevTools; or fabricate a name → wrong symbol shown in a stack frame.

6.14 Index maps flatten losslessly

  • Rule — IndexedSourceMap::flatten() produces a flat SourceMap semantically equivalent to the sectioned form: sections sorted by offset, non-overlapping (invariant enforced); flatten shifts each section's generated positions by its offset (offset.line added to every generated line; offset.column added only to the first line of the section).
  • Tradeoff — flatten is O(total segments) and discards section boundaries; we keep both representations addressable.
  • Operational — Phase 8 (## 20) property: query(flatten(idx), p) == query(idx, p) for all p; overlap or unsorted sections is a typed error, not silent merge.
  • Violation — add offset.column to every line of a section (not just the first) → all but the first line of each chunk shift sideways; concatenated-bundle frames misalign per chunk.

6.15 One codec, two directions, mirror invariants

  • Rule — encode and decode are a single conceptual machine: any invariant added to EncoderState (reset/carry, field count 1/4/5, sign-bit VLQ) has a mirror in DecoderState, validated by the round-trip property rather than two independently-written code paths.
  • Tradeoff — changes touch both sides together; no "fix the decoder only."
  • Operational — Phase 3 keeps encode/decode adjacent and co-tested; BadFieldCount/NegativeColumn/SourceIndexOob are decoder-side guards mirroring encoder preconditions.
  • Violation — patch the decoder to accept 2- or 3-field segments the encoder never emits → divergence from the spec's strict 1/4/5 set; maps that round-trip locally but fail in conformant consumers.

7. System Invariants

Hard rules the toolkit must NEVER violate. Each invariant: stmt · why · enforced · detect · telemetry · recover · debug. INV-n IDs are stable, cited by phases + conformance suite (Phase 11, ## 23). Severity: CRITICAL = silent data corruption (wrong line in a stack trace), HARD = panic / refusal. Enforcement modes: debug_assert! (dev), assert!/Result (always-on), proptest/fuzz (CI), differential (CI vs @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec).

7.1 Invariant Register

ID Invariant (one-line) Severity Primary owner
INV-1 decode_vlq(encode_vlq(v)) == v for v ∈ [i32::MIN, i32::MAX] CRITICAL smap-vlq
INV-2 encode_mappings(decode_mappings(s)) semantically equal (canonical) CRITICAL smap-codec
INV-3 gen_column resets to 0 at each ;; source/orig_line/orig_column/name carry across CRITICAL smap-codec
INV-4 Every segment has 1, 4, or 5 fields — never 0/2/3 HARD smap-codec
INV-5 source < sources.len(); name < names.len(); ignoreList in-range HARD smap-model / smap-builder
INV-6 Lines + columns 0-based inside codec; 1-based only at API/CLI boundary CRITICAL all
INV-7 Consumer indexes monotonic; binary search returns exact GLB (default) / LUB CRITICAL smap-consumer
INV-8 compose associative; loses no resolvable mapping CRITICAL smap-compose
INV-9 Index sections sorted by offset + non-overlapping; flatten lossless CRITICAL smap-index
INV-10 decode_mappings/from_json/decode_vlq total — never panic on arbitrary bytes HARD smap-vlq / codec / json
INV-11 VLQ value never overflows i32 range silently — checked, Overflow returned HARD smap-vlq
INV-12 gen_column never negative; per-line segments non-decreasing in gen_column CRITICAL smap-codec / consumer
INV-13 Interner determinism: identical string → identical index; index stable for map lifetime HARD smap-builder

7.2 INV-1 — VLQ round-trip

  • Stmt: ∀ v ∈ [i32::MIN..=i32::MAX]: encode_vlq(v,&mut s); decode_vlq(s.as_bytes(),&mut 0) == Ok(v). Sign-bit+magnitude, NOT two's complement.
  • Why: every mapping field is a VLQ delta; one mis-coded char shifts every downstream column → entire generated line maps wrong.
  • Enforced: proptest over full i32 range + boundary set {0, ±1, 15, 16, 31, 32, ±2^k, i32::MIN, i32::MAX}; debug_assert! round-trip inside encode_vlq (dev).
  • Detect: Phase 1 (## 13) round-trip property; fuzz target fuzz_vlq asserts decode∘encode == id.
  • Telemetry: pure fn — none. On debug-assert fail, dump (value, emitted_chars_hex, decoded_value).
  • Recover: none — failure is a bug, not a runtime condition. Encode is infallible for in-range input.
  • Debug: bisect on failing magnitude; inspect 5-bit grouping low→high; verify bit-5 (32) continuation set on all-but-last group; verify LSB-of-value = sign. Common cause: shifting sign in before/after magnitude split.

7.3 INV-2 — mappings canonical round-trip

  • Stmt: decode_mappings(s)? then encode_mappings(&d) yields s' with decode_mappings(s')? == d. Byte-equality NOT required (messy input — leading ,, redundant fields, unsorted — normalizes to canonical).
  • Why: re-encoding must preserve meaning; tools chain encode/decode (compose, flatten) — drift accumulates into mis-attribution.
  • Enforced: Result-typed; conformance fixture pairs (messy_input, canonical_output); proptest decode∘encode∘decode == decode.
  • Detect: Phase 3 (## 15) + Phase 11 differential vs @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec (DecodedMap equality, not string equality).
  • Telemetry: decode emits segments_decoded, lines, tolerated_anomalies (empty groups, trailing comma).
  • Recover: tolerant decode (INV-10) accepts messy real-world input; canonical re-encode repairs.
  • Debug: diff DecodedMap line/segment-wise. Strings differ but DecodedMaps match → expected (canonicalization). DecodedMaps differ → delta-carry bug (suspect INV-3).

7.4 INV-3 — delta reset/carry discipline (the #1 bug source)

  • Stmt: in EncoderState/DecoderState: prev_gen_col resets to 0 at every ;; prev_source, prev_orig_line, prev_orig_column, prev_name persist across ;. Never swapped, never all-reset, never all-carried.
  • Why: canonical decoder bug. Reset orig_line per line → every line after 0 mis-attributed; carry gen_column → columns explode. Silent: produces parseable-but-wrong map.
  • Enforced: debug_assert_eq!(state.prev_gen_col, 0) immediately after ; handling in decoder; encoder symmetric. Named-field state struct (not a tuple) prevents accidental reordering.
  • Detect: golden fixtures ≥3 generated lines mapping to ascending original lines; Phase 11 differential. Property: 2-line map decoded == two independently-decoded lines with carried state seeded.
  • Telemetry: decoder trace mode logs per-segment (gen_col_abs, src, orig_line_abs, orig_col_abs, name) post-accumulation.
  • Recover: none at runtime — guarded by tests; violation is a logic bug.
  • Debug: enable trace; verify first segment of line 1 has correct absolute orig_line (= line-0 last value + delta). Reproduce with minimal 2-line, 2-segment fixture.

7.5 INV-4 — legal field counts {1,4,5}

  • Stmt: decoded segment field count ∈ {1,4,5}. 1 = generated-column-only (unmapped); 4 = gen_col+source+orig_line+orig_col; 5 = +name. Counts 0/2/3 malformed.
  • Why: 2/3-field segments have no defined semantics in Source Map v3; accepting them invents meaning.
  • Enforced: decode_mappings counts VLQs per comma-delimited segment → CodecError::BadFieldCount(n) for n∉{1,4,5}. Builder constructs only via typed add_mapping (orig present → 4 or 5).
  • Detect: fuzz fuzz_mappings must surface BadFieldCount, never panic; corpus includes 2/3-field adversarial strings.
  • Telemetry: BadFieldCount carries observed n + byte offset of segment start.
  • Recover: strict → reject whole map; lenient CLI --tolerant → drop offending segment, increment dropped_segments, continue.
  • Debug: byte-offset → locate segment; count commas/VLQ boundaries; usual cause = stray comma producing empty (0-field) or truncated emit.

7.6 INV-5 — index bounds (source/name/ignoreList)

  • Stmt: every Segment.source (when Some) < sources.len(); every Segment.name (when Some) < names.len(); every ignore_list entry < sources.len().
  • Why: OOB source index → consumer panics or reads wrong filename → stack frame attributed to wrong file.
  • Enforced: SourceMapConsumer::new validates all indices post-decode → CodecError::SourceIndexOob; builder interners only return valid indices (closed system); from_json validates after parse.
  • Detect: Phase 6 (## 18) unit tests with crafted OOB maps; fuzz JSON corpus with inflated indices.
  • Telemetry: SourceIndexOob carries (kind: source|name|ignore, index, len).
  • Recover: strict reject; tolerant mode coerces OOB source→None (segment → 1-field), logs coerced_oob.
  • Debug: check whether map was hand-composed (compose must remap indices through target interner — INV-8 interaction); verify sources.len() vs max referenced index.

7.7 INV-6 — 0-based interior, 1-based boundary

  • Stmt: all line/column in Segment, Position, DecodedMap, codec state are 0-based. Sole 1-based representation: smap-cli query surface + human-facing print. Conversion (+1 in / −1 out) happens exactly once, at the boundary.
  • Why: mixing conventions → off-by-one on every query; the classic "everything is one line off" devtools bug.
  • Enforced: type-level discipline + "0-based" doc comment on every public fn; CLI layer is sole site of line+1/line-1; debug_assert! that no codec path adds 1.
  • Detect: CLI integration test: query a known (1-based) generated position, assert (1-based) original matches a fixture authored in editor coordinates.
  • Telemetry: CLI logs both: query gen 1-based (L,C) -> internal 0-based (l,c).
  • Recover: none — convention is invariant; a leak is a bug.
  • Debug: results uniformly off by 1 → audit for a second +1/-1 inside codec/consumer (there must be none).

7.8 INV-7 — monotonic indexes + correct GLB/LUB

  • Stmt: by-generated index sorted by (gen_line, gen_column) asc; by-original index by (source, orig_line, orig_column) asc. original_position_for(q, GLB) returns segment with greatest (line,col) ≤ q; LUB returns least ≥ q; None iff none exists.
  • Why: binary search correctness depends on sortedness; wrong bias → adjacent-segment misattribution at token boundaries.
  • Enforced: indexes built by stable sort in SourceMapConsumer::new; debug_assert!(is_sorted) post-sort; one canonical partition_point-style routine parameterized by Bias (no copy-paste variants).
  • Detect: property: random query q → GLB result ≤ q AND next segment > q; LUB symmetric; linear-scan oracle cross-check.
  • Telemetry: counters queries_total, queries_miss, glb_hits, lub_hits.
  • Recover: miss → None (caller falls back to nearest enclosing scope); never panic.
  • Debug: dump sorted index window around q; verify comparator orders column within line; classic bug = comparing column before line.

7.9 INV-8 — composition associativity + lossless resolution

  • Stmt: compose associative: remap([a,b,c]) ≡ compose(a, compose(b,c)) ≡ compose(compose(a,b), c) (semantic DecodedMap equality). No end-to-end-resolvable mapping dropped: if mid pos mapped in orig_to_mid, composed segment carries that original source/line/col (+ deepest name); only genuinely-unmapped mid positions become 1-field.
  • Why: transpile→minify pipelines chain 2–4 maps; a lost mapping → stack frame resolves to the intermediate, not author source.
  • Enforced: compose remaps each result segment's source/name through the output interner (prevents INV-5 violation); null-segment rule explicit; remap folds left.
  • Detect: property roundtrip-through-chain: positions mapped author→final via direct lookup == via composed map; associativity property over 3 random maps.
  • Telemetry: compose emits segments_in, segments_resolved, segments_nulled, names_preserved.
  • Recover: unmapped mid → 1-field segment (correct, not an error); never fabricate an original position.
  • Debug: pick a final position resolving wrong; trace mid lookup (Bias=GLB) in orig_to_mid; verify name follows deepest mapping; check interner remap table.

7.10 INV-9 — index sections sorted, non-overlapping; flatten lossless

  • Stmt: IndexedSourceMap.sections sorted asc by offset (line, column); no two sections cover the same generated position. flatten() shifts section k's gen positions by offset.line (added to every gen line) and offset.column (added only to the section's first line), yielding a flat map whose query results equal querying the indexed map directly.
  • Why: sections come from concatenated chunk outputs; overlap or mis-shift → duplicate/colliding generated coordinates → wrong resolution.
  • Enforced: IndexedSourceMap construction validates sort + non-overlap → CodecError; flatten applies documented offset arithmetic (column only on first line).
  • Detect: property: flatten then query == indexed query, over random section sets; overlap-detection unit tests.
  • Telemetry: flatten emits sections, total_segments, offset_shifts_applied.
  • Recover: overlap/unsorted → reject at construction (strict); never silently merge.
  • Debug: a position past a section boundary wrong → verify only-first-line column shift (frequent bug: adding offset.column to every line of the section).

7.11 INV-10 — total decode (no panic on arbitrary bytes)

  • Stmt: decode_vlq, decode_mappings, from_json, extract_source_mapping_url return Result/Option for ALL &[u8]/&str inputs — truncated, adversarial, non-UTF-8-adjacent — and NEVER panic, overflow-panic, or index-OOB.
  • Why: map JSON + sourceMappingURL are untrusted (arbitrary build artifacts / web); a panic = DoS in a build server or browser.
  • Enforced: no unchecked indexing (get not []) on decode paths; checked/bounded arithmetic; #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in vlq/codec/json; cargo-fuzz gate in CI (Phase 11).
  • Detect: fuzz targets fuzz_vlq, fuzz_mappings, fuzz_json run ≥1e8 execs / 24h with zero crashes before release; Miri on hot paths.
  • Telemetry: parse errors carry byte offset + 16-byte context window.
  • Recover: error propagates as typed *Error; CLI prints diagnostic + nonzero exit; library caller decides.
  • Debug: fuzz crash → minimized input checked into corpus/regressions/; reproduce via cargo fuzz run <target> <file>.

7.12 INV-11 — checked VLQ overflow

  • Stmt: decoding a VLQ whose accumulated magnitude exceeds i32 range returns VlqError::Overflow — never silent wrap, never truncated value. Encode accepts only i64 representing in-range deltas; out-of-range → caller bug surfaced in debug.
  • Why: v3 fields are conceptually 32-bit signed; a wrapped delta corrupts the running accumulator for the rest of the line/file.
  • Enforced: decoder accumulates into a widened type, checks against i32 bounds after each continuation group, checked_shl/checked_add; returns Overflow on breach.
  • Detect: unit tests with >6-group VLQs + crafted i32::MAX+1 encodings; fuzz must surface Overflow, never wrap.
  • Telemetry: Overflow carries byte offset where the bound was breached.
  • Recover: strict reject; tolerant CLI drops the segment + logs overflow_dropped.
  • Debug: count continuation groups (>5 for a 32-bit value is suspicious); inspect accumulator before the breaching group.

7.13 INV-12 — non-negative, non-decreasing generated columns

  • Stmt: after delta accumulation, each segment's absolute gen_column ≥ 0; within a single generated line, segments non-decreasing in gen_column. Decoded negative gen_column → CodecError::NegativeColumn.
  • Why: a negative or backward column is unrepresentable and breaks the consumer's sorted-by-gen invariant (INV-7).
  • Enforced: decoder checks accumulated gen_column ≥ 0 and ≥ prev_gen_col_on_line; builder sort guarantees order pre-encode.
  • Detect: property: decoded per-line gen_columns sorted asc; adversarial fixtures with negative deltas exceeding the prefix.
  • Telemetry: NegativeColumn carries (line, accumulated_value, byte_offset).
  • Recover: strict reject; tolerant mode clamps to prev + logs clamped_column (rare, last resort).
  • Debug: trace per-line accumulation; a single bad delta makes all subsequent columns on the line suspect.

7.14 INV-13 — interner determinism + index stability

  • Stmt: add_source(s)/add_name(s) return the SAME index for equal s within one builder, and assigned indices never change for the map's lifetime (append-only, no compaction). First-seen order == emit order in sources/names.
  • Why: segments reference indices; reassignment after a segment is recorded → silent mis-reference (INV-5 cascade).
  • Enforced: interner backed by insertion-ordered map; add_* idempotent on the string key; no remove/reorder API exposed.
  • Detect: unit test: interleave add_source + add_mapping, assert final sources order + segment indices consistent.
  • Telemetry: builder exposes sources_interned, names_interned, dedup_hits.
  • Recover: none — append-only by construction.
  • Debug: a segment references the wrong source → confirm no code path mutates the interner post-add_mapping for that segment.

7.15 Cross-cutting enforcement matrix

Mechanism Invariants covered Phase
proptest round-trip INV-1, INV-2, INV-7, INV-8, INV-9, INV-12 1,3,6,7,8
differential vs @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec INV-2, INV-3 11
cargo-fuzz (no panic) INV-4, INV-10, INV-11 11
debug_assert! (interior) INV-3, INV-6, INV-7 1–9
typed Result (always-on) INV-4, INV-5, INV-10, INV-11, INV-12 3,4,6
construction-time validation INV-5, INV-9, INV-13 5,6,8
#![forbid(unsafe_code)] INV-10 (memory safety) all decode crates

Invariant precedence: HARD invariants (panic-avoidance, bounds) enforced unconditionally. CRITICAL semantic invariants (delta discipline, 0-based, composition) enforced in debug + validated in CI, but assume well-formed input in release hot paths after boundary validation in SourceMapConsumer::new / from_json has run. Tolerant-mode coercions (--tolerant) are the ONLY sanctioned deviations and every coercion increments a named counter — silent coercion is itself an invariant violation.

8. Domain Model — Source Map v3, VLQ, and the Mappings Grammar

The single normative reference for the whole toolkit. Every crate (smap-vlq → smap-codec → smap-json/smap-builder/smap-consumer) derives its invariants from here. Lines/columns are 0-based everywhere inside the model and codec; the 1-based presentation conversion lives only at the smap-cli/public-API boundary (§8.3, §8.8).

8.1 Base64 VLQ bit layout

VLQ = Variable Length Quantity, Base64-encoded. One integer → one or more Base64 chars; one char carries one 6-bit digit = 1 continuation bit + 5 payload bits.

  • Digit layout (6 bits): C dddd d — bit 5 (value 32) = continuation (1 = more digits follow), bits 0..4 = 5 payload bits.
  • Sign: NOT two's complement. The integer is transformed to sign-magnitude before grouping: vlq = (|n| << 1) | (n < 0). So bit 0 of the first digit's payload is the sign bit (1 = negative). The remaining bits are the magnitude, low-to-high.
  • Grouping: take vlq, emit low 5 bits as first digit; while bits remain, set continuation on the emitted digit and shift right 5; final digit has continuation 0.
  • Alphabet (smap-vlq::B64, index→char): ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/. A=0 … /=63. Reverse table maps char→0..63, all other bytes → InvalidBase64Char.
encode_vlq(123):  n=123  -> vlq = (123<<1)|0 = 246 = 0b11110110
   group0 = vlq & 31      = 0b10110 = 22 ; vlq>>=5 -> 7 (more) -> set cont(32) -> 54 -> '2'
   group1 = 7  & 31       = 0b00111 = 7  ; vlq>>=5 -> 0 (done)  -> cont=0       -> 7  -> 'H'
   chars: '2H'
   digit0 byte:  [C=1][payload=10110]  (sign bit = LSB of payload = 0 -> positive)
   digit1 byte:  [C=0][payload=00111]

encode_vlq(-1):   n=-1   -> vlq = (1<<1)|1 = 3 = 0b00011
   group0 = 3 & 31 = 3 ; vlq>>=5 -> 0 (done) -> cont=0 -> 3 -> 'D'
   chars: 'D'
   digit byte: [C=0][payload=00011]  (sign bit = 1 -> negative ; magnitude bits = 0b001 = 1)

encode_vlq(0):    n=0 -> vlq=0 -> single digit 0 -> 'A'

decode_vlq(input, pos): read digit, shift payload into result at shift (0,5,10,…), stop when continuation clears; then negative = result & 1, magnitude = result >> 1, final = if negative { -magnitude } else { magnitude }; advance *pos. Errors: UnexpectedEof (continuation set but no next byte), InvalidBase64Char(b), Overflow (>32 bits accumulated, i.e. >6 digits / magnitude exceeds i64 safe range).

8.2 The "mappings" grammar (EBNF)

mappings   = line , { ";" , line } ;          (* ";" begins a new GENERATED line *)
line       = [ segment , { "," , segment } ]; (* a line may be empty *)
segment    = field4or5 | field1 ;             (* see field-count rule below *)
field1     = vlq ;                            (* gen_column only          *)
field4or5  = vlq , vlq , vlq , vlq , [ vlq ] ;(* gen_col, src, oline, ocol[, name] *)
vlq        = vlqdigit , { vlqdigit } ;        (* >=1 Base64 char          *)
vlqdigit   = "A" | … | "Z" | "a" | … | "z" | "0" | … | "9" | "+" | "/" ;

Field-count rule (LOCKED): a segment has exactly 1, 4, or 5 fields. 2 or 3 → CodecError::BadFieldCount(n). Counts decode as:

Fields Meaning Has source? Has name?
1 generated column only (unmapped — "generated-only") no no
4 gen col + source + orig line + orig col yes no
5 the above + name index yes yes

8.3 Relative-delta semantics (the #1 source of bugs)

Each field is a relative delta against running state held in EncoderState/DecoderState. The reset discipline is the load-bearing invariant:

# Field Decoded into Segment Relative to Reset per ; (new gen line)? State name
1 generated column gen_column previous segment's gen_column on the same line YES → reset to 0 prev_gen_col
2 source index source previous segment's source index NO → carries ACROSS lines prev_source
3 original line orig_line previous segment's orig line NO → carries prev_orig_line
4 original column orig_column previous segment's orig column NO → carries prev_orig_column
5 name index name previous segment's name index NO → carries prev_name

Unmissable rule: only prev_gen_col resets at ;; the other four persist for the entire map. A 1-field segment advances only prev_gen_col. Encoder mirrors decoder: emit cur - prev, then prev = cur.

8.4 v3 JSON envelope schema

{
  "version":        3,            // u8, MUST == 3 ; else JsonError::BadVersion(n)
  "file":           "out.js",     // string | absent  -> Option<String>
  "sourceRoot":     "src/",       // string | absent  -> Option<String> ; prepended to each source
  "sources":        ["a.ts", null],     // Vec<Option<String>> (null allowed)
  "sourcesContent": ["…", null],        // Vec<Option<String>> ; index-aligned to sources
  "names":          ["foo","bar"],      // Vec<String>
  "mappings":       "AAAA,…",           // string (REQUIRED) -> MissingField if absent
  "ignoreList":     [1]                 // Vec<u32> ; indexes into sources ; a.k.a. x_google_ignoreList
}
  • version and mappings are required; all else optional (MissingField("mappings")).
  • sourcesContent: content[i] is the full text of sources[i]; null/shorter array ⇒ that source has no embedded content. smap-json pads to sources.len() with None.
  • ignoreList: source indexes the debugger should skip (vendored/framework code). Emitted as "ignoreList"; parsed from "ignoreList" and legacy "x_google_ignoreList". Out-of-range index → validation warning (§8.7).
  • sourceRoot: resolution = sourceRoot + sources[i] (string concat with single / normalization); never alters indexes.
  • Index integrity: every segment source < sources.len(), every name < names.len() (enforced in smap-consumer::new).

8.5 Fully worked example

Generated file out.js (2 lines) maps to two sources, a.ts (idx 0) and b.ts (idx 1).

Intent (0-based gen/orig, names ["x"]):

gen line gen col source orig line orig col name
0 0 0 (a.ts) 0 0 —
0 5 0 (a.ts) 0 4 0 (x)
1 0 1 (b.ts) 2 0 —

DecodedMap (Vec<Vec<Segment>>, outer = gen line):

line 0: [ Segment{gen_column:0, source:Some(0), orig_line:0, orig_column:0, name:None},
          Segment{gen_column:5, source:Some(0), orig_line:0, orig_column:4, name:Some(0)} ]
line 1: [ Segment{gen_column:0, source:Some(1), orig_line:2, orig_column:0, name:None} ]

Hand-encode to "mappings" — running state starts all-zero; prev_gen_col resets at ;:

== line 0 ==  (prev: gcol=0 src=0 oln=0 ocol=0 name=0)
 seg(0,0,0,0):    dcol=0-0=0 -> A   dsrc=0-0=0 -> A   doln=0-0=0 -> A   docol=0-0=0 -> A     => "AAAA"
                  state: gcol=0 src=0 oln=0 ocol=0
 seg(5,0,0,4,n0): dcol=5-0=5 ->'K'  dsrc=0-0=0 -> A   doln=0-0=0 -> A   docol=4-0=4 ->'I'  dname=0-0=0 -> A  => "KAAIA"
                  ( 5 -> vlq=10 -> 'K' ; 4 -> vlq=8 -> 'I' )
                  state: gcol=5 src=0 oln=0 ocol=4 name=0
 line 0 string: "AAAA,KAAIA"
== ";" -> reset gcol to 0 ; src/oln/ocol/name CARRY (src=0 oln=0 ocol=4 name=0) ==
== line 1 ==
 seg(0,1,2,0):    dcol=0-0=0 -> A   dsrc=1-0=1 ->'C'  doln=2-0=2 ->'E'  docol=0-4=-4 ->'J'   => "ACEJ"
                  ( 1 -> vlq=2 -> 'C' ; 2 -> vlq=4 -> 'E' ; -4 -> vlq=(4<<1)|1=9 -> 'J' )
 line 1 string: "ACEJ"

mappings = "AAAA,KAAIA;ACEJ"

Complete JSON:

{"version":3,"file":"out.js","sourceRoot":"","sources":["a.ts","b.ts"],
 "sourcesContent":["const x = 1;\n","export const y = 2;\n"],
 "names":["x"],"mappings":"AAAA,KAAIA;ACEJ","ignoreList":[]}

8.6 sourceMappingURL comment + inline data: URL

extract_source_mapping_url(code) scans from EOF for the last match (line or block form):

//# sourceMappingURL=out.js.map          -> SourceMapRef::Url("out.js.map")
//@ sourceMappingURL=out.js.map          (legacy '@' accepted on read, '#' on emit)
/*# sourceMappingURL=out.js.map */       (block form)
//# sourceMappingURL=data:application/json;base64,eyJ2ZXJ…  -> SourceMapRef::Inline(SourceMap)
//# sourceMappingURL=data:application/json;charset=utf-8;base64,…

Inline data: URL: split on first ,; require MIME application/json; if ;base64 present, Base64-decode the tail then from_json; else URL-decode then from_json. Result wrapped SourceMapRef::Inline. Emit always uses //# (hash form).

8.7 Malformed / edge examples → error

Input fragment Detected by Error / behavior
segment with 2 or 3 fields (e.g. "AA", "AAA") smap-codec field count CodecError::BadFieldCount(2) / (3)
VLQ overflowing i64 (>6 continuation digits) decode_vlq CodecError::Vlq(VlqError::Overflow)
source index ≥ sources.len() after deltas smap-consumer::new CodecError::SourceIndexOob
original column delta drives orig_column negative smap-codec CodecError::NegativeColumn
mappings line with leading "," (e.g. ",AAAA") smap-codec empty-segment → BadFieldCount(0)
truncated VLQ (continuation set, no next char, e.g. "g") decode_vlq VlqError::UnexpectedEof → CodecError::Vlq
non-Base64 byte in mappings (e.g. "=A") decode_vlq VlqError::InvalidBase64Char(b'=')
"version": 2 (or 4) from_json JsonError::BadVersion(2)
mappings field absent from_json JsonError::MissingField("mappings")
XSSI prefix )]}' before JSON from_json stripped silently, then parse
UTF-8 BOM (EF BB BF) before JSON / XSSI from_json stripped silently, then parse
empty mappings:"" decode_mappings valid → DecodedMap(vec![]) (0 lines)
trailing ; (e.g. "AAAA;") decode_mappings valid → final empty line appended

Invariant: decode_vlq, decode_mappings, and from_json NEVER panic on arbitrary bytes — all faults surface as the typed errors above (fuzz-enforced, Phase 11).

8.8 original_position_for binary-search flow

Consumer holds segments sorted by (gen_line, gen_column). Query gen: Position (0-based), bias:

original_position_for(gen, bias):
  1. row = index of generated line gen.line          (outer Vec index; absent -> None)
  2. segs = row's segments, sorted ascending by gen_column
  3. binary-search segs for gen.column:
        GreatestLowerBound (default): largest seg with gen_column <= gen.column
        LeastUpperBound:              smallest seg with gen_column >= gen.column
  4. chosen seg: if seg.source.is_none() -> None (generated-only / unmapped)
                 else -> Some(OriginalLocation{
                            source: seg.source,
                            line:   seg.orig_line,
                            column: seg.orig_column,
                            name:   seg.name })
  5. (CLI boundary only) convert line/column 0-based -> 1-based for display

generated_position_for(source, orig, bias) mirrors this over the by-original index, sorted (source, orig_line, orig_column), returning the generated Position. Both are O(log n) per query; index build is O(n log n) once in SourceMapConsumer::new (Phase 6).

Non-goals restated: this section models, encodes, decodes, and queries source maps only. JS parsing/transform (#264), minification, AST work, devtools UI, and the wasm/DWARF source-map extension are out of scope (the latter gated behind PhaseDeferred).

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture Decision Records governing project 265. Each row is LOCKED; downstream phases (## 12–## 24) MUST NOT contradict. Status = Accepted unless marked Deferred.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
001 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 JS (Mozilla source-map); C/C++; Go Integer/alloc hot paths → zero-cost, bounds-checked codec; untrusted-map parse stays memory-safe. References (Sentry sourcemap, SWC/OXC, @jridgewell) map cleanly. JS wins when the toolkit ships inside a JS bundler/devtools already paying the V8 cost (Mozilla source-map, @jridgewell); a WASM Rust core there is pure overhead.
002 Repo shape Cargo workspace, 10 smap-* members (vlq, model, codec, json, builder, consumer, compose, index, edit, cli) Single monolith crate; per-feature flags in one crate Crate boundaries enforce layering (vlq←codec←builder/consumer←compose/index); no_std cores isolated from std I/O; per-crate test/bench/fuzz. A monolith wins for a tiny single-binary tool with one consumer and no no_std requirement — fewer Cargo.tomls, faster cold build (the Sentry sourcemap crate is one crate for exactly this reason).
003 Core crate environment no_std + alloc for smap-vlq, -model, -codec, -builder, -consumer, -compose, -index std everywhere Embeds in WASM/sandboxed transpilers; std only where I/O is needed (json, edit, cli). std-everywhere wins for a server-only symbolication backend that never targets WASM/embedded — std HashMap/io is simpler and the no_std discipline buys nothing.
004 In-memory map representation DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>), outer index = generated line (0-based) Flat Vec<Segment> with gen_line field; rope/btree Outer-by-line mirrors ; structure → per-line gen_column reset is natural; line-local sort cheap. A flat Vec<Segment> wins for query-only consumers over maps with millions of single-segment lines, where per-line Vec headers dominate RAM (the CSR layout of §31.5).
005 Base64 decode strategy Table-driven: 64-entry B64 forward + 256-entry reverse LUT (invalid→sentinel) Arithmetic char-class branches ('A'..='Z') Branchless byte→6-bit lookup; ~2–4× faster hot loop; LUT is const. Arithmetic branches win on extreme-cache-pressure targets where a 256 B table evicts hotter data, or on a tiny embedded core where ROM for the table is scarcer than cycles.
006 VLQ accumulator type i64 accumulator, validate result fits checked 32-bit range before emit i32 accumulator Intermediate shifts + delta sums transiently exceed i32; i64 avoids UB/wrap, then VlqError::Overflow. An i32 accumulator wins only on a 32-bit MCU with no fast 64-bit ALU, and with a hard guarantee inputs never exceed a few groups — a regime this toolkit's untrusted-input contract forbids.
007 VLQ encoding scheme Sign-bit + magnitude: LSB of value = sign, 5-bit groups low→high, bit 32 = continuation Two's-complement encoding Source Map v3 mandates sign-magnitude; interop with all reference codecs requires it. Two's-complement wins for a new private wire format unconstrained by Source Map v3 (e.g. an internal IR's varints) where branchless decode matters more than spec parity — see protobuf zig-zag (#178).
008 Delta reset semantics gen_column reset to 0 at every ;; source/orig_line/orig_column/name carried ACROSS lines Reset all fields per line; reset none Matches v3 spec exactly (the #1 codec bug); reset logic lives in one place. "Reset all per line" would win only for a hypothetical absolute-coordinate map format that traded compactness for trivial random line access — explicitly not v3.
009 Coordinate base 0-based line + column inside model/codec; 1-based conversion only at API/CLI boundary 1-based internally Spec is 0-based; 0-based codec avoids ±1 errors in deltas/binary search. 1-based-internal wins for a tool whose only surface is a user-facing editor/CLI (1-based everywhere) with no spec-level codec — the conversion seam then adds cost with no codec to protect.
010 Generator API SourceMapBuilder with source/name interning + add_mapping + sort + dedup → into_sourcemap Tools construct DecodedMap/SourceMap directly Interning dedups; builder enforces sort + ordering + dedup before encode. Mirrors SourceMapGenerator/gen-mapping. Direct construction wins when the producer already emits sorted, deduped, interned segments (a codegen that walks the AST in output order) — the builder's sort/dedup is then redundant work.
011 Consumer query structure Sorted by-generated index + by-original index, binary search HashMap keyed by position; linear scan Queries need nearest-≤/≥ (bias); hashmaps give only exact-match; binary search is O(log n). A HashMap wins when only exact generated positions are ever queried (e.g. a coverage tool keyed on emitted statement starts) — no bias needed, O(1) beats O(log n).
012 Default query bias Bias::GreatestLowerBound LUB default; no default (require explicit) Matches DevTools/V8 + Mozilla consumer: greatest generated position ≤ query. LUB-as-default wins for a "jump to next mapped statement" UX (a step-over debugger feature) where landing on the following segment is the desired behavior.
013 Composition strategy compose via SourceMapConsumer lookups on orig_to_mid, walking mid_to_gen segments Fused single-pass codec composition Reuses correct binary-search + bias path; explicit null-segment propagation; associative. A fused single-pass compose wins for an extreme-throughput build server composing millions of segments per second, where one decode+merge pass beats per-segment binary search — at the cost of re-deriving query logic.
014 Index-map flatten flatten() shifts each Section by offset: offset.line to all gen lines; offset.column to first line only Concatenate raw mappings; renumber sources only Column offset applies solely to the section's first generated line (spec); enforce sorted + non-overlapping. Raw-mappings concat wins when every chunk starts at column 0 on its own line (newline-delimited concatenation) — then no column offset is needed and string concat is O(bytes), not O(segments).
015 JSON layer serde-based parse with zero-copy borrowing (&str into input buffer) Hand-rolled minimal parser; full owned-String DOM serde_json gives robust spec coverage; borrowing avoids per-string alloc on big sources/sourcesContent. A hand-rolled parser wins for the strict no_std core that cannot pull serde, or to shave the last allocations on a SIMD streaming path (§31.9) — at a large spec-coverage maintenance cost.
016 XSSI prefix handling from_json strips leading )]}' (+ newline); to_json does NOT emit it by default Always emit prefix; never strip CDNs/tools prepend )]}' to defeat XSSI; tolerant decode required; emission opt-in. "Always emit" wins when the map is served by a same-origin endpoint that fetches it via XHR/fetch and requires the guard — a browser-served-map deployment then opts the prefix in.
017 sourcesContent policy Opt-in: set_source_content / flag; omitted ⇒ null entry, length = sources length Always embed; never embed Content embedding is large and optional; positional null preserves index alignment. "Always embed" wins for self-contained offline debugging artifacts (a crash-report bundle, a CI build you must debug without the repo) where map size is irrelevant but source availability is critical.
018 MagicString model Interval/chunk bookkeeping over original buffer + column tracking → map without re-parse Re-tokenize edited output; diff-based map Edits recorded as intervals; columns from chunk lengths; no parser dependency (parsing is #264). Diff/re-parse wins when edits are produced by an opaque external formatter (e.g. Prettier) that returns only the new text — there are no recorded intervals to bookkeep, so a text diff is the only source of a map.
019 Overflow / DoS caps VLQ Overflow on >32-bit; configurable caps on segments/lines/depth/JSON size; never panic Unbounded decode; trust input Maps are untrusted (browser/error-reporter input); bounded resources + total functions prevent DoS. Unbounded/trusting decode wins for a fully local, single-user build where the map is self-emitted moments earlier — caps and Result-threading then add latency guarding against an impossible adversary.
020 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets: VLQ, mappings, v3 JSON; invariant = no panic + round-trip Manual edge cases only; AFL cargo-fuzz integrates with the Rust toolchain + corpus; covers all untrusted surfaces. AFL++ wins for deep, long-horizon campaigns with custom mutators/dictionaries on a dedicated fuzz farm — its havoc/cmplog stages outpace libFuzzer on structured grammars given the infra to run them.
021 Differential oracle Cross-check encode/decode/query vs Mozilla source-map + @jridgewell corpora Self-consistency only External oracle catches spec-misreads self-tests miss; corpora pinned as fixtures. Self-consistency-only wins for a brand-new format with no reference implementation to diff against — round-trip + property tests are then the only available oracle.
022 Benchmarking criterion benches: encode/decode M-segment maps, query latency, flatten, compose #[bench] nightly; ad-hoc timing criterion gives stable statistics on stable toolchain + regression tracking. iai-callgrind/instruction-count benching wins on noisy shared CI runners where wall-clock variance hides regressions (and is itself adopted here in §31.3 for exactly that).
023 CI GitHub Actions: build/test/clippy/fmt on MSRV+stable, no_std check, fuzz smoke, bench gate Local-only; other CI Matrix enforces MSRV + no_std cores + lint/format; fuzz + differential per PR. A self-hosted CI (BuildKite/Jenkins) wins when fuzz/bench need bare-metal pinned hardware GitHub-hosted runners cannot provide stably — the perf-gate noise floor demands it.
024 Dependency policy + licensing Minimal deps (serde/serde_json in smap-json; criterion/cargo-fuzz dev-only); dual MIT OR Apache-2.0 Heavy dep tree; single license Cores stay zero-dep no_std+alloc; serde confined to json; dual license = Rust-ecosystem norm. A heavier dep tree wins when time-to-market dominates and pulling a full-featured crate (e.g. simd-json) outweighs the audit surface — a one-off internal tool, not a foundational library #264 depends on.

Deferred (PhaseDeferred, NON-GOAL — see project identity): WebAssembly DWARF / wasm source-map extension; SIMD JSON for the streaming path; a fused single-pass compose. Revisit only with profiling evidence from Phase 12 (## 24).

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace; 10 smap-* members under crates/. Directory tree below; every path carries an inline purpose comment. Dirs align 1:1 to phases (crates/smap-vlq ↔ Phase 1, crates/smap-codec ↔ Phase 3, fuzz/ ↔ Phase 11, benches/ ↔ Phase 12, …). Layering rule: a crate depends only downward — smap-vlq ← smap-codec ← {smap-builder,smap-consumer} ← {smap-compose,smap-index}; smap-model is the shared leaf; smap-json/smap-edit/smap-cli sit at the std boundary. no_std + alloc: vlq, model, codec, builder, consumer, compose, index. std: json, edit, cli. cargo deny (deny.toml) enforces the dep graph + license allowlist; no up-edges.

10.6 Layout rationale (one row per decision)

Path / convention Decision Why
crates/<name>/{src,tests,benches} uniform per-crate shape predictable navigation; criterion bench lives beside its crate
smap-model no internal deps shared leaf every crate references model types without a cycle
smap-vlq no deps + #![no_std] bottom of graph codec is hot-path; keep it minimal + portable
std confined to json/edit/cli I/O at the edge core codec usable in no_std/wasm hosts
fuzz/ is its own workspace member cargo-fuzz requirement isolates nightly/libFuzzer toolchain from main build
conformance/ read-only + MANIFEST.md vendored corpora with provenance reproducible differential tests; license traceability
docs/{adr,threat-model,performance,failure-analysis} docs mirror spec sections §9/§11/§12/Failure-Modes stay in sync with code
1-based↔0-based only in smap-cli/main.rs boundary conversion centralized codec/model stay strictly 0-based (ADR 0003)
.github/workflows split ci/fuzz/bench/release separate cadences CI per-PR; fuzz+bench nightly; release on tag

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative type catalog. Every phase (## 12..## 24) builds against these exact signatures, module homes, and semantics. Signatures + doc comments only (lprompt CRITICAL RULE 10) — no bodies beyond 1–2 illustrative lines. Lines/columns are 0-based everywhere inside the model and codec; 1-based presentation happens only at the API/CLI boundary (smap-cli, MagicString line reporting).

11.1 Cross-cutting contracts

Property Rule
Immutability SourceMap, DecodedMap, Segment, Position, OriginalLocation, IndexedSourceMap, Section are plain value structs — immutable once built; mutate by rebuilding, never in place.
Mutable accumulators SourceMapBuilder (interners + per-line buffers) and MagicString (ordered edit list) are the only mutable types; both consume/snapshot into immutable values (into_sourcemap, generate_map).
Concurrency All value types are Send + Sync (no interior mutability, no Rc). SourceMapConsumer is read-only after new → freely shareable across threads behind &. Builders/MagicString are single-owner, not shared mutably.
Cloning Cheap structural Clone (Vec copy). No Arc in the public surface; callers wrap in Arc<SourceMapConsumer> for fan-out querying.
Hashing/eq Segment, Position, OriginalLocation derive Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash (small POD). SourceMap/DecodedMap derive Clone, PartialEq only (own String/Vec).
Serialization One canonical wire form: the v3 JSON envelope via smap-json (to_json/from_json). Transports: raw .map file, inline data:application/json;base64, URL, //# sourceMappingURL= comment. DecodedMap is in-memory only — it is the decoded form of mappings, never serialized.
Compatibility version MUST equal 3 (else JsonError::BadVersion). New fields are additive + optional (e.g. ignoreList/x_google_ignoreList); unknown JSON keys ignored on parse, never emitted on write unless modeled.
Error discipline Every fallible path returns Result<_, E> with a closed enum; no panics on untrusted input (VLQ, JSON, mappings) — fuzzed in Phase 11 (## 23).

11.2 smap-vlq — Base64 VLQ primitives (no_std + alloc)

/// 64-char Base64 alphabet, index = digit value: A-Z(0-25) a-z(26-51) 0-9(52-61) '+'(62) '/'(63).
pub const B64: [u8; 64];
/// Reverse lookup (char byte -> 0..63, 0xFF = invalid). 256-entry table for branch-free decode.
pub(crate) const B64_REV: [u8; 256];

/// Encode one integer as Base64 VLQ, appending chars to `out`.
/// Sign-bit-and-magnitude (NOT two's complement): LSB of the pre-grouped value = sign
/// (1 = negative); remaining bits split into 5-bit groups low->high; bit 5 (value 32) = continuation.
pub fn encode_vlq(value: i64, out: &mut String);

/// Decode one VLQ integer starting at `*pos`; advances `*pos` past consumed chars.
/// Stops at the first group whose continuation bit is clear; reconstructs sign from bit 0.
pub fn decode_vlq(input: &[u8], pos: &mut usize) -> Result<i64, VlqError>;

pub enum VlqError {
    UnexpectedEof,            // continuation bit set but input ended
    InvalidBase64Char(u8),   // byte not in the alphabet
    Overflow,                // accumulated magnitude exceeds i64 / shift > 63
}
  • Ownership: stateless free functions; out/pos are caller-owned. decode_vlq allocates nothing (table lookup over a byte slice).
  • Invariants: encode_vlq then decode_vlq round-trips every legal i64; decode_vlq never reads past input.len(); never panics.

11.3 smap-model — core data model (no_std + alloc)

/// A point in a text buffer: 0-based line + column (UTF-16 code units per v3 column semantics).
#[derive(Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct Position { pub line: u32, pub column: u32 }

/// One mapping entry on a generated line. ABSOLUTE values in memory (deltas live only in the
/// encoded "mappings" string). A 1-field segment has source=None, name=None (generated-only).
#[derive(Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct Segment {
    pub gen_column: u32,        // generated column on this line
    pub source: Option<u32>,    // index into SourceMap.sources; None => generated-only segment
    pub orig_line: u32,         // original line (0-based)   — meaningful iff source.is_some()
    pub orig_column: u32,       // original column (0-based) — meaningful iff source.is_some()
    pub name: Option<u32>,      // index into SourceMap.names; requires source.is_some()
}

/// Decoded mappings: outer Vec index = generated line (0-based); inner = segments on that line,
/// sorted ascending by gen_column. Empty inner Vec => a generated line with no mappings (";;").
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct DecodedMap(pub Vec<Vec<Segment>>);

/// The v3 JSON shape (envelope). `mappings` holds the encoded VLQ string (decode via smap-codec).
/// `sources`/`sources_content` are positionally aligned (same length; index i => same source).
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct SourceMap {
    pub version: u8,                          // always 3
    pub file: Option<String>,                 // generated artifact name
    pub source_root: Option<String>,          // prefix prepended to each `sources` entry
    pub sources: Vec<Option<String>>,         // source paths/URLs (None = anonymous source)
    pub sources_content: Vec<Option<String>>, // inline original contents, aligned to `sources`
    pub names: Vec<String>,                   // symbol names referenced by segment.name
    pub mappings: String,                     // encoded VLQ "mappings" string
    pub ignore_list: Vec<u32>,                // x_google_ignoreList: indices into `sources` to skip
}

/// Result of a consumer original lookup: resolved absolute original location (+ optional name).
#[derive(Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct OriginalLocation { pub source: u32, pub line: u32, pub column: u32, pub name: Option<u32> }
  • SourceMap owns all its strings/vecs; self-contained, no borrows → trivially Send + Sync.
  • Field invariants (enforced at construction / SourceMapConsumer::new): sources.len() == sources_content.len(); every Segment.source/OriginalLocation.source < sources.len(); every Segment.name < names.len(); ignore_list entries < sources.len().
  • DecodedMap is the canonical decoded representation consumed by smap-codec/consumer/compose; never serialized directly.

11.4 smap-codec — mappings string codec (no_std + alloc)

/// Encode a DecodedMap into the "mappings" string. Emits ';' between generated lines, ',' between
/// segments; each field as a relative VLQ delta per the reset rules (gen_column resets each ';').
pub fn encode_mappings(decoded: &DecodedMap) -> String;

/// Decode a "mappings" string into a DecodedMap (tolerant of real-world maps). Validates each
/// segment has 1, 4, or 5 fields; rejects negative absolute columns / out-of-range source indices.
pub fn decode_mappings(mappings: &str) -> Result<DecodedMap, CodecError>;

pub enum CodecError {
    BadFieldCount(usize),  // segment had != {1,4,5} fields
    Vlq(VlqError),         // underlying VLQ decode failed
    NegativeColumn,        // a delta drove an absolute line/column below 0
    SourceIndexOob,        // source/name index out of range vs the map's tables
}

/// Running-delta state. prev_gen_col RESETS to 0 at every ';' (new generated line);
/// the other four CARRY ACROSS lines. Mirrored shape for encode and decode.
pub(crate) struct EncoderState {
    prev_gen_col: u32,                                          // reset per line
    prev_source: u32, prev_orig_line: u32, prev_orig_column: u32, prev_name: u32, // carried across ';'
}
pub(crate) struct DecoderState { /* identical fields + reset rules */ }
  • Mutation: EncoderState/DecoderState are private single-pass cursors, not exposed. The two public functions are pure (input → owned output).
  • Delta semantics (the #1 bug source — restated as a contract): field1 gen_column relative + reset per ;; field2 source, field3 orig_line, field4 orig_column, field5 name — all relative, carried across ;.

11.5 smap-json — v3 JSON, URL, inline data: (std)

/// Serialize a SourceMap to the canonical v3 JSON envelope (version:3, stable key order).
pub fn to_json(map: &SourceMap) -> String;

/// Parse v3 JSON into a SourceMap. Strips the XSSI ")]}'" prefix if present; requires version==3.
pub fn from_json(text: &str) -> Result<SourceMap, JsonError>;

/// Find the last "//# sourceMappingURL=" (or legacy "//@") in generated code and resolve it:
/// external URL, or an inline "data:application/json;base64,..." map decoded in place.
pub fn extract_source_mapping_url(code: &str) -> Option<SourceMapRef>;

pub enum SourceMapRef { Url(String), Inline(SourceMap) }  // data: URL decoded to a SourceMap

pub enum JsonError {
    Parse(String),                 // malformed JSON / bad data: URL / bad base64
    BadVersion(u8),                // version field present but != 3
    MissingField(&'static str),    // required field absent (e.g. "version", "mappings")
}
  • to_json emits only modeled keys; ignore_list serialized as x_google_ignoreList, omitted when empty. XSSI prefix not emitted by default (opt-in for browser-served maps).
  • from_json ignores unknown keys (forward-compat) and decodes inline data: URLs eagerly into SourceMapRef::Inline.

11.6 smap-builder — generator API (no_std + alloc)

/// Mutable accumulator implementing the SourceMapGenerator contract. Interns sources + names,
/// buffers segments per generated line, then sorts + dedups + encodes on finalize.
pub struct SourceMapBuilder { /* sources interner, names interner, Vec<Vec<Segment>>, content map */ }

impl SourceMapBuilder {
    pub fn add_source(&mut self, src: &str) -> u32;        // returns stable source index (dedups)
    pub fn add_name(&mut self, name: &str) -> u32;         // returns stable name index (dedups)
    pub fn set_source_content(&mut self, source: u32, content: &str);  // attach inline content
    /// Record one mapping. `orig = Some((source_idx, original_position))` for a mapped segment,
    /// `None` for a generated-only (1-field) segment. `name` requires `orig.is_some()`.
    pub fn add_mapping(&mut self, gen: Position, orig: Option<(u32, Position)>, name: Option<u32>);
    pub fn into_sourcemap(self) -> SourceMap;  // sort by (gen_line, gen_column), dedup, encode_mappings
}
  • Ownership: owns its interners + buffers; into_sourcemap consumes self → one-shot, no reuse-after-finalize footgun. Output is an immutable SourceMap.
  • Semantics: add_mapping may arrive out of order (smap-edit relies on this); ordering + dedup deferred to into_sourcemap. Interning guarantees first-occurrence index stability.

11.7 smap-consumer — query API (no_std + alloc)

/// Tie-break direction for position lookups when no exact match exists.
pub enum Bias {
    GreatestLowerBound,   // default: greatest mapped position <= query
    LeastUpperBound,      // least mapped position >= query
}

/// Read-only queryable view: holds the DecodedMap plus a by-generated index (sorted (line,col))
/// and a by-original index (sorted (source,line,col)) for reverse lookup.
pub struct SourceMapConsumer { /* decoded, by_gen index, by_orig index, sources/names/content */ }

impl SourceMapConsumer {
    pub fn new(map: SourceMap) -> Result<Self, CodecError>;   // decodes mappings + builds indexes
    /// generated -> original. Binary-searches the by-generated index with `bias`.
    pub fn original_position_for(&self, gen: Position, bias: Bias) -> Option<OriginalLocation>;
    /// original -> generated. Binary-searches the by-original index with `bias`.
    pub fn generated_position_for(&self, source: u32, orig: Position, bias: Bias) -> Option<Position>;
    pub fn source_content_for(&self, source: u32) -> Option<&str>;
    /// All generated positions mapping to a given original position.
    pub fn all_generated_positions_for(&self, source: u32, orig: Position) -> Vec<Position>;
}
  • Concurrency: fully immutable post-new; all query methods take &self → share one consumer across threads (Arc<SourceMapConsumer>) for parallel stack-frame symbolication.
  • Cost: new is O(N log N) (build + sort indexes); each query O(log N) binary search. source_content_for borrows from the owned SourceMap (no copy).

11.8 smap-compose — composition / chaining (no_std + alloc)

/// Compose two stages: orig->mid (as a consumer) and mid->gen (as a DecodedMap + its names).
/// For each gen segment, look up its mid position in `orig_to_mid`; if mapped, emit gen->original
/// (source/line/col/name); if unmapped, emit a 1-field generated-only segment. Names follow the
/// deepest available mapping.
pub fn compose(orig_to_mid: &SourceMapConsumer, mid_to_gen: &DecodedMap, mid_names: &[String]) -> SourceMap;

/// Fold `compose` over an ordered chain orig -> .. -> gen. Associative; collapses to one flat map.
pub fn remap(chain: &[SourceMap]) -> Result<SourceMap, CodecError>;
  • Inputs borrowed (&), output owned SourceMap. compose is associative → remap may fold left or right with the same result (property-tested, Phases 7/11).
  • Null-segment propagation: an unmapped intermediate position degrades to generated-only, never fabricates an original location.

11.9 smap-index — sectioned (index) maps (no_std + alloc)

/// One section of an index map: its starting offset in the generated output + its own SourceMap.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Section { pub offset: Position, pub map: SourceMap }

/// A sectioned ("index") map. Sections MUST be sorted by `offset` and non-overlapping.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct IndexedSourceMap {
    pub version: u8,                  // always 3
    pub file: Option<String>,
    pub sections: Vec<Section>,
}

impl IndexedSourceMap {
    /// Merge into a single flat SourceMap: shift each section's generated positions by its offset
    /// (offset.line added to every gen line; offset.column added only to the section's first line).
    pub fn flatten(&self) -> Result<SourceMap, CodecError>;
}
  • Invariants (enforced on build/flatten): sections sorted ascending by offset.line then offset.column; no two sections overlap; each inner map.version == 3. Violations → CodecError.
  • Ownership: owns its sections (each owning a full SourceMap); flatten borrows &self, returns a new owned flat map.

11.10 smap-edit — MagicString source editor (std)

/// Options controlling map generation: filenames, content embedding, mapping resolution.
pub struct MapOptions {
    pub file: Option<String>,    // generated file name
    pub source: Option<String>,  // original source name (single-input editor)
    pub include_content: bool,   // embed original into sources_content
    pub hires: bool,             // per-character vs per-edit-boundary mappings
}

/// Mutable editor over an original buffer. Records ordered edits (insert/remove/overwrite/move)
/// against original byte offsets; emits transformed text + a correct map WITHOUT re-parsing,
/// via interval bookkeeping + column tracking. Builder-style: each method returns &mut Self.
pub struct MagicString { /* original: String, ordered edits, intro/outro buffers */ }

impl MagicString {
    pub fn overwrite(&mut self, start: usize, end: usize, content: &str) -> &mut Self; // replace [start,end)
    pub fn append(&mut self, s: &str) -> &mut Self;   // append after all content (outro)
    pub fn prepend(&mut self, s: &str) -> &mut Self;  // prepend before all content (intro)
    pub fn remove(&mut self, start: usize, end: usize) -> &mut Self;  // delete [start,end)
    pub fn to_string(&self) -> String;                // materialize transformed output
    pub fn generate_map(&self, opts: &MapOptions) -> SourceMap;  // builds via SourceMapBuilder
}
  • Mutation: the one heavily-mutated public type; &mut self chaining mirrors magic-string. Offsets are byte offsets into the original; out-of-range/overlapping edits are rejected via the Phase 9 (## 21) error path (panic-free).
  • generate_map takes &self (pure w.r.t. the editor) and drives smap-builder internally → the editor never re-parses and never hand-rolls VLQ.

11.11 Type → crate → phase map

Type / fn Crate Introduced
B64, encode_vlq, decode_vlq, VlqError smap-vlq Phase 1 (## 13)
Position, Segment, DecodedMap, SourceMap, OriginalLocation smap-model Phase 0/2 (## 12/## 14)
encode_mappings, decode_mappings, CodecError, Encoder/DecoderState smap-codec Phase 3 (## 15)
to_json, from_json, extract_source_mapping_url, SourceMapRef, JsonError smap-json Phase 4 (## 16)
SourceMapBuilder smap-builder Phase 5 (## 17)
Bias, SourceMapConsumer smap-consumer Phase 6 (## 18)
compose, remap smap-compose Phase 7 (## 19)

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton, workspace, and the "encode one segment, decode it back" smoke test

0.1 Objectives

  • Stand up the Cargo workspace (10 smap-* members) + dependency DAG matching section 10; pin edition 2021, MSRV 1.78.
  • Define type shells only (no logic): SourceMap, DecodedMap, Segment, Position, OriginalLocation, error enums VlqError/CodecError/JsonError, and SourceMapBuilder/SourceMapConsumer/MagicString placeholder structs.
  • Wire CI (fmt → clippy -D warnings → build → test), Makefile, Dockerfile, rust-toolchain.toml, deny.toml.
  • Ship the smoke test: one-Segment DecodedMap → encode_mappings → decode_mappings → assert structural equality. Prove the end-to-end skeleton path is green.
  • NON-GOAL: real VLQ math, real JSON, real query/compose — those are Phases 1–12. This phase only proves the wiring compiles and round-trips one trivial segment.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • Workspace root Cargo.toml: [workspace] members=["crates/smap-*"], resolver="2", shared [workspace.package] (version/edition/rust-version), [workspace.lints] (clippy pedantic opt-in per-crate) (per ADR-002).
  • Crate shells (crates/smap-{vlq,model,codec,json,builder,consumer,compose,index,edit,cli}) each lib.rs (or main.rs for cli) with #![cfg_attr(not(feature="std"), no_std)] + extern crate alloc; for the 8 no_std cores (per ADR-003). Deps wired per spine: codec→{vlq,model}; json→model; builder→{model,codec}; consumer→{model,codec}; compose→{consumer,builder}; index→{model,builder}; edit→builder; cli→all.
  • smap-model src/{position,segment,decoded,sourcemap,location,error}.rs: field-exact structs from spine; Segment/Position derive Clone,Copy,Debug,PartialEq,Eq; DecodedMap(pub Vec<Vec<Segment>>) (per ADR-004).
  • Error enums: VlqError{UnexpectedEof,InvalidBase64Char(u8),Overflow} (smap-vlq); CodecError{BadFieldCount(usize),Vlq(VlqError),NegativeColumn,SourceIndexOob} (smap-codec); JsonError{Parse(String),BadVersion(u8),MissingField(&'static str)} (smap-json) — all derive Debug, impl Display+core::error::Error (per §6.2, INV-10).
  • Stub fns compile but return placeholder: encode_vlq/decode_vlq, encode_mappings/decode_mappings, to_json/from_json/extract_source_mapping_url, builder/consumer/MagicString methods → unimplemented!() EXCEPT the minimal VLQ+codec path the smoke test exercises (single 4-field positive-delta segment).
  • smap-cli clap-derived subcommand enum (encode/decode/validate/compose/flatten/query/inspect/extract-url) → all arms unimplemented!(); only --version/--help functional. The 0-based↔1-based boundary (per §6.4, MM5) lives ONLY here.
  • CI/tooling: rust-toolchain.toml (channel 1.78, components rustfmt+clippy); .github/workflows/ci.yml matrix {stable, 1.78 MSRV} (per ADR-023); Makefile targets fmt check clippy build test smoke; multi-stage Dockerfile (builder→distroless); deny.toml (advisories/licenses/bans) (per ADR-024).

0.3 Deliverable

  • Compiling 10-crate workspace; cargo build --workspace + cargo build --workspace --no-default-features (no_std cores) both succeed.
  • smap --version runs; one passing integration test tests/smoke.rs in smap-codec.
  • Green CI on stable + 1.78; make smoke exits 0.

0.6 Pitfalls

  • no_std purity leak: a String/Vec from std instead of alloc compiles under default features but breaks --no-default-features. Root cause: missing extern crate alloc. Debug: build each core crate --no-default-features in CI from day one.
  • gen_column reset not modeled in shells: a stubbed encoder that omits the per-; reset will "pass" the smoke test (single line) yet hide the Phase 3 invariant. Mitigation: smoke test uses 1 line — document that line-reset is NOT covered here.
  • Derive mismatch: Segment lacking PartialEq makes the round-trip assert_eq! uncompilable. Root cause: forgetting derives on the model. Fix: derive PartialEq,Eq on all model structs.
  • Cargo cyclic dep: accidentally making model depend on codec creates a cycle. Debug: cargo metadata / build error "cyclic package dependency". Keep arrows one-directional per spine.
  • clippy MSRV drift: pedantic lints that need >1.78 fail the MSRV job. Fix: gate or #[allow] with rationale; lock clippy via rust-toolchain.toml.

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • None functional this phase. Establish baselines only: record cargo build --workspace cold time (target <30s on CI), incremental rebuild <3s.
  • Pre-size nothing yet; the smoke path allocates 1 String + 1 Vec<Vec<Segment>>. Reserve real perf targets (millions of segments) for Phase 12.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty DecodedMap → empty "" mappings (no ;).
  • Trailing ; semantics: N semicolons = N+1 generated lines (deferred to Phase 3, but shell decode must not panic).
  • 1-field segment (generated-only) shell must construct without source/orig_* set — Segment.source=None.
  • All-unimplemented!() CLI arms must not abort the binary at startup (only when invoked).

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
Crate won't compile CI build stage block merge; one crate ≠ whole workspace
Smoke round-trip mismatch assert_eq! on Segment dump encoded string + decoded struct
no_std regression dedicated --no-default-features job fail fast, name offending crate
MSRV regression +1.78 matrix leg pin/replace the API, document in ADR

0.10 Operational Notes

  • Branch phase-0-skeleton; PR gated on full CI matrix green.
  • make smoke is the canonical local gate before push; mirrors CI exactly.
  • Dockerfile produces a smap image (distroless) for reproducible later-phase runs; tag smap:phase0.
  • deny.toml + cargo deny run in CI; advisory DB refreshed weekly.
  • Tag the repo v0.0.0-skeleton once green to mark the verifiable end-to-end baseline.

13. Phase 1 — Base64 VLQ codec

Implements smap-vlq in full: the Base64 alphabet + reverse table, encode_vlq, decode_vlq, VlqError. no_std + alloc. The single integer hot path under every encode/decode path. Sign-bit+magnitude, NOT two's complement.

1.1 Objectives

  • Encode signed i64 → Base64 VLQ; decode Base64 bytes → i64, advancing a caller-held cursor.
  • 5-bit groups low→high; bit 5 (value 32) = continuation; LSB of value = sign (1 = negative).
  • Total function: never panic on arbitrary &[u8]; all errors via Result<_, VlqError>.
  • Range: encode any i32-fitting source delta; decode rejects values outside [i32::MIN, i32::MAX] (Overflow).
  • Non-goal: mappings grammar (Phase 2/3), JSON (Phase 4). This crate knows only single integers.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • File crates/smap-vlq/src/lib.rs (#![no_std] + extern crate alloc); tables.rs; error.rs.
  • pub const B64: [u8; 64] = A..Z a..z 0..9 + / (indices 0..63). const B64_REV: [i8; 256] = reverse, -1 for non-alphabet (build via const fn loop or build.rs-free literal).
  • encode_vlq(value: i64, out: &mut String): let mut u = if value < 0 { ((-value) as u64) << 1 | 1 } else { (value as u64) << 1 }; loop: let mut d = (u & 0x1f) as u8; u >>= 5; if u != 0 { d |= 0x20; } out.push(B64[d as usize] as char); until u == 0. Always ≥1 char (value 0 → "A"). Sign-magnitude encoding per §8.1 (not two's complement; per MM1).
  • decode_vlq(input, pos) -> Result<i64, VlqError>: accumulate into u64 result, shift 0,5,10…; per char: b = input.get(*pos).ok_or(UnexpectedEof)?; *pos += 1; let v = B64_REV[b as usize]; if v < 0 { return Err(InvalidBase64Char(b)); } let cont = v & 0x20; result |= ((v & 0x1f) as u64) << shift; shift += 5; if shift > 32+5 && cont != 0 { return Err(Overflow); } exit when cont == 0. The i64 accumulator width guards against narrowing overflow (per ADR-006, INV-11).
  • Extract sign: let neg = result & 1; let mag = result >> 1; let signed = if neg != 0 { -(mag as i64) } else { mag as i64 }; range-check mag <= i32::MAX as u64 + 1 (allow i32::MIN); else Overflow (per INV-11). The sign is the LSB of the pre-grouped value, orthogonal to the continuation bit (per MM1).
  • pub enum VlqError { UnexpectedEof, InvalidBase64Char(u8), Overflow } — #[derive(Debug,Clone,PartialEq,Eq)], impl core::fmt::Display. Every decode path returns Result — never panics on any input (per §6.2, INV-10).

1.3 Deliverable

smap-vlq crate: encode_vlq, decode_vlq, B64, VlqError exported. cargo test -p smap-vlq green incl. proptest round-trip + fuzz-byte non-panic. Builds under --no-default-features (no_std target e.g. thumbv7em-none-eabi).

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

ID Check Target
A1 round-trip decode(encode(v)) == v ∀ v ∈ i32 proptest 100k cases pass
A2 known vectors (table 1.5) exact byte match
A3 decode on arbitrary &[u8] ≤ 16B never panic; only Ok/Err (proptest 1M)
A4 pos advance == chars consumed byte-exact
A5 overflow input (7+ cont groups) Err(Overflow), no UB
A6 encode 0 "A", 1 char

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

tests/fixtures/vlq/:

File Input → Expected
vectors.tsv 0→A, 1→C, -1→D, 2→E, -2→F, 16→gB, 123→2H, -123→3H, 1073741823 (i32::MAX/2)→…, 2147483647→+/////D, -2147483648→//////D
boundary.tsv i32::MIN, i32::MAX round-trip
malformed/eof.bin "g" (cont set, truncated) → UnexpectedEof
malformed/badchar.bin "=" / "." / space / 0xFF → InvalidBase64Char(b)
malformed/overflow.bin "ggggggggggg" → Overflow

1.6 Pitfalls

  • Sign as two's complement — root cause: treating MSB/native sign instead of LSB magnitude flag → -1 decodes wrong. Debug: assert encode(-1)=="D".
  • -i32::MIN overflow in encode — (-value) as u64 on i64 holding i32::MIN is safe only because widened to i64 first; do not narrow before negate. Debug: unit-test i32::MIN.
  • shift overflow / silent truncation — accumulating >6 groups shifts past 32 bits; without the shift>37 && cont guard a huge map decodes garbage instead of Overflow. Debug: feed "ggggggggggg".
  • Reverse-table off-by-one for +// — easy to misplace; +=62, /=63. Debug: B64_REV[b'+']==62.
  • Empty output — forgetting the do-while shape drops value 0 (loop body never runs). Debug: encode(0)=="A".
  • Signed-byte index i8 negative — using b as usize after sign-extend; index by raw u8. Debug: bytes ≥128 must hit -1 lane, not panic.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Table-driven encode/decode: B64/B64_REV arrays, no branchy char math; both fully branch-predictable.
  • encode_vlq writes via String::push (ASCII, single byte); caller pre-reserves in Phase 3. Target: ≥200M chars/s decode, ≥150M ints/s encode single-thread.
  • No heap alloc inside either fn; decode_vlq uses only stack accumulator. Inlinable (#[inline]) — sub-10ns per typical 1–2 char int.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • 0 → "A"; -0 impossible (i64 has no neg zero) but value==0 always positive branch.
  • i32::MIN (-2147483648): magnitude 2147483648 = 0x80000000, fits the +1 allowance; encodes 6 groups.
  • Single-group max (value ±15) → 1 char. Trailing chars after a complete VLQ ignored (cursor stops at continuation clear).
  • Leading-zero-padded groups ("ABA") — tolerated by decode (extra high zero groups), still bounded by overflow guard.
  • Decode at *pos == input.len() → immediate UnexpectedEof.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Detection
Off-by-one in all sources downstream sign/magnitude swap A2 vectors
Panic on fuzz as usize on signed, or [idx] not .get() A3 1M proptest
Wrong pos → cascading mapping corruption not advancing on each char / advancing past EOF A4
Accepts garbage as huge int missing overflow guard A5
Hang continuation loop without EOF check bounded by input.len()

1.10 Operational Notes

  • VlqError is internal; Phase 3 CodecError::Vlq(VlqError) wraps it; never surfaced raw to CLI.
  • cargo fuzz target fuzz_decode (Phase 11 wires corpus); local proptest covers CI gate.
  • MSRV 1.78; no unsafe; #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in crate. no_std verified in CI matrix via cross-build target with default-features = false.
  • Document the 0-based, codec-internal convention here is N/A — VLQ is convention-agnostic (raw integers); the 0/1-based boundary lives at the API/CLI layer (Phase 5/6).

14. Phase 2 — Data model and the mappings-string grammar

2.1 Objectives

  • Lock smap-model: Position, Segment, DecodedMap, SourceMap, OriginalLocation + index newtypes — exact layout, 0-based internal convention.
  • Specify (NOT yet encode/decode — that is Phase 3) the mappings string grammar as canonical EBNF + relative-delta semantics + per-field reset rules.
  • Encode invariants in code: field-count {1,4,5}, index-range bounds, per-line gen_column ascending sort, version == 3.
  • Provide invariant-checking constructors (Segment::with_*, DecodedMap::from_lines) that reject malformed shapes at construction, not at encode time.
  • Establish the 0-based ↔ 1-based boundary contract: conversion lives only at API/CLI edge, never inside model/codec.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File (crate smap-model) Adds
src/position.rs Position{line:u32,column:u32} (0-based); Position::new; Ord by (line,column)
src/segment.rs Segment (5 fields per spine); SegmentShape enum {Generated,Mapped,Named} = field-count 1/4/5; Segment::generated_only(col), with_source(...), with_name(...)
src/decoded.rs DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>); from_lines, line(n), iter_segments, validate()
