        │ from_json / decode_mappings /         │  into_sourcemap        │ (outside library)
        ▼ extract_source_mapping_url            ▼                        ▼
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  smap-json (bounded parse)  →  smap-model (validated)  →  smap-codec       │
  │  → smap-consumer/compose/index/edit  : Result<_,Err>, NEVER panic/IO       │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  • B1 raw bytes → from_json / extract_source_mapping_url: parse, depth/size caps, XSSI strip, data: validation.
  • B2 validated SourceMap → decode_mappings: field-count + VLQ-range + index-range checks.
  • B3 DecodedMap → consumer/compose/index/edit: index-bounds + offset-overflow + interval checks.
  • B4 library output → application: opaque sources/sourceRoot/sourceMappingURL labels handed back; library performs no fetch. Application decides fetch/sandbox.

25.2 Threat catalogue

# Threat Vector Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Decode DoS — billion-segment / huge mappings adversarial mappings string blows CPU/RAM hard caps: max_segments (def 50M), max_lines (def 10M), max_mappings_bytes (def 64 MiB); streaming line-at-a-time decode; pre-size from byte budget not attacker count running counters vs cap during decode decode.segments, decode.bytes, decode.cap_hit{kind} abort with CodecError::LimitExceeded; partial state dropped
T2 Pathological deltas / overflow VLQ deltas that overflow accumulators (prev_orig_line += huge) accumulate in i64, validate result into u32 (0..=u32::MAX); reject negative gen/orig column range check each field post-add decode.overflow{field} CodecError::NegativeColumn / VlqError::Overflow; no wrap
T3 VLQ integer overflow / sign confusion >6 continuation groups; flipped sign bit; value > i32 range decode_vlq caps shift at ≤32 effective bits (5+5+5+5+5+5 w/ guard), errors past it; sign = LSB of value (not two's-complement) — single decode path shift counter; magnitude check vlq.overflow, vlq.bad_char typed VlqError::{Overflow,UnexpectedEof,InvalidBase64Char}; caller maps to CodecError::Vlq
T4 JSON bomb — deep nesting / huge array / dup keys data:+JSON with 10⁵ nesting or giant sources[] bounded parser: max_depth (def 128), max_array_len, max_string_bytes; duplicate keys → last-wins and counted; no recursion w/o depth guard (iterative or depth-checked) depth counter; element counters json.depth_hit, json.dup_key, json.bytes JsonError::Parse w/ position; bounded memory
T5 Index OOB — source/name index source/name field ≥ sources.len()/names.len(); ignoreList entry OOB validate every index against arrays during decode_mappings / SourceMapConsumer::new range compare on each Segment decode.index_oob{kind} CodecError::SourceIndexOob; never index-panic
T6 Path traversal via sources/sourceRoot sources=["../../etc/passwd"], abs paths, UNC, file:// never resolve or open; treat as opaque labels; no path join/canonicalize inside library; expose raw strings only n/a (no fs op to detect) meta.source_label_suspicious (heuristic ../abs, advisory only) application sandboxes before any fetch; library returns label verbatim
T7 SSRF via sourceMappingURL / sources map points URL at http://169.254.169.254/… or internal host extract_source_mapping_url parses, never fetches; returns SourceMapRef::Url(String) for caller policy; inline data: decoded in-process only n/a in library url.extracted{scheme} caller's allowlist/SSRF guard decides; library does zero egress
T8 Source-code disclosure via sourcesContent proprietary source leaked when map shipped to prod embedding sourcesContent is opt-in in MapOptions/builder; ignoreList + a strip option drop content for vendored/internal sources; to_json omits empty sourcesContent policy lint: content present for ignoreList source emit.content_sources, emit.stripped strip before emit; default = no content unless requested
T9 XSSI / data: parsing tricks leading )]}', garbage prefix, mismatched media type, oversized base64 strip exactly one )]}'(+newline) prefix safely (no eval); validate data: media type ∈ {application/json, …charset…}; require ;base64, or treat as percent-encoded; cap decoded size by T1/T4 limits prefix/media-type check json.xssi_stripped, url.bad_media_type reject non-JSON media → JsonError::Parse; bounded decode
T10 Compose/flatten amplification chain of N maps each expanding segments; section offset overflow remap caps fold depth + cumulative segments (T1 caps apply to result); flatten adds offsets in i64, validates into u32; reject overlapping/unsorted sections per-step segment count; offset add check compose.depth, index.offset_overflow, index.overlap CodecError::LimitExceeded/SourceIndexOob; reject map
T11 Builder misuse → corrupt map unsorted/duplicate/negative positions from a buggy producer add_mapping records, into_sourcemap sorts by (gen_line,gen_column) + dedups + validates monotonicity; reject negative via u32 typing ordering check at finalize build.dedup, build.reorder finalize errors on unrecoverable; no silent corrupt output
T12 Supply-chain malicious/yanked transitive dep minimal deps (core crates no_std+alloc, zero-dep codec); deny.toml (advisories, license allowlist, multiple-versions, wildcards=deny); pinned versions; cargo-vet CI cargo deny check CI gate block merge on advisory/license/dup

25.3 Resource limits (defaults — all caller-overridable via *Options)

Limit Default Rationale
max_mappings_bytes 64 MiB bounds T1 input before decode
max_segments 50,000,000 ~real giant-bundle ceiling (Phase 12 scale)
max_lines 10,000,000 one Vec<Segment> per gen line
json.max_depth 128 v3 envelope is shallow; T4 guard
json.max_array_len 10,000,000 sources/names upper bound
json.max_string_bytes 256 MiB total sourcesContent aggregate cap
data: decoded size = T1/T4 caps inline maps share the same ceilings
remap chain depth 64 T10 amplification guard

25.4 Cross-cutting invariants

  • No panic on untrusted input — every parse/decode path returns Result; unwrap/expect/indexing-panic are CI-banned in smap-vlq/codec/json/model (clippy indexing_slicing, unwrap_used deny). Verified by Phase 11 fuzzing (T1–T5, T9 corpora).
  • No implicit I/O — smap-vlq/model/codec/builder/consumer/compose/index are no_std; only smap-json(parse only)/smap-edit/smap-cli touch std; none opens sockets or files for a map's sources/sourceMappingURL. Egress is the application's job (B4).
  • Paths are opaque — library never canonicalizes, joins, or opens sources/sourceRoot; mitigates T6/T7 by construction.
  • Caps before allocation — size/depth/count checked before growing buffers; pre-sizing derives from the byte budget, never from an attacker-declared count (T1/T4).
  • Determinism — same bytes → same Result; no time/locale/network dependence; reproducible builds.

25.5 Out-of-scope (deferred / non-goals)

  • WebAssembly DWARF / wasm source-map extension → PhaseDeferred (own threat model later).
  • JS parsing/transform safety → owned by #264 Bundler/Transpiler; this library never executes or interprets sourcesContent.
  • TLS / HTTP client hardening, DNS-rebind, allowlists → embedding application (library hands back opaque SourceMapRef::Url).

26. Failure Modes and Recovery

Taxonomy of the failure modes that produce silently wrong maps (the dangerous class) or hard errors. Severity scale: S1 = silent miscorrelation (stack trace -> wrong original line; worst, undetectable without fixtures); S2 = decode/parse hard-fail (loud, recoverable); S3 = degraded (lossy but not misleading). Each entry: symptoms · root cause · detection · automatic recovery · manual recovery · operator actions · forensic artifacts · long-term mitigation.

26.1 Severity ranking (read first)

# Mode Sev Silent? Layer
F1 Delta-reset bug (gen_column carried / others reset) S1 yes smap-codec
F2 Composition off-by-one (chain -> wrong original line) S1 yes smap-compose
F3 Consumer returns wrong segment (bias / search boundary) S1 yes smap-consumer
F4 Index-map offset arithmetic error S1 yes smap-index
F5 0-based / 1-based confusion at boundary S1 yes API/CLI edge
F6 UTF-16 vs byte column mismatch with producer S1/S2 usually smap-builder/edit
F7 sourcesContent misaligned with sources S2/S3 partial smap-json/builder
F8 Wrong field count (2 or 3 fields) S2 no smap-codec
F9 Malformed VLQ (truncated / overflow) S2 no smap-vlq
F10 JSON parse failure / bad version S2 no smap-json
F11 Stale / dangling sourceMappingURL S3 partial smap-json/CLI

F1 and F2 are the highest-severity modes: they corrupt every query without erroring. All others are loud or bounded. Conformance (Phase 11) + fixtures are the only defense against the silent class.

26.2 F1 — Delta-reset bug (HIGHEST severity)

The classic "off-by-everything." Each ; starts a new generated line; the reset rules are the #1 source of bugs.

  • Symptoms: small maps decode plausibly; multi-line maps drift — gen_column grows monotonically and never resets, OR source/orig_line/orig_column reset to 0 each line. Errors compound after line 1; line 0 looks correct (decoy).
  • Root cause: EncoderState/DecoderState reset the wrong subset at ;. Correct: gen_column resets to 0 per ;; prev_source / prev_orig_line / prev_orig_column / prev_name carry ACROSS lines. Common inversions: (a) carrying gen_column across lines; (b) resetting one of the four carried deltas at ;; (c) resetting on , instead of ;.
  • Detection: differential decode vs @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec over the corpus (Phase 11) — first divergence pins the line; known-good fixture delta_reset.map (>=3 generated lines, >=2 sources, non-monotonic columns) fails fast in CI; round-trip encode(decode(m)) == normalize(m) diverges on the first multi-line case.
  • Automatic recovery: none — output is structurally valid VLQ; nothing to catch at runtime. Guarded only at build time by the conformance gate, which MUST block release.
  • Manual recovery: delta-dump methodology — instrument DecoderState to log per segment (raw_field_deltas) -> (gen_col, source, orig_line, orig_col, name) and the state snapshot after each ;. Diff against the same dump from the reference codec; first row where the post-; snapshot differs localizes the mis-reset field. Confirm with a 2-line hand-encoded fixture.
  • Operator actions: maps already emitted are unrecoverable from the artifact alone (the wrong deltas are baked in); re-emit from source after fixing the codec. Quarantine all maps produced by the bad build.
  • Forensic artifacts: delta-dump log; offending mappings substring with ; positions marked; reference-codec diff; codec version stamp.
  • Long-term mitigation: single shared reset routine used by both encode and decode (one reset list, no duplication); property test "decode∘encode == id" on random DecodedMaps with >=4 lines; assert in DecoderState that gen_column is exactly 0 immediately after each ;.

26.3 F2 — Composition off-by-one (GRAVEST correctness failure)

A chain orig -> mid -> gen that traces a generated frame to the wrong original line — typically off by one, or to an adjacent statement.

  • Symptoms: single-link maps query correctly; composed maps are subtly wrong; debugger lands one line above/below the true source; error reporter blames the wrong function.
  • Root cause: in compose, looking up S's mid position with the wrong bias, or using gen instead of mid position as the lookup key; treating an unmapped mid position as mapped instead of emitting a 1-field segment; name not following the deepest mapping. Correct: for each segment S in mid_to_gen, look up S's original (mid) position in orig_to_mid with GLB; if found, composed segment maps gen -> that source/line/col(+name); if unmapped, emit a 1-field (generated-only) segment.
  • Detection: end-to-end chain fixture chain_3.map (A -> B -> C with hand-verified expected original for ~10 generated positions); assert remap([A_to_B, B_to_C]) query == direct A_to_C for every probe; associativity check compose(compose(a,b),c) == compose(a,compose(b,c)).
  • Automatic recovery: none — composed map is well-formed. CI chain fixture is the gate; release MUST block on failure.
  • Manual recovery: bisect-the-chain methodology — for a misbehaving probe position, query each link in isolation top-down: gen ->(C) mid, mid ->(B) upper, upper ->(A) original. The link where the isolated result first disagrees with the composed result is the faulty hop. Then delta-dump that single compose call: log per gen segment gen_pos | mid_lookup_pos | bias | mid_hit?(GLB) | resolved_orig | name_src. The first row whose resolved_orig differs from the bisect's link-by-link answer pins the bug (usually bias or key choice).
  • Operator actions: distrust ALL composed/remapped maps from the bad build (single-link maps are fine); re-run composition after fix. Flag downstream error-grouping that used wrong frames for re-aggregation.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-link query trace; compose delta-dump; chain fixture diff; the exact gen position + all three intermediate positions.
  • Long-term mitigation: compose expressed as fold of a verified binary compose (remap = fold); explicit unit test for the unmapped-mid -> 1-field-segment path; never reuse a Bias constant across builder vs consumer without a test; property test: composing through an identity map is the identity.

26.4 F3 — Consumer returns wrong segment (bias / boundary)

  • Symptoms: queries near segment boundaries return the neighbor; exact-column hits return the previous segment; LUB/GLB swapped.
  • Root cause: binary search returns insertion point not predecessor; <= vs < boundary error; GLB returning index instead of index-1 on a non-exact hit; sort not by (gen_line, gen_column).
  • Detection: boundary-probe fixtures querying exactly-on, one-before, one-after each segment column for both GLB (default) and LUB; assert exact matches return their own segment, gaps return GLB neighbor.
  • Automatic recovery: none (valid index, wrong answer). Optional debug-build assertion: returned segment's gen position <= query under GLB.
  • Manual recovery: dump the sorted by-generated index with computed search index for the failing probe; verify predecessor logic at the boundary.
  • Operator actions: re-query after fix; consumer-only bug, maps on disk are fine.
  • Forensic artifacts: sorted index slice around the probe; search index; bias used.
  • Long-term mitigation: exhaustive boundary property test (on/before/after every segment); shared search helper with documented GLB/LUB contract.

26.5 F4 — Index-map offset arithmetic error

  • Symptoms: flatten() of a sectioned map mis-locates positions after the first section; the first line of section N is shifted wrong.
  • Root cause: section offset misapplied — correct: offset.line added to every gen line in the section; offset.column added only to the first line of the section; sections MUST be sorted by offset and non-overlapping.
  • Detection: fixture of >=3 concatenated chunk maps with known flat expectation; non-overlap + ascending-offset invariant check; cross-check flatten() query vs per-section query plus offset.
  • Automatic recovery: reject overlapping/unsorted sections at build (CodecError); column-on-non-first-line never applied.
  • Manual recovery: dump each section's offset and the first/last gen position pre- and post-shift; verify the column-only-on-first-line rule.
  • Operator actions: rebuild index map; re-flatten.
  • Forensic artifacts: section offset table; pre/post-shift positions; overlap report.
  • Long-term mitigation: assert non-overlap + sorted in IndexedSourceMap construction; property test flatten-then-query == sectioned-query.

26.6 F5 — 0-based / 1-based confusion at the boundary

  • Symptoms: every line off by one in user-facing output; CLI query reports line N+1 or N-1.
  • Root cause: conversion done inside the codec/model instead of only at the API/CLI boundary. Invariant: lines and columns are 0-based everywhere inside smap-model/codec/consumer; 1-based conversion happens ONLY at the smap-cli / public-API edge.
  • Detection: golden CLI fixture asserting exact 1-based output; round-trip CLI->internal->CLI on a known position.
  • Automatic recovery: none; structurally valid.
  • Manual recovery: grep all + 1/- 1 on line/column; ensure exactly one conversion site, at the boundary.
  • Operator actions: re-run CLI/API after fix; internal maps unaffected.
  • Forensic artifacts: CLI output vs golden; list of conversion sites.
  • Long-term mitigation: newtype or doc-asserted boundary; single conversion function at the CLI layer; test internal values stay 0-based.

26.7 F6 — UTF-16 vs byte column mismatch

  • Symptoms: columns correct for ASCII, off after emoji/astral/multibyte chars; drift proportional to non-ASCII count on a line.
  • Root cause: Source Map v3 columns are UTF-16 code units; producer counted bytes or Unicode scalar values. smap-edit/builder must track UTF-16 columns at the boundary even though Rust strings are UTF-8 byte-indexed.
  • Detection: fixture with astral chars (e.g. 4-byte/2-unit) at known columns; differential vs a UTF-16-correct reference producer.
  • Automatic recovery: none if upstream miscounts; smap-edit computes columns via UTF-16 unit counting, not byte offsets.
  • Manual recovery: dump per-segment gen column alongside the source line's UTF-16 vs byte vs scalar counts; the divergence column reveals the unit error.
  • Operator actions: re-emit with corrected counting; quarantine maps over non-ASCII sources.
  • Forensic artifacts: offending source line; three column counts (byte/scalar/UTF-16); segment dump.
  • Long-term mitigation: explicit UTF-16 column helper in smap-edit; conformance fixtures with astral chars; document the unit contract at every column entry point.

26.8 F7–F11 — Loud / bounded modes

# Mode Detection Automatic recovery Manual / operator Mitigation
F7 sourcesContent misaligned (length != sources, content at wrong index) length-equality + index check on from_json/into_sourcemap pad/null-fill missing entries; reject length mismatch as JsonError::Parse in strict mode re-associate content to source index; verify interner order invariant sources_content.len() == sources.len(); index content by interned source id only
F8 Wrong field count (2 or 3 fields) decode_mappings validates count ∈ {1,4,5} -> CodecError::BadFieldCount(n) hard error with byte offset + segment ordinal; never silently pad inspect mappings slice at offset; identify truncating producer field-count assertion per segment; fuzz never panics, always returns BadFieldCount
F9 Malformed VLQ (truncated / overflow) decode_vlq -> VlqError::UnexpectedEof / InvalidBase64Char(u8) / Overflow hard error, pos left at fault byte; no panic on any input dump byte + position; check continuation bit at EOF; verify i64 range exhaustive fuzz (Phase 11); reject continuation bit set at input end; bound group count to i64
F10 JSON parse failure / bad version from_json -> JsonError::Parse/BadVersion/MissingField; XSSI )]}' stripped first error with field name / found version; strip XSSI before parse inspect head bytes for stray prefix/BOM; confirm version:3 require version == 3; tolerant of unknown keys (x_*); test XSSI + BOM + trailing comma corpus
F11 Stale / dangling sourceMappingURL extract_source_mapping_url returns URL; resolve/inline check separate inline data: decoded directly; external URL returned as SourceMapRef::Url for caller to resolve re-point or re-emit comment; verify map filename emit absolute-or-correct-relative URL; CLI extract-url + validate to catch dangling refs in CI

26.9 Cross-cutting recovery posture

  • Loud over silent: smap-vlq, smap-codec, smap-json fail with typed errors (never panic — Phase 11 fuzz invariant); the silent classes (F1–F6) are caught ONLY by the conformance + fixture gates, which are release-blocking.
  • Two debugging primitives, reused everywhere: (1) delta-dump — log running deltas + post-; state snapshots (F1) or per-segment compose resolutions (F2/F3); (2) bisect-the-chain — isolate each link's query to localize a composition fault (F2). Both diff against a known-good fixture or the reference codec.
  • Quarantine rule: any map emitted by a build that failed an S1 gate is presumed corrupt and re-emitted from source; on-disk artifacts carry the wrong data and cannot be repaired in place.
  • Provenance: every emitted map records producer + codec version (out-of-band) so a regression can be scoped to the exact build and all affected artifacts re-generated.

27. Operational Readiness

smap-* ships as library crates + the smap binary, not a 24×7 service. "Operations" = what an embedding host (transpiler #264, minifier, bundler, symbolication backend, error reporter) observes, alerts on, and run-books. The library is silent (no logging, no global state, no I/O outside json/edit/cli); all signals are typed errors + return values + an optional validation report the host wires into its telemetry.

27.1 Metrics a host exposes (instrument at the call boundary)

Metric Type Wrap point Why
smap.maps_decoded_total / maps_encoded_total counter from_json+decode_mappings / into_sourcemap+to_json volume/fan-out
smap.decode_bytes_per_sec hist bytes(mappings)/elapsed throughput regression
smap.segments_per_map hist per decode (Σ len(DecodedMap.0[i])) size distribution; outliers >1M
smap.query_latency p50/p95/p99 hist original_position_for/generated_position_for symbolication hot path
smap.decode_error_rate ratio Err(CodecError|JsonError)/total corrupt/incompatible upstream
smap.overflow_reject_rate ratio VlqError::Overflow/segments hostile/garbage input signal
smap.compose_chain_len / flatten_section_count hist remap len / sections.len() composition/section fan-in
smap.cache_hit_rate ratio host's SourceMapConsumer cache build-once/query-many efficiency

Label by stage {stage=transpile|bundle|symbolicate, source=upload|build}. The library exposes no clocks or metric sinks — the host times the call and records.

27.2 Introspection without a logger (on-demand, not ambient)

  • smap inspect <map> (--json) — decoded shape: counts, per-line segment counts, field-count histogram (1/4/5), ignoreList membership, gaps, max gen_column. Deterministic.
  • smap validate <map> — emits the validation report (27.3); exit code = severity.
  • --decode-trace=<line> — dumps the running deltas for generated line N (prev_gen_col reset, the four carried prev_*) after each segment → "where did the delta go wrong". Off by default, zero cost.
  • --explain-query <gen-line:col> — the segment chosen, bias used, the two straddling candidates, the resulting OriginalLocation → "why did this frame resolve here".
  • Library hook: a feature-gated DecodeTrace callback (no-op default) the codec calls per segment with (gen_line, segment, deltas) — streams the same data without the CLI.

27.3 Logging philosophy

The library does not log — no log/tracing macros in the no_std tier; it returns Result and the host decides severity/sampling/sink (a stray log line in a hot symbolication loop is a latency + PII hazard: source paths, sourcesContent). The structured alternative is a typed report, not text:

ValidationReport { errors: Vec<Finding>, warnings: Vec<Finding> }
Finding { code: ValidationCode, where: Loc, detail: String }   // Loc = byte offset / (gen_line, seg_idx)
enum ValidationCode {
  BadVersion, BadFieldCount, SourceIndexOob, NameIndexOob, NegativeColumn,
  UnsortedSegments, DuplicateMapping, SectionOverlap, SectionUnsorted,
  SourcesContentLenMismatch, MissingSource, EmptyMappings, TrailingGarbage }

std-tier (json/edit/cli) MAY emit to stderr (never stdout — reserved for map output); --quiet/-v/-vv. Library tier stays silent.

27.4 SLO-style targets

SLO Target Window Breach action
Zero-panic decode 100% on any input always sev-1; fuzz corpus + fix; never relax
original_position_for p99 / p50 < 100 µs / < 5 µs (warm, ≤1M seg) rolling 1h check consumer reuse / map size / index regression
decode throughput ≥ 150 MB/s mappings per release profile codec (table-driven B64)
decode error rate / overflow reject < 1% / < 0.1% rolling 1h bisect upstream emitter / inspect provenance
round-trip fidelity encode∘decode == identity per release (conformance) block release

Library SLI = return-value correctness + non-panic, not uptime. Availability is the host's; smap contributes determinism + bounded latency. Dashboard pins: top = correctness/safety (error + overflow rate, zero-panic PASS); middle = query p50/p95/p99 vs SLO; bottom = decode MB/s vs 150 floor and segments/map p99 vs 1M ceiling.

27.5 Incident response + runbooks

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Stack traces resolve to wrong lines/cols 0/1-based mismatch at boundary; bad bias; broken compose link smap validate; --explain-query L:C (confirm segment+bias); check 0↔1 conversion is at the edge only; bisect remap pairwise, --explain-query each hop
Off-by-one only on first column of a line gen_column not reset at ; (F1) codec round-trip; --decode-trace=<line> to see prev_gen_col reset
Vendored code shown as "user" code missing/incorrect ignoreList smap inspect --json → verify indices; re-emit with Phase 10 ignore policy
Decode slow / latency spike huge map; cold consumer rebuilt per query check segments_per_map; build SourceMapConsumer once, reuse; streaming decode (§24) for >N MB; confirm zero-copy path
SourceIndexOob/NameIndexOob table shorter than referenced index (truncated upload) smap validate; reject map, alarm decode_error_rate; no partial-trust
VlqError::Overflow burst corrupted/hostile mappings (>32-bit deltas) confirm via overflow_reject_rate; quarantine source; never panic
Composed map wrong after remap null segments not propagated, or names dropped verify null-segment propagation + names-follow-deepest (Phase 7); --explain-query across chain
SectionOverlap/SectionUnsorted on flatten sections unsorted/overlapping smap inspect sections; fix concat order (Phase 8 invariant)
Inline map not found in code wrong //# sourceMappingURL= form / data: URL not base64 smap extract-url <file> to confirm parse

Escalation: zero-panic violation = sev-1 page (memory-safety/DoS). Wrong-line symbolication = sev-2 (debuggability). Throughput/size regressions = sev-3 (ticket + bench).

27.6 Deployment, versioning, fetch policy

  • Crates publish under strict semver: any breaking public-item change (SourceMap, Segment, Bias, error enums, builder/consumer APIs) → MAJOR; a serialization change that alters bytes for the same input is MAJOR even if types are unchanged; error enums are #[non_exhaustive] so adding a variant is MINOR. smap ships as a static artifact (smap --version reports crate+build); subcommand/flag removal = MAJOR, additive = MINOR. Hosts pin minor; the Phase 11 conformance corpus gates every release. The no_std tier carries an explicit alloc-only contract for WASM/sandbox embedding.
  • Fetch policy (host responsibility, out of scope): smap parses sourceMappingURL and decodes inline data: maps but never fetches remote URLs — network retrieval, redirect/SSRF handling, size limits, and sourcesContent trust are the host's (Threat Model T6–T8). Recommended host guardrails: cap fetched size, allowlist schemes, treat sourcesContent as untrusted text (never execute, escape before render).

28. Compatibility Guarantees

Defines the stability contract across the smap-* workspace: what callers may depend on, what may change, and the migration path. Two axes: API stability (Rust surface, semver) and format compatibility (Source Map v3 wire format). Both are versioned independently of each other.

28.1 API stability (semver across the smap-* crates)

  • Versioning unit — each smap-* crate carries its own semver; the workspace ships them in lockstep at a shared 0.x / 1.0 line. Breaking a public item bumps MAJOR for that crate and every downstream crate that re-exports it (cargo semver-checks gate in CI, Phase 11).
  • Primary public surfaces (stable, semver-protected):
Surface Crate Stability tier Notes
SourceMapBuilder smap-builder Tier-1 (frozen at 1.0) generator contract; method set additive only
SourceMapConsumer smap-consumer Tier-1 query contract; Bias variants frozen
SourceMap struct smap-model Tier-1 field set = v3 envelope; new fields #[non_exhaustive]
DecodedMap, Segment, Position, OriginalLocation smap-model Tier-1 layout stable; field types frozen
encode_mappings / decode_mappings smap-codec Tier-1 signatures frozen
to_json / from_json / extract_source_mapping_url smap-json Tier-1
compose / remap, IndexedSourceMap::flatten, MagicString compose/index/edit Tier-2 semantics frozen; helper methods additive
EncoderState / DecoderState internals, interner internals codec/builder Tier-3 (internal) NOT public; may change any release
smap CLI flags/subcommands smap-cli Tier-2 subcommand set additive; flag removal = MAJOR
  • #[non_exhaustive] on SourceMap, all error enums (VlqError, CodecError, JsonError, SourceMapRef), and Bias → adding fields/variants is MINOR, not MAJOR; downstream match arms must keep a wildcard.
  • Constructors — SourceMap is built via SourceMapBuilder::into_sourcemap / from_json, never struct-literal across crate boundaries (the #[non_exhaustive] enforces this), so new fields never break construction sites.
  • No panics in the public API = part of the contract, not just a quality bar: decode_* / from_json / consumer queries return Result/Option; a panic on untrusted input is a semver-breaking defect (Phase 11 fuzz gate).

28.2 Format compatibility — Source Map v3 is pinned

  • Wire format = Source Map Revision 3 Proposal (TC39 source-map spec), pinned. version field MUST be 3; the codec/JSON layers target exactly this revision. No v1/v2 (version 1/2 → reject with JsonError::BadVersion); no speculative "v4."
  • version != 3 is rejected at from_json (BadVersion(u8)) — never silently coerced. Index maps: outer version also 3; each Section.map.version == 3.
  • Format revision is independent of crate semver: a new optional v3 field is read/written without an API MAJOR (it lands as a #[non_exhaustive] SourceMap field + builder setter, MINOR).

28.3 Additive fields — read tolerantly, emit optionally

Field Read Emit Mapping
ignoreList / x_google_ignoreList tolerant: accept either key, both → ignoreList wins optional, off by default; emitted only if non-empty SourceMap.ignore_list: Vec<u32>
future debug-id (debugId / x_com_bloomberg_sourcesFileId-class) parsed if present, retained round-trip via opaque slot emitted only when set reserved extension slot (28.7)
unknown top-level keys ignored on read, NOT retained unless in the extension slot never invented on emit —
  • Policy: unknown keys never error (from_json ignores them). Known additive keys round-trip. Emit stays minimal — absent fields are omitted, never written as null/[] placeholders (keeps output byte-comparable to references; see 28.8).

28.4 Tolerant-decode vs strict-encode (Postel — a hard, testable boundary)

Aspect Decode (tolerant) Encode (strict / canonical)
Trailing , / ;; empty groups (;;) accepted/ignored; blank lines accepted never spurious; blank only where semantic
Segment field counts accept 1/4/5; reject 2/3 (BadFieldCount) always minimal 1/4/5
Over-long / leading-zero VLQ decode if fits i32 else VlqError::Overflow minimal-length groups only
sources/sourcesContent length skew tolerated (pad/truncate documented) lengths kept consistent
null in sources/sourcesContent → Option::None preserved as null
XSSI )]}' prefix stripped if present not emitted (caller opts in)
OOB source/name index SourceIndexOob at decode-to-consumer builder interning makes OOB impossible

Decode→encode is value-identity (same DecodedMap), not byte-identity for loose inputs — re-encoding normalizes to a canonical, deterministic, reference-re-parseable form (§28.6). Normalization is intentional.

28.5 Column convention + MSRV

  • Columns are opaque u32 — codec/model assign no unit; producer and consumer agree out-of-band. Ecosystem default = UTF-16 code units (V8/DevTools/@jridgewell converged there; spec text is unit-agnostic). smap-edit (owns a buffer) documents its basis and offers byte↔UTF-16 helpers; everything else passes integers verbatim. Mixing byte- and UTF-16-column maps in compose/flatten is a documented caller error, not auto-detected. 0-based internal everywhere; 1-based only at the CLI/OriginalLocation display edge (§6.4, F6).
  • MSRV = Rust 1.78, edition 2021, pinned in every crate + CI (1.78 + stable + beta). MSRV bump = MINOR (never patch); no nightly features, no build.rs for cores. std into any no_std crate = MAJOR (feature-gate instead).

28.6 Additive fields + differential-parity guarantee

  • Additive procedure (each step keeps consumers green): add a #[non_exhaustive] SourceMap field defaulting empty/None (no construction site breaks); from_json reads it tolerantly, to_json emits only if set/non-empty; add a builder setter (MINOR); old consumers ignore the unknown JSON key, old producers never set it → forward+backward compatible. A reserved opaque extension slot retains unrecognized vendor keys (debugId-class) across a decode→encode round-trip. Unknown keys never error; emit stays minimal (absent fields omitted, never null/[] placeholders — keeps output byte-comparable to references).
  • Parity (CI, Phase 11) vs pinned references (@jridgewell/{sourcemap-codec,gen-mapping,trace-mapping}, Mozilla source-map, Sentry Rust sourcemap; versions in the CI lockfile): VLQ byte-for-byte; decode_mappings→equal segments + canonical-encode→equal string on canonical inputs; original_position_for/generated_position_for (both biases, incl. tie-break/edge) ≡ trace-mapping+Mozilla; into_sourcemap re-parses identically under gen-mapping/Mozilla. Parity is on observable semantics, not internal structures; where references disagree, the v3 spec wins (documented in conformance notes).

28.7 Index-map + inline-map compatibility

Index maps read/written per spec {version:3, file?, sections:[{offset:{line,column}, map}]}; flatten() yields a flat v3 map a v3-only consumer accepts; sections sorted+non-overlapping on emit, tolerant decode normalizes unsorted/touching on flatten. extract_source_mapping_url accepts //# (and legacy //@) for external URLs → SourceMapRef::Url and data:application/json;base64, → SourceMapRef::Inline; charset/;base64 variations tolerated; emit uses canonical //#. Closing contract: produce strictly, consume liberally, stay byte-parity-tested — any map smap emits (flat/index/inline) is consumed by the pinned references and DevTools/V8, and vice versa.

29. Testing Strategy

Test pyramid: unit (per-crate, per-rule) → property (round-trip + algebraic laws) → differential (vs source-map + @jridgewell) → integration (end-to-end pipelines) → fuzzing (never-panic) → golden fixtures (tsc/esbuild/terser) → criterion perf gates. Every class binds to a Section 7 invariant (cited INV-*). CI runs unit+property+differential+golden on every PR; fuzz on a nightly budget; perf gates on every PR with regression thresholds.

29.1 Test taxonomy & invariant mapping

Class Crate(s) Asserts INV
VLQ unit smap-vlq per-value-class encode/decode bytes INV-VLQ-SIGNMAG, INV-VLQ-CONT
Codec delta unit smap-codec per field reset/carry rule INV-COL-RESET, INV-CARRY, INV-FIELDCOUNT
JSON field unit smap-json each envelope field + XSSI + URL INV-VERSION3, INV-XSSI
Consumer query unit smap-consumer GLB/LUB boundary, miss INV-BIAS, INV-SORTED
Compose unit smap-compose mapped/unmapped/name cases INV-NULLSEG, INV-NAME-DEEPEST
Index unit smap-index flatten offset arithmetic INV-SECTION-SORT, INV-OFFSET
Edit unit smap-edit each op traces correctly INV-EDIT-TRACE
Property all round-trip + algebraic laws INV-ROUNDTRIP, INV-ASSOC
Differential codec/consumer/compose byte/query/applySourceMap equality INV-REF-PARITY
Fuzz vlq/codec/json no panic, no OOB, no UB INV-NOPANIC
Golden json/builder/compose exact bytes vs frozen fixtures INV-DETERMINISTIC

29.2 Unit tests

smap-vlq — value classes: 0→A; 1→C; -1→D; 15→e; 16→gB (continuation); -16→hB; i32::MAX/MIN; i64 near overflow → VlqError::Overflow. Malformed: empty &[]→UnexpectedEof; '='/'$'→InvalidBase64Char(b); truncated continuation (bit32 set, EOF)→UnexpectedEof. decode_vlq advances pos exactly past consumed bytes. Assert sign-bit + magnitude (NOT two's complement): decode("D")==-1.

smap-codec — one focused test per delta rule:

  • gen_column reset: [[0,_],...];[[5,_]] → second line's first field is absolute 5, NOT delta from prev line. INV-COL-RESET.
  • source/orig_line/orig_column/name carry across ;: decode a 2-line map, assert line-2 fields are deltas from line-1's last segment. INV-CARRY.
  • field-count: 1/4/5 accepted; 2/3-field segment → CodecError::BadFieldCount(n).
  • NegativeColumn when accumulated gen_column < 0; SourceIndexOob when source idx ≥ sources.len().

smap-json — per field: missing version→MissingField("version"); version!=3→BadVersion(n); sparse sources:[null]/sourcesContent:[null] preserved as None; sourceRoot absent→None; ignoreList↔x_google_ignoreList alias. XSSI )]}'/)]}\n stripped by from_json. extract_source_mapping_url: //# sourceMappingURL=foo.js.map→Url; //@ legacy form; data:application/json;base64,…→Inline (decoded); none→None.

smap-consumer — query/bias: build 3-segment line; query exact col → that segment; query between cols → GLB returns lower, LUB returns upper; query before first col → GLB None, LUB first; query past last → GLB last. generated_position_for uses by-original index. source_content_for returns embedded content or None.

smap-compose — cases: (a) mid position mapped in orig_to_mid → composed segment carries original source/line/col/name; (b) mid position unmapped → 1-field generated-only segment (INV-NULLSEG); (c) name resolution prefers deepest available mapping (INV-NAME-DEEPEST); (d) sourcesContent preserved from origin map.

smap-index — flatten: 2 sections at offsets (0,0) and (10,0) → section-2 gen lines shifted +10; intra-line offset.column added ONLY to section's first line (INV-OFFSET). Overlapping/out-of-order sections → CodecError. Single-section flatten == input map.

smap-edit — one test per op over a known buffer: overwrite(s,e,txt), append, prepend, remove, move, indent. Assert to_string() output AND that generate_map() maps each surviving original char to its new generated position; removed chars produce no mapping (INV-EDIT-TRACE).

29.3 Integration tests

Pipeline E2E: SourceMapBuilder add_source/add_name/add_mapping → into_sourcemap → to_json → from_json → SourceMapConsumer::new → original_position_for. Assert queried original positions equal those inserted (full loop, INV-ROUNDTRIP).

Real chain: TS source → JS (mid, map A) → bundle concat (smap-index or smap-compose) → minify (map B). remap(&[A, B]) → composed map; query a minified (line,col) → must land on the original TS identifier. Validates compose + names + sourcesContent across a realistic 4-stage pipeline.

CLI E2E: invoke smap binary — encode, decode, validate, compose, flatten, query, inspect, extract-url — over fixture files; assert exit codes + stdout. Verify 1-based↔0-based conversion happens only at the CLI boundary (query --line 1 --col 0 hits internal (0,0)).

29.4 Differential testing (reference parity — INV-REF-PARITY)

Shared corpus (§29.6 fixtures). For each fixture, compare against JS references via a Node harness producing JSON oracle output:

  • decode equality: decode_mappings vs @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec decode → identical DecodedMap (segment-by-segment).
  • query equality: original_position_for vs source-map SourceMapConsumer.originalPositionFor across a grid of generated positions, both biases.
  • applySourceMap equality: compose vs source-map SourceMapGenerator.applySourceMap/@jridgewell gen-mapping → equal decoded result.
  • encode is many-to-one (whitespace/dedup); compare DECODED equality, not raw mapping bytes, for encode parity.

Mismatch fails CI with a minimized diff (offending segment + both decodings printed).

29.5 Property tests (proptest)

Property Statement INV
VLQ round-trip decode_vlq(encode_vlq(v)) == v ∀ v: i64 in range INV-VLQ
Codec stability decode(encode(decode(m))) == decode(m) ∀ valid m INV-ROUNDTRIP
Builder fidelity output of into_sourcemap decodes to exactly the inserted (sorted,deduped) mappings INV-BUILDER
Compose assoc remap([A,B,C]) ≅ compose(compose(A,B),C) ≅ compose(A,compose(B,C)) (decoded eq) INV-ASSOC
Index↔flat IndexedSourceMap.flatten() queries == directly-built flat map queries INV-OFFSET
Edit total trace every retained original char maps to exactly one generated position; offsets monotonic INV-EDIT-TRACE

Generators: arbitrary DecodedMap with valid monotonic-per-line gen cols, bounded source/name indices; arbitrary edit-op sequences with in-bounds, non-overlapping ranges.

29.6 Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz, libFuzzer)

Target Input Oracle
fuzz_vlq raw bytes decode_vlq never panics; round-trips when valid
fuzz_mappings arbitrary string decode_mappings returns Ok/Err, never panics/OOB
fuzz_json arbitrary bytes from_json never panics; valid→to_json→from_json stable
fuzz_roundtrip structured DecodedMap (arbitrary) encode→decode == input

CI budget: 5 min/target/PR (smoke), 4 h/target nightly. Corpus + crashes committed under fuzz/corpus/, fuzz/artifacts/. Deterministic replay: each crash artifact is a regression unit test (cargo fuzz run T artifacts/crash-… reproduces deterministically; seed pinned). INV-NOPANIC: zero panics, zero UB under ASan/UBSan.

29.7 Golden fixtures

Frozen real-world maps under tests/fixtures/: emitted by tsc, esbuild, terser over a small TS app; plus pathological maps (sparse sources, sourcesContent:null, large ignoreList, index/sectioned maps, inline data: maps). Golden assertions: to_json byte-exact vs committed .expected.json (INV-DETERMINISTIC); decode→re-encode→decode stable. Regenerate via cargo xtask bless; diffs reviewed in PR. Fixtures double as the differential + perf corpus.

29.8 Coverage, perf gates, CI wiring

  • Coverage: cargo llvm-cov; gate ≥ 90% line / ≥ 85% branch on smap-vlq, smap-codec, smap-consumer, smap-compose (core correctness crates); ≥ 80% workspace-wide. PR fails below threshold.
  • Perf-regression gates (criterion, Phase 12 targets): benches for encode/decode (1M segments), JSON parse (zero-copy), query (binary search). critcmp vs committed baseline; fail PR on > 10% regression. Targets restated in Phase 12; gates enforce them here.
  • CI matrix: MSRV 1.78 + stable; --all-features and --no-default-features (verify no_std+alloc builds of smap-vlq/model/codec/builder/consumer/compose/index). miri run over smap-vlq + smap-codec unit tests (UB detection in delta arithmetic).
  • Determinism: identical inputs → identical output bytes across runs/platforms (no HashMap iteration order in emitted JSON; interners use insertion order). Asserted by golden + a same-input-twice equality test.

30. Build and Release Engineering

30.1 Reproducible builds

Lever Pin Rationale
rust-toolchain.toml channel="1.78.0", components=[rustfmt,clippy], targets=[x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu,aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu] MSRV == toolchain; CI + dev + release all build identical bits
Cargo.lock committed for the smap-cli bin; libs .gitignore their lock per crate convention bin = application → exact deps reproducible; libs resolve fresh
SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set to commit timestamp in release job deterministic mtimes in tarball
CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_* lto="fat", codegen-units=1, panic="abort", strip="symbols" smallest/fastest smap; abort OK (codec never unwinds across FFI)
RUSTFLAGS -C target-feature=+crt-static (musl), --remap-path-prefix=$PWD=/build static bin; path-independent debuginfo for byte-identical rebuild
  • Verify reproducibility: two independent cargo build --release --locked runs → sha256 of smap MUST match (gate in release.yml).
  • --locked everywhere in CI: any Cargo.lock drift fails the build, never auto-updates.

30.2 Workspace build/test matrix

Job Toolchain Targets / scope Command (abbrev)
test-stable stable full workspace cargo test --workspace --locked --all-features
test-msrv 1.78.0 full workspace cargo test --workspace --locked
no_std-check stable smap-vlq,smap-codec,smap-model cargo check -p <c> --no-default-features --target thumbv7em-none-eabi
fuzz nightly cargo-fuzz targets cargo fuzz run <t> -- -max_total_time=300 (PR), -runs=50M (nightly)
bench stable criterion cargo bench --workspace -- --save-baseline pr
  • no_std target thumbv7em-none-eabi (no host std, no alloc-via-std) proves the 3 core crates compile #![no_std] + alloc only. CI fails if any of the 3 pull in std.
  • smap-json/smap-edit/smap-cli are std-only → excluded from no_std-check (assert via per-crate #![cfg_attr(...)] doc-comment + a compile_fail test on accidental std use in core crates).
  • Feature matrix: each lib builds with --no-default-features AND --all-features; CLI builds default only.

30.3 Quality gates (blocking on every PR)

Gate Command Policy
fmt cargo fmt --all -- --check zero diff
clippy cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings deny all warnings; -D clippy::pedantic on codec/vlq hot paths
doc cargo doc --workspace --no-deps -D rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links no broken links
deny cargo deny check see 30.4
coverage cargo llvm-cov --workspace --fail-under-lines 85 core crates ≥ 95% line

30.4 Supply-chain auditing + SBOM

  • cargo-deny (deny.toml) checks 4 categories:
    • advisories — RUSTSEC DB; vulnerability=deny, unmaintained=warn, yanked=deny.
    • licenses — allow MIT,Apache-2.0,Unicode-DFS-2016,BSD-3-Clause; confidence-threshold=0.9; deny copyleft.
    • bans — multiple-versions=deny (dup-dep hygiene; small dep tree by design), wildcard * versions denied.
    • sources — only crates.io; no git/path deps in published graph.
  • cargo-auditable builds the smap bin with an embedded SBOM (dependency tree in a .dep-v0 section) → cargo audit bin smap re-audits the shipped binary post-release.
  • Emit a CycloneDX SBOM artifact (cargo cyclonedx -f json) attached to each GitHub Release.

30.5 crates.io publication

  • Publish order (strict — a crate's deps must already be live):
    smap-vlq → smap-model → smap-codec → smap-json → smap-builder
  → smap-consumer → smap-compose → smap-index → smap-edit → smap-cli
  • Workspace-unified version: all 10 crates share one version via [workspace.package]; intra-workspace deps use { path=..., version="=X.Y.Z" } (exact pin) so a release is internally consistent.
  • Semver stamping: a release tag vX.Y.Z drives the version. cargo-release bumps [workspace.package].version, updates the =X.Y.Z intra-deps, commits, tags, then cargo publish -p <c> per crate in the order above, with a publish-availability poll between crates (crates.io index lag).
  • Semver discipline: cargo-semver-checks gate on the public API of every lib crate before publish; a breaking change without a major bump fails the job. smap-vlq/smap-model/smap-codec are the stability-critical surfaces (downstream #264).
  • [package.metadata.docs.rs] sets all-features=true so docs.rs renders no_std + std paths.

30.6 smap binary release artifacts

Triple OS/arch linkage
x86_64-unknown-linux-musl Linux x86_64 static
aarch64-unknown-linux-musl Linux aarch64 static
x86_64-apple-darwin macOS Intel dynamic (system libSystem)
aarch64-apple-darwin macOS Apple Silicon dynamic
x86_64-pc-windows-msvc Windows dynamic
  • Each artifact: tarball/zip + .sha256. Signing: cosign sign-blob (keyless, OIDC) → .sig + .pem; macOS builds additionally codesign + notarize. Provenance: SLSA build attestation via actions/attest-build-provenance.
  • Naming: smap-vX.Y.Z-<triple>.{tar.gz,zip}. Release notes auto-generated from Conventional-Commit history.

30.7 .github/workflows/ topology

Workflow Trigger Jobs
ci.yml push, PR fmt, clippy, test-stable, test-msrv, no_std-check, deny, coverage (matrix fan-out)
fuzz.yml nightly cron, manual cargo-fuzz: fuzz_vlq, fuzz_mappings, fuzz_json corpora; upload crash artifacts; never-panic assertion
bench.yml PR (label perf), main push criterion vs committed baseline; regression > 5% on encode/decode/query → fail + comment
release.yml tag v* reproducibility check → build matrix (5 triples) → sign/attest → SBOM → GitHub Release → ordered cargo publish
  • ci.yml is concurrency-grouped (cancel-in-progress per ref); release.yml is NOT (releases never cancel).
  • Caching: Swatinem/rust-cache keyed on Cargo.lock + toolchain; fuzz corpora cached across nightly runs.

30.8 Conformance-corpus snapshot pinning

  • The Phase 11 corpus (Source Map v3 spec vectors, @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec + reference source-map fixtures) is vendored under tests/corpus/ and pinned by content hash, NOT fetched at test time → hermetic, offline-reproducible, immune to upstream churn.
  • Provenance manifest tests/corpus/SOURCES.toml: each fixture records upstream URL, git SHA / npm version, license, sha256. A verify-corpus CI step recomputes hashes → drift fails.
  • Refresh is a deliberate PR (xtask update-corpus) that re-pins hashes + bumps the manifest; never automatic. Differential-decode baselines (encode/decode/query golden outputs) are regenerated only alongside a corpus refresh and reviewed as part of that PR.

31. Performance Engineering

Performance contract derives from the section-4 targets; this section operationalizes them: the benchmarks that gate each target, the profiling that diagnoses regressions, and the data-layout / codec decisions that earn the numbers. Hot path = smap-vlq::decode_vlq -> smap-codec::decode_mappings (byte-at-a-time loop), and smap-consumer::original_position_for (binary search). Cold/build path = into_sourcemap sort + encode, consumer index build, flatten, compose.

31.1 Section-4 target restatement (gates)

ID Metric Target Bench owner Phase Gate (regression fails >)
M1 VLQ decode throughput ≥ 400 M ints/s (1 core) bench_vlq Phase 1 (8%)
M2 VLQ encode throughput ≥ 250 M ints/s bench_vlq Phase 1 (8%)
M3 decode_mappings ≥ 250 MB/s of mappings text bench_codec Phase 2 (5%)
M4 encode_mappings ≥ 200 MB/s of output bench_codec Phase 2 (5%)
M5 from_json (full parse) ≥ 150 MB/s envelope bench_json Phase 3 (5%)
M6 original_position_for ≤ 0.3 µs p50, ≤ 1.5 µs p99 bench_query Phase 6 (10% p99)
M7 Consumer index build ≤ 40 ms / 1 M segments bench_query Phase 6 (10%)
M8 compose (chain depth 2) ≤ 2× sum of decode+query bench_compose Phase 8 (10%)
M9 flatten (N sections) ≤ 1.2× decode of concatenation bench_index Phase 9 (10%)
M10 Peak RSS, decode 1 M seg ≤ 56 B/segment resident dhat profile Phase 12 (12%)

segment reference cost: Segment = 5x u32 + 2 niche-packed Option<u32> -> 20 B; Vec<Segment> per line + outer Vec overhead amortizes to the 56 B/seg ceiling including index vectors.

31.2 Benchmark strategy (criterion)

Micro-benches (criterion, --bench, 100 samples, 3 s warmup, outlier-trimmed):

  • bench_vlq: encode/decode over fixed mixed-magnitude int streams (1-byte 60% / 2-byte 30% / 3-5-byte 10%, matching observed delta distributions). Report ints/s; assert no heap alloc inside decode loop.
  • bench_codec: encode_mappings / decode_mappings on synthetic DecodedMaps (10 K / 100 K / 1 M segments, mean 6 seg/line). Report MB/s vs mappings byte length (the true denominator — throughput is per-byte, not per-segment).
  • bench_json: from_json / to_json on full envelopes incl. sourcesContent; measure parse separated from mappings decode (lazy boundary).
  • bench_query: index build (timed once via iter_batched with SmallInput), then original_position_for / generated_position_for query loop (build-once / query-many; the build MUST NOT be inside the timed query closure). p50/p99 via criterion's measured distribution + a --features hdr HdrHistogram harness for tail.
  • bench_compose, bench_index: chain depth 2-4; section counts 1/16/256.

Macro-benches (bench_real, gated --features corpus): multi-MB real maps — minified react-dom, a webpack vendor chunk, a tsc --sourceMap emit, an esbuild bundle. Measures end-to-end from_json -> consumer build -> 10 K random queries. These guard against synthetic distributions hiding real branch behavior.

Bench data lives in bench/fixtures/; large corpus maps fetched by make corpus (not committed). CI runs micro-benches on a pinned runner; critcmp diffs against a stored baseline JSON; regression beyond the per-metric threshold fails the job.

31.3 Profiling methodology

Tool Target What it answers
perf stat -e cache-misses,branch-misses,instructions decode hot loop branch-miss % (target < 2%), IPC (target > 2.5)
cargo flamegraph (perf/dtrace) macro-bench run where wall time goes; expect codec > json > query
perf record -e branch-misses + perf annotate decode_vlq which branch mispredicts (continuation test)
dhat / dhat-rs full decode+build alloc count, peak bytes, B/segment
valgrind --tool=cachegrind decode loop D1/LL miss rate on the reverse-Base64 table
iai-callgrind (CI) VLQ + codec instruction-count stability (deterministic, no noise)

Hot-loop counters captured on the decode_mappings 1 M-segment bench. Acceptance: branch-miss < 2%, D1 miss < 5% (the 256-entry reverse table fits in L1: 256 B). iai-callgrind gives deterministic instruction counts for CI so regressions show even on noisy shared runners where wall-clock criterion is unreliable.

31.4 Codec micro-optimization (the wins)

  • Table-driven Base64: 256-entry [i8; 256] reverse table (-1 = invalid) replaces branchy range checks. Single load + sign test per byte. Forward B64: [u8; 64] table for encode. Win: ~3-4x over comparison-chain decode; eliminates the alphabet-class branch.
  • Byte-at-a-time decode: operate on &[u8], never char/UTF-8 iteration — mappings are pure ASCII by spec. Advance a usize cursor; no bounds re-check beyond the loop guard. Avoids UTF-8 decode overhead (~2x on the scan).
  • Continuation as data, not branch: accumulate (digit & 31) << shift; loop while digit & 32 != 0. The one unavoidable data-dependent branch; keep its body tiny so misprediction cost is minimal.
  • Sign extraction last: VLQ is sign-bit+magnitude — let neg = value & 1; value >>= 1; if neg { -value } else { value } after accumulation, not two's complement. One predictable branch (signs ~50/50 but cheap conditional-negate; consider branchless (value ^ -neg) + neg).
  • Pre-sized output buffers: encode_mappings reserves decoded.0.iter().map(|l| l.len()).sum() * 6 + lines up front (≤6 bytes/segment-field heuristic) -> zero reallocation mid-encode. decode_mappings pre-sizes the outer Vec<Vec<Segment>> by counting ; in one pass (cheap memchr), then per-line Vecs grow geometrically.
  • memchr for ; / ,? No — segment scan is fused with VLQ decode (we must decode fields anyway), so a separate delimiter scan only helps the line-count pre-pass. Use memchr only there.

31.5 Allocation analysis

  • Zero-copy JSON borrows: from_json borrows &str slices of the input for sources / names where the JSON has no escapes (fast-path scan); allocates owned String only on escape or when the SourceMap must outlive the input. mappings is borrowed as &str and decoded lazily — never copied into SourceMap.mappings redundantly. sourcesContent (often the bulk of envelope bytes) is borrowed/lazily materialized, not eagerly cloned.
  • DecodedMap layout: Vec<Vec<Segment>> — outer = generated line. Trade-off: per-line Vec = 24 B header each; pathological maps with millions of 1-segment lines waste headers. Mitigation (Phase 12): optional flat CSR layout — one Vec<Segment> + Vec<u32> line-offset array — behind a builder flag for query-heavy consumers; cuts per-line overhead to 4 B/line. Default stays Vec<Vec<_>> for edit-friendliness.
  • Interner cost: add_source / add_name use a HashMap<&str,u32> over arena-held strings; O(1) amortized, dedup at insert. No per-mapping allocation in add_mapping (writes into a pre-grown per-line buffer).
  • Acceptance: decode of 1 M segments = O(lines) allocations (one per generated line + amortized growth), not O(segments). dhat asserts alloc count ≤ 2 × line-count + constant.

31.6 Consumer: build-once, query-many

  • Build cost: sort segments by (gen_line, gen_column) for the by-generated index (already grouped by line in DecodedMap, so it is per-line sort of small runs — near-linear), plus a by-original index sorted by (source, orig_line, orig_column) for generated_position_for. Build = O(S log(S/L)) ≈ O(S) for typical seg/line; ~40 ms / 1 M seg.
  • Query cost: original_position_for = binary search within the target generated line's segment slice — O(log k), k = segments on that line; with Bias::GreatestLowerBound (default) pick greatest ≤, LeastUpperBound least ≥. ≤ 0.3 us p50. generated_position_for binary-searches the by-original index.
  • Build dominates only if queries are few. Amortization crossover: index build pays off past ~10^4 queries; below that, the lazy/streaming path (31.7) wins. CLI query for a single position uses lazy decode + a single linear-or-bisect scan, skipping full index build.

31.7 Streaming / lazy decode (query-only)

  • SourceMapConsumer::new can defer full DecodedMap materialization: decode line-by-line on demand for original_position_for when only a handful of frames (stack-trace symbolication) are queried. Keep a Vec<u32> of byte offsets to each generated line in the mappings string (built in one cheap pass) so a query jumps to its line, decodes just that line's segments, binary-searches, discards. Memory: O(lines) offsets, not O(segments) decoded. Use case: an error reporter resolving < 50 frames against a 5 MB map — avoids decoding millions of segments.
  • Trade-off: repeated queries re-decode lines -> cache decoded lines in a small LRU, or fall back to full build past a query-count threshold.

31.8 Composition & index cost

  • compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen, …): one consumer lookup per segment of mid_to_gen -> O(S_gen · log) where the log is the orig_to_mid per-line search. Linear in output segments. remap folds compose over a chain: cost ≈ Σ per-stage compose; depth-d chain = d-1 composes, each materializing an intermediate consumer (reuse the prior result's consumer to avoid rebuild). Null-segment (unmapped mid position) path emits a 1-field segment — branch is rare on well-formed maps, predicts well.
  • flatten (index map): O(Σ section segments); offset arithmetic is add-only (offset.line to every gen line; offset.column only to the section's first line). Target ≤ 1.2x a plain decode of the concatenation — overhead is purely the per-segment line/col add + re-encode.

31.9 SIMD / branch-reduction (optional, gated)

  • Reverse-Base64 validation + delimiter scan are SIMD-amenable (std::simd / core::arch SSE2/AVX2/NEON, behind --features simd): vectorized classify-and-validate of 16/32 bytes, find next ;/, via pmovmskb. Realistic win on the scan, but VLQ decode is inherently serial (running deltas) — SIMD does NOT vectorize the delta accumulation. So SIMD targets validation/delimiter-finding only; expect +15-25% on decode of segment-dense maps, 0% on the accumulate. Default OFF (portability + no_std cores); measured before shipping.
  • Branch reduction: branchless sign negate (31.4); precompute per-line segment counts to make the inner segment loop a counted for (better unrolling) instead of delimiter-driven while.

31.10 Tail latency & pathological maps

Pathology Effect Mitigation
Millions of single-segment lines per-line Vec header blowup CSR flat layout (31.5)
One line with 10^6 segments O(log) query still fine; build sort cost on one huge run parallel/merge sort that run if > threshold
Huge orig_column deltas (5-byte VLQ) slower decode, more cont-bit branches accounted in mixed-magnitude bench; overflow guarded -> VlqError::Overflow
Adversarial deep section nesting flatten recursion iterative flatten, bounded by section count
50 MB sourcesContent RSS spike lazy/borrowed content; never eager-clone

Tail SLA: original_position_for p99 ≤ 1.5 us even on the 1-line-10^6-segment map (binary search is log-bounded). Decode p99 governed by the worst-case 5-byte VLQ density; the mixed-magnitude bench's 10% multi-byte tier must keep decode_mappings ≥ 250 MB/s — that is the binding gate tying this section back to the section-4 throughput target.

32. Scalability Analysis

Cost model for the smap-* toolkit across the four scaling axes — segment count n, source/name cardinality s/k, chain length L, map byte size B. All asymptotics assume the LOCKED DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>) representation.

Cost vs. segment count n

Operation Crate Complexity Notes
decode_mappings smap-codec O(n) time, O(n) space single forward pass; DecoderState carries 5 running deltas; no sort
encode_mappings smap-codec O(n) time, O(B) space iterate sorted DecodedMap; emit ≤5 VLQ fields/segment
SourceMapBuilder::into_sourcemap smap-builder O(n log n) sort by (gen_line, gen_column) dominates; dedup O(n) linear scan post-sort
SourceMapConsumer::new smap-consumer O(n log n) builds by-gen index (already gen-sorted per line → O(n)) + by-orig index (full sort O(n log n))
original_position_for smap-consumer O(log n) per-line gen array binary search; bias GLB/LUB resolved in O(1)
generated_position_for smap-consumer O(log n) binary search on by-orig index keyed (source, orig_line, orig_column)
all_generated_positions_for smap-consumer O(log n + m) m = matches at that original line

Constants: decode is the cheap pass (no sort); the by-orig index build is the consumer's dominant cost — amortize by building lazily on first generated_position_for call, skip entirely for pure stack-frame (original_position_for-only) workloads.

Cost vs. source/name cardinality s, k

  • Interners (smap-builder): add_source/add_name use HashMap<&str→u32> → O(1) amortized insert/lookup; total interner memory O(Σ source_path_len + Σ name_len + 2·(s+k)·sizeof(entry)).
  • sources_content: O(Σ |content|) — the largest non-segment allocation; embedding all source bodies dwarfs the mappings string. Gate via embedding policy (Phase 10).
  • Index newtype (source:Option<u32>, name:Option<u32>) keeps per-segment width independent of s,k — cardinality affects only interner/string memory, never segment array stride. s,k ≤ 2³² hard ceiling (u32).

Memory scaling of DecodedMap (dominant cost)

Per-Segment budget (5 fields):

gen_column u32       4
source     Option<u32> 4   (niche-optimized: Option<u32> = 4? NO — u32 has no niche)
orig_line  u32       4
orig_column u32      4
name       Option<u32> 8   (Option<u32> = 8 w/ padding; no niche on u32)
---------------------------------
struct size ≈ 24 B (with alignment; Option<u32> costs a discriminant word)
  • DecodedMap overhead = n·~24 B (segments) + L_g·24 B (outer Vec<Vec<Segment>> headers, L_g = generated-line count) + capacity slack.
  • 1 M segments ≈ 24 MB resident; 10 M ≈ 240 MB; 100 M ≈ 2.4 GB → the practical RAM wall.
  • Optimization lever (Phase 12): replace Option<u32> with a u32 sentinel (u32::MAX = None) → segment shrinks to 20 B, niche reclaimed, ~17% reduction; or struct-of-arrays (5 parallel Vec<u32>) for cache-friendly encode scan. Both internal-only; LOCKED public Segment shape unchanged.

Zero-copy vs. owned tradeoff (smap-json):

Strategy sources/names/mappings Pro Con
Owned (String) copied out of JSON map outlives input buffer; mutable builder 2× transient peak (input + owned)
Zero-copy (&str/Cow into input) borrow JSON slices ~0 alloc for sources/names; halves peak map lifetime tied to input; no mutation

Default from_json → owned SourceMap (LOCKED type holds String). Zero-copy is a Phase-12 read-only fast path for query-only consumption of large maps; the mappings string is borrowed, decoded segment-by-segment on demand.

Streaming / lazy-decode lever

  • Problem: materializing DecodedMap for a query-only or huge map wastes O(n) memory when only O(queries) segments are touched.
  • Lever: decode-one-line-on-demand. Pre-scan mappings for ; byte offsets → Vec<u32> line-start index (O(B) scan, O(L_g) memory, ~4 B/line). original_position_for(gen) decodes only line gen.line (O(segments-on-that-line)), caches it.
  • Wins: peak memory drops from O(n·24 B) to O(line-index 4 B/line + decoded-line cache). A 240 MB map → ~few-MB line index for sparse query load.
  • Caveat: cross-line carried deltas (source/orig_line/orig_column/name) require a per-line carry checkpoint — store the DecoderState snapshot at each ; (O(L_g·20 B)) so any line decodes in isolation without replaying predecessors. Without checkpoints, lazy decode of line i is O(segments ≤ line i), not O(segments on line i).

Parallelism

  • Many maps: SourceMap/DecodedMap/SourceMapConsumer are immutable values post-construction → decode/build/query trivially parallel across distinct maps (bundler emitting N chunk maps, error reporter resolving N stack frames against shared &Consumer). &SourceMapConsumer is Sync → fan out frame resolution lock-free.
  • Single huge map decode: mostly serial — the running-delta state machine is inherently sequential within a line's field stream. Per-line parallelizable: split mappings on ; (parallel byte scan for line boundaries), decode each line independently only if the cross-line carry (source/orig_line/orig_column/name) is resolved first. Two-pass: (1) serial O(L_g) scan computing each line's entry carry-state (cheap — sums first-segment deltas), (2) parallel per-line VLQ decode. Speedup ≈ cores for the field-decode pass, which is ~90% of decode time.
  • Composition/flatten: each chain link / section processed independently then merged → parallel across links/sections.

Cost vs. chain length L (composition)

  • compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen): for each of n_gen segments, one original_position_for lookup into orig_to_mid → O(n_gen · log n_mid) per link.
  • remap(chain) folds compose over L maps → O(Σ_links n_i · log n_{i-1}); with bounded per-link sizes, linear in total segments O(Σ n_i) times a log factor. compose is associative → fold order does not affect result, only intermediate sizes.
  • Null-segment propagation (unmapped mid position → 1-field segment) does not grow n; composed map ≤ size of mid_to_gen.

Bottlenecks and caps (mitigation)

Bottleneck Trigger Cap / mitigation
RAM wall multi-hundred-MB map → 100 M+ segments → 2.4 GB DecodedMap MAX_SEGMENTS cap (default 64 M); switch to streaming lazy-decode above threshold
Adversarial billion-segment input hostile JSON with huge mappings reject via MAX_MAPPINGS_BYTES (default 256 MB) before decode; never pre-allocate from untrusted length
Quadratic carry replay naive lazy decode of late line per-line carry checkpoints (O(L_g·20 B)) → O(1) line seek
by-orig index build consumer over huge map, query-only lazy / on-first-generated_position_for; skip for original-only workloads
sourcesContent blowup embedding all bodies, large repo embedding policy off by default for vendored/ignoreList sources (Phase 10)
VLQ overflow on huge deltas delta > i64 range VlqError::Overflow — bounded, never panic; reject map
String reallocation in encode unsized output buffer pre-size String to ~n·6 B estimate; single allocation

All caps are configurable constants; defaults chosen so a legitimate ~50 MB production map decodes in well under target latency while a billion-segment attack is rejected before allocation. Memory ceiling per invocation enforced; streaming path keeps a huge map's query workload O(line-index + cache) rather than O(n).

33. Mental Models & First Principles

Expert compressed insights: what each predicts, the bug class without it, where this blueprint applies it.

ID Mental Model Expert Insight Predicts Bug Class Without It Resolving §
MM1 VLQ is sign-magnitude, not two's complement The sign is stored in the LSB of the pre-grouped value; continuation is bit 5 of each group. These are two orthogonal bit fields on two different levels. Which bit patterns encode -1 vs +1; maximum magnitude for N groups -1 decodes as a large positive; i32::MAX+1 silently wraps; off-by-everything on negative deltas §8.1, §13 (Phase 1)
MM2 The mappings decoder is a running-delta state machine with asymmetric reset Five delta accumulators. prev_gen_col resets to 0 at every ;. The other four carry across ;. This is not a symmetric reset — it is a precise invariant. Why every line's first segment has a column relative to 0, but an original-line relative to the last segment of the previous generated line Carrying gen_column across lines (columns explode) or resetting prev_source on ; (every line starts at source 0) — both produce wrong but parse-valid maps §6.3, §8.3, §15 (Phase 3)
MM3 Composition is a fold of partial functions over a shared domain compose(A, B) = for each point in B's domain, look it up in A, yield A's value if it exists, else yield a "hole" (1-field segment). remap = left-fold compose. The algebra is a partial-function composition, not a join. Why unmapped intermediate positions must produce 1-field (generated-only) segments, not be dropped; why compose is associative Dropping unmapped-mid segments (removes generated columns); snapping to nearest (fabricates origins); losing associativity (fold order changes result) §6.5–6.6, §19 (Phase 7)
MM4 Binary search for nearest is a GLB/LUB question, not an equality question Source map queries almost never hit an exact segment; the goal is "greatest segment ≤ query" (GLB) or "least segment ≥ query" (LUB). These are classical lower/upper-bound problems on a sorted array. Which standard partition_point variant to use; why a hash map gives wrong answers; why the chosen bias must be consistent throughout compose Using == (no match → wrong segment or panic); returning the insertion point rather than the predecessor; swapping GLB/LUB in compose vs query §6.8, §8.8, §18 (Phase 6)
MM5 0-based inside, 1-based at the boundary — one seam, nowhere else The entire codec/model/consumer operates in 0-based coordinates. The only place that converts is the CLI/API surface. One conversion seam means one place to audit and one place to test. Where every +1 / -1 on a line/column field is a bug Stray +1 inside the codec shifts every downstream column; a missing -1 in the consumer shifts every query result; "everything is one line off in the UI but tests pass" §6.4, §7.7 (INV-6), §14 (Phase 2)
MM6 Index-map offset is a "first-line-only column shift" The section offset.column applies only to the section's first generated line. Subsequent lines of the section carry their natural gen_column (relative to 0). This is a spec rule, not a convention. Why a 2-line section whose offset is (5, 7) has column 7 + seg_col on gen line 0 but just seg_col on gen line 1 Applying offset.column to all lines (shifts every non-first line sideways by the section offset) §6.14, §8 (Domain Model), §20 (Phase 8)
MM7 A source map is the inverse function of a multi-stage transformation A minified JS file is the image of a composition of partial functions (tsc → bundle → minify). The source map is a representation of the preimage function: given a point in the image, recover the corresponding point in the domain. Composition = chaining the preimage functions. Why there must be exactly one composed map for a pipeline; why stage order in remap is deepest-first; why a 1-field segment means "no preimage at this point" Reversing the chain order in remap; treating composition as map metadata merging rather than function chaining §2.2, §6.5, §7.9 (INV-8)
MM8 The trust boundary is at every decode entry point The library never fetches sources or URLs. Every byte of map JSON, VLQ, and sourceMappingURL is attacker-controlled. The library's job is to return a typed error for any malformed input, never panic, and never allocate without a cap. Why size caps must precede allocation; why unwrap in a decode path is a DoS Panic on truncated VLQ (DoS in a build server); OOM on a billion-segment map; path traversal via sourceRoot if library resolved paths §4.4, §6.2, §25 (Threat Model)

34. Comparative Architecture Table

This design vs four real systems in the source-map / position-mapping space.

Dimension smap (this design) Mozilla source-map @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec + gen-mapping + trace-mapping Sentry Rust sourcemap crate Chrome DevTools / V8 consumer
Language / runtime Rust, no_std+alloc core, std at I/O edge JavaScript (Node/browser) JavaScript (Node/browser) Rust, std C++ (V8 engine)
Crate/module granularity 10 crates, strict layered DAG (vlq→model→codec→builder/consumer→compose/index) 1 npm package, monolithic 3 separate packages (codec, gen-mapping, trace-mapping) 1 crate Embedded in V8, no public boundary
In-memory segment layout Vec<Vec<Segment>> outer=gen line, Segment=5×u32 (~24 B/seg) Array of objects per mapping (~128 B+ per mapping object, GC-managed) Flat typed arrays (Int32Array), column-interleaved Vec<SourceMapIndex> with owned strings CSR-style flat arrays in C++ with pointer indices
Codec hot path Table-driven 256-entry reverse-Base64 LUT, byte-at-a-time on &[u8], no UTF-8 decode charCodeAt loop on JS string, per-char branch Optimized typed-array scan, still JS allocation on each segment Safe Rust loops, no SIMD C++ with SIMD optional
Generator API SourceMapBuilder: sort-on-finalize (into_sourcemap), interner returns stable u32 indices addMapping + sort-on-serialize; sorts entire array on each serialize gen-mapping: insert sorted (binary search on each add, kept sorted) Builder via SourceMapBuilder type, immediate sort No public generator; consumers only
Consumer query / bias Dual sorted indexes (by-gen, by-orig), explicit Bias enum, GLB default Flat sorted array, GREATEST_LOWER_BOUND/LEAST_UPPER_BOUND flags trace-mapping: cached decoded segments, explicit bias Single sorted array, no explicit bias type Sorted flat array, GLB only
Composition compose + remap fold, null-segment propagation, associativity-tested applySourceMap via consumer lookup No built-in compose; users chain gen+trace No built-in compose No composition API
Sectioned (index) maps IndexedSourceMap, flatten(), offset arithmetic validated Read-only support for sections; no flatten No sectioned-map support IndexedSourceMap type, lookup_token Reads sectioned maps; no construction
Tolerant decode Explicit --tolerant flag vs strict mode; both return Result Always tolerant (silently skips bad segments) Strict by default Tolerant by default Always tolerant (browser robustness)
Untrusted-input safety #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in codec/vlq/json; size caps; cargo-fuzz gate No DoS caps; panic possible on hostile input No DoS caps; no fuzzing infra No explicit caps; Rust memory safety C++ with size limits in browser sandbox
no_std / WASM no_std+alloc for 7 core crates, designed for WASM targets Browser-native JS; no WASM API Browser-native; WASM build exists for codec only std-required V8-embedded; no standalone

Mozilla source-map. Chose a monolithic package with per-segment JS objects and always-tolerant decode. This wins inside a webpack or Babel build running in Node.js: the GC handles allocation, the ecosystem already has V8, there's no WASM target requirement, and error-throwing on malformed input would break existing toolchains that produce messy maps. The one-package surface is a practical maintenance win for a JS ecosystem tool with a single primary consumer.

@jridgewell/sourcemap-codec + gen-mapping + trace-mapping. Chose deliberate package separation (codec / generate / query) with flat typed arrays and insert-sorted generation. This wins for maximum JS-ecosystem throughput: typed arrays are JIT-friendly and GC-transparent; the codec is the hottest path (often called millions of times) and can be optimized in isolation without touching the generator or consumer. The 3-package split lets a bundler pull only what it needs. The absence of composition support is intentional — bundlers compose at a higher level than the codec.

Sentry Rust sourcemap crate. Chose a single std Rust crate with owned data everywhere and tolerant-by-default decode. This wins for a server-side symbolication backend: the single crate is simpler to audit and deploy; std libraries are available; no WASM requirement; the tolerate-by-default policy matches the use case (Sentry must decode maps from arbitrary third-party JS toolchains, not panic, and produce a best-effort result). The lack of composition support reflects that Sentry receives final maps, not intermediate pipeline stages.

Chrome DevTools / V8. Chose a C++ CSR-style flat-array layout embedded in V8 with no public construction API and always-tolerant decode. This wins for the browser runtime: the consumer runs inside a GC'd V8 heap with strict memory budgets; C++ with SIMD is the only way to hit the latency targets for breakpoint resolution; tolerant decode is mandatory (a bad map must never crash a page's debugger); the absence of a generator API reflects that the browser is a pure consumer, never a producer. The design is tightly coupled to V8's heap layout and cannot be extracted.

35. Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived step-by-step from the System Constraints table (§4).

# Quantity Formula Result Notes
C1 Bytes per segment (DecodedMap resident) 5 fields × 4 B (u32) + 4 B (Option<u32> for source) + 4 B (Option<u32> for name, +pad) = 5×4 + 4 + 8 ~24 B/segment Option<u32> has no niche; actual struct has alignment padding → measured 24 B
C2 RAM for 1 M-segment map (DecodedMap) 1,000,000 segments × 24 B/seg 24 MB segments + ~line_count × 24 B Vec headers Per §4.1 ceiling: 64 MB excl. sourcesContent; segments = 24 MB leaves ~40 MB for indexes + strings
C3 RAM for dual consumer indexes (by-gen + by-orig) by-gen: 1M × ~16 B (gen_line, gen_col, seg ref); by-orig: 1M × ~24 B (source, orig_line, orig_col, gen back-ref) ~40 MB index overhead Total at 1M segments: 24 MB (DecodedMap) + 40 MB (indexes) = 64 MB → matches §4.1 ceiling
C4 Encoded mappings string size 1,000,000 segments × ~7 chars/segment avg (typical field deltas are small; 1-2 Base64 chars each, ~5 fields avg 1.4 chars) ~7 MB mappings string Real maps: react-dom.min.js.map mappings ~3 MB for ~500K segments = 6 B/seg
C5 VLQ decode throughput ceiling from hardware CPU processes ~1 Gops/s on a 1-wide scalar loop; each VLQ decode is ~5 ops (table lookup + shift + accumulate + cont check + advance pos) 1e9 / 5 = 200M ints/s §4.1 target: >200M ints/s/core; matches hardware budget; table-driven LUT reduces the branch to 1 predictable test
C6 from_json zero-copy parse budget Input: 50 MB JSON envelope. Allocation ceiling §4.1: < 1.2× input size → < 10 MB overhead. Sources/names/mappings borrowed from &str → 0 copy; Vec of &str slices: N_sources × 16 B ptr+len 100K sources × 16 B = 1.6 MB pointer overhead Well within 1.2× budget; the risk is eager-clone of each source string (adds 50 MB overhead); zero-copy path keeps it at ~few MB
C7 Consumer index build time at 1M segments Sort: 1M × log₂(1M) = 1M × 20 = 20M comparisons; at 200M comparisons/s → 100 ms. by-orig needs full O(n log n) sort; by-gen leverages existing per-line order (near-linear) ~40–100 ms §4.1 target: < 100 ms; by-orig is the binding constraint
C8 Binary search query depth log₂(1M) = 20 comparisons ≤20 comparisons/query §4.1: p50 < 1 µs; at 3 ns/comparison → 60 ns → well within budget; p99 includes cache miss (~50 ns extra)
C9 encode_mappings throughput target Output: 7 MB mappings for 1M segments; target: < 200 ms → 7 MB / 0.2 s = 35 MB/s minimum; §4.1 target: > 200 MB/s (from the overall throughput target) Target: 7 MB in < 35 ms Pre-sized String::with_capacity(7M) ensures single allocation; actual bottleneck is the VLQ encode loop, not allocation
C10 sourcesContent memory at embedding-enabled Typical TS project: 100 source files × 50 KB each = 5 MB content. smap-builder stores one String per source (deduplicated by interned index). 5 MB content + 24 MB segments = 29 MB total With embed_sources_content=true; the 64 MB ceiling per §4.1 comfortably holds both
