C11 Section count limit for flatten performance flatten is O(Σ_sections × segments_per_section) = O(total_segments). At 1M total segments split across 1000 sections, flatten cost = same as single-section decode: < 200 ms. No section-count penalty given fixed total segments The per-section decode overhead (DecoderState init) is O(1) per section; 1000 sections adds ~1000 ns total → negligible
C12 VLQ overflow guard: max safe groups i64 accumulates up to 63 bits. VLQ groups carry 5 bits each; 7 groups = 35 bits (covers 32-bit signed with sign bit). 8th group would bring us to 40 bits but no valid i32 needs it. 7 groups max before Overflow §4.3: VLQ groups ≤ 7; reject on the 8th group → VlqError::Overflow; prevents i64 overflow accumulation

36. Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each question requires synthesis of ≥2 sections; none is answerable from a single line.

  1. If you encode a 3-line map with 2 sources and 5 segments, then decode it back, which fields are you asserting byte-identical and which are you asserting only semantically equal? What inputs would break byte-identity but preserve semantic equality? (§4.2, §8.3, Phase 3 §3.4)

  2. An error reporter ingests a data:application/json;base64,... map from an untrusted third-party. List every attack surface and the library's mitigation at each layer, from the raw bytes to the final original_position_for result. Where does the library stop defending and the host begin? (§4.3–4.4, §25 Threat Model, §6.2)

  3. A 4-stage TypeScript→JS→bundle→minify pipeline produces a map where a minified frame resolves to the wrong TypeScript line by exactly +1. Walk through the bisect-the-chain methodology to isolate the fault. Which compose argument is most likely at fault, and what specific field would you delta-dump first? (§19 Phase 7, §26.2–26.3, §7.9 INV-8)

  4. Given that compose is claimed to be associative, write the property test that would catch a failure. What three concrete maps (A, B, C) and what six probe positions would you choose to maximize coverage of the null-segment-propagation and name-deepest-mapping invariants? (§6.5–6.6, §7.9 INV-8, §29.5)

  5. The by-original index in SourceMapConsumer is more expensive to build than the by-generated index, yet many workloads never call generated_position_for. Describe the lazy-build strategy, its memory cost, and the cache-invalidation edge case when the consumer is shared across threads via Arc<SourceMapConsumer>. (§18 Phase 6, §32 Scalability, §11.7 §6.2)

  6. A production smap flatten call on an index map returns segments whose columns are wrong by exactly offset.column on every generated line of a section except the first. What is the root cause? What is the exact fix? Write the property-test invariant and the minimal fixture that catches it. (§6.14, §20 Phase 8, §7.10 INV-9, §26.5 F4)

  7. You discover that two CI runs with identical source inputs produce different mappings strings. Walk through every source of nondeterminism in the pipeline—from add_source/add_name ordering through into_sourcemap sort to to_json—and identify which ADRs prevent each one. What would still break if you replaced the interners' insertion-ordered maps with HashMap? (§6.11, §9 ADR table ADR-011 ADR-013, Phase 5 §17, §29.7)

  8. The Source Map v3 spec says columns are in "UTF-16 code units". Explain why this matters only at the API boundary, not inside smap-codec, and write the exact conversion a smap-edit column-tracker must perform for a line containing "hello 🎉 world". How many UTF-16 code units is the emoji? How does this interact with generate_map's column tracking? (§6.4, §8.3, §28.5, Phase 9 §21, F6)

  9. A build tool wants to use smap-index for concatenated modules instead of smap-compose. Under what conditions is smap-index the cheaper choice? Give the Big-O comparison. When does it degrade to being equivalent in cost? When does it produce a semantically different result from smap-compose? (§20 Phase 8, §32 Scalability, §6.13–6.14)

  10. Describe the "carry-checkpoint" approach to lazy per-line decode. Why can't you simply jump to a ; offset and decode from there without the checkpoint? What is the exact data structure for the checkpoint, its memory cost for a 10M-line map, and how it fits within the §4.1 memory ceiling? (§32 Scalability streaming section, §8.3 delta reset, §12.2 Phase 12)

  11. If from_json silently ignores unknown JSON keys (forward-compat policy), and a future spec adds a debugId field, what is the exact additive-procedure sequence (per §28.6) to support it? At which step does it become a MINOR semver change? What would make it a MAJOR change? (§28.1–28.3, §28.6, §9 ADR-024)

  12. A fuzz run discovers a crash in decode_vlq on the 8-byte input \x67\x67\x67\x67\x67\x67\x67\x67 ("gggggggg"). Write the exact expected error path, the accumulator state at each byte, and the VLQ group where the overflow guard fires. What VlqError variant is returned and what does the byte offset carry? (§8.1, §13 Phase 1 §1.6, §7.12 INV-11)

  13. A smap-consumer built on a 1M-segment map is shared via Arc<SourceMapConsumer> across 32 threads, each symbolizing a different stack trace. Describe the locking/synchronization model, the memory cost, and any contention hotspot. Contrast with using 32 independent consumers on the same decoded data. (§11.7, §18 Phase 6, §32 Scalability, §11.1 Core Schema §6.2 P2-P3)

  14. In the Threat Model, T6 says the library never resolves paths. But Phase 10 defines path normalization in EmitMapper::finish. Precisely delineate what normalization is safe inside the library vs what is the host's responsibility. What attack is prevented by the library's boundary? What attack is the host still responsible for? (§25 T6–T7, §22 Phase 10, §6.9 P-sources)

  15. smap-edit::MagicString.move(s, e, index) relinks a chunk range. What invariants must the by_start: BTreeMap satisfy after the move? Does generate_map after a move produce segments with the moved chars' output positions mapping to their original locations? Write the exact assertion a test should make for a move-before-first-char operation. (§21 Phase 9, §7.14 INV-13, §11.10 smap-edit types)

  16. Compare the --tolerant vs default strict-decode modes from the perspective of composability. If you decode a map in tolerant mode and then re-encode it, is the result guaranteed to be strict-mode-decodable? What invariant makes this true? What pathological tolerated input would violate it if the invariant were missing? (§6.7, §15 Phase 3 §3.9, §28.4, INV-2 §7.3)

  17. Given the phase-by-phase timeline of 24 weeks, which two phases have the highest risk of timeline slip, why, and what early mitigation is specified in their N.6 Pitfalls? How do the conformance fixtures in Phase 11 serve as the risk backstop for the entire preceding 10 phases? (Appendix B, §15 Phase 3 §3.6, §19 Phase 7 §7.6, §23 Phase 11)

37. Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Four fundamentally different architectures for the same system; each: ASCII sketch, winning regime, real embodiment, rejection reason.

Alt-1: Single flat Vec<Segment> with embedded gen_line field (CSR-style)

mappings: "AAAA,KAAIA;ACEJ"
                     |
              decode to:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ flat: Vec<Segment> with gen_line field   │
│ [Seg{gen_line:0,gen_col:0,...},           │
│  Seg{gen_line:0,gen_col:5,...},           │
│  Seg{gen_line:1,gen_col:0,...}]           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────┘
                     |
              + line_starts: Vec<u32>  (one offset per gen line)
              line_starts = [0, 2]    → binary search for line k

Regime where it wins: Query-only consumers that never sort or build per-phase; large maps with millions of single-segment lines (no per-line Vec header overhead — headers dominate RAM in the Vec<Vec<>> layout); read-once symbolication over a flat segment stream. The V8/Chrome DevTools consumer uses essentially this layout internally for its decode path.

Real system: Chrome DevTools / V8 source_map_consumer.cc uses a flat segment array with a parallel line_offsets array. The CSR (Compressed Sparse Row) layout is also the canonical representation for sparse matrices in scientific computing.

Why rejected: The locked DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>) representation mirrors the ";" structure of the mappings grammar directly — each inner Vec is one generated line. This makes the reset rule (gen_column reset per ";", others carry) natural to implement (the outer-Vec iteration IS the line boundary). Per-line Vec also enables natural chunk editing in smap-edit (each inner Vec is a mutable slice of one generated line). The flat CSR layout is noted as a Phase-12 optimization path for query-only paths behind a builder flag, but the public Segment type stays unchanged.

Alt-2: Event-driven streaming codec (no DecodedMap, callback per segment)

from_json(text) → SourceMap (envelope only, mappings opaque)
                                |
                                ▼
decode_mappings_streaming(mappings, |seg| { callback(seg) })
          ← zero allocation beyond line-start index
          ← no Vec<Vec<Segment>> materialized
          ← segments consumed one-by-one, in order

Regime where it wins: Single-pass consumers that need to scan every segment exactly once in order (coverage tools, linters, sourcesContent strippers, map size analyzers); giant maps (>100 MB) that would OOM if fully materialized; server-side tooling where query is always sequential and the consumer never re-queries the same position twice. The @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec has a streaming-oriented internal API for exactly this reason.

Real system: @jridgewell/sourcemap-codec decode returns a SourceMapSegment[][] (allocates) but has an underlying streaming-friendly internal path; tools like magic-sdk's map walking use single-pass callbacks. DWARF line-program readers (e.g. in LLVM's libDWARF) are fully streaming with no materialized table.

Why rejected: The primary consumers of this toolkit — smap-consumer, smap-compose, smap-index — all require random-access to segment arrays (binary search, multi-pass traversal, per-section random access). A streaming-only codec cannot support original_position_for or compose without first materializing the segments anyway. The streaming path is provided as an additive API (decode_mappings_into) in Phase 12 for the coverage/scan use case, but the default interface materializes DecodedMap because random-access is the primary need.

Alt-3: Persistent/immutable-tree segment store with structural sharing

SourceMap immutable tree:
  root: PersistentVec<PersistentVec<Segment>>
  (each Vec is a persistent balanced tree, e.g. a 32-way B-tree / Clojure PersistentVector)

smap-compose: share unchanged lines across map versions
smap-edit: structural diff produces a new version, shared prefix

Regime where it wins: Incremental/watch-mode build tools that update the map as single files change; diffing two maps to produce a delta for hot-module-replacement; in-memory version-history of maps in a language server (undo/redo of transforms); tooling where many map versions are live concurrently (e.g. a history-aware debugger). Clojure's PersistentVector and Scala's immutable collections use this model; it is the foundation of most Clojure/ClojureScript tooling's source-map handling.

Real system: Clojure's cljs.source-map toolchain uses hash-array-mapped tries (HAMTs) for its segment store; the immutability model matches ClojureScript's generally immutable data philosophy.

Why rejected: The dominant use case is build (emit once, write to disk) and query (decode once, query many times against a fixed map). Neither requires versioning or structural sharing. The overhead of a persistent tree — extra pointer indirections, higher constant factors for iteration, incompatibility with no_std+alloc (HAMT typically needs HashMap or similar) — would degrade the hot decode/encode path and the binary-search query. Structural sharing is noted as a future direction for watch-mode build-tool integration with smap-compose, but the core representation stays Vec<Vec<Segment>>.

Alt-4: Source maps encoded as a sorted k-d tree / interval tree over 2D position space

(gen_line, gen_col) ─────────────── 2D point store
(orig_source, orig_line, orig_col) ─ satellite data

        Interval tree:
        each node covers a range of generated positions
        binary search replaces the dual-index approach
        range query: "all mappings in gen box [l0,l1)×[c0,c1)"

Regime where it wins: Devtools features that need range queries rather than point queries: "show all mappings in generated lines 50–100" (coverage highlighting), "find all original positions for a generated function body" (semantic navigation), "diff which mappings changed between two builds" (incremental highlighting). A pure interval tree also avoids the by-generated / by-original dual index build cost when both are needed simultaneously.

Real system: LLVM's DWARFDebugLine uses a sorted PC-range table with binary search, not an interval tree, but tools like addr2line's gimli Rust library cache it as a sorted vec with range semantics. The Firefox DevTools had an interval-tree-based source map consumer prototype for range-based coverage visualization.

Why rejected: The primary query is a point query (given one generated position, find one original), not a range query. A sorted flat array with binary search (the locked design) is strictly faster and simpler for point queries than any tree structure. The dual by-gen/by-orig index is the minimum structure needed to answer both original_position_for and generated_position_for in O(log n) with a single shared sorted store per query direction. Range queries (for coverage) are served by the linear-scan path over DecodedMap, which is already O(total segments) and simpler to implement. The interval-tree overhead would be paid on every decode and every query, for a range-query capability that is not in the primary use case.

38. Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred(...) call anywhere in this blueprint (§1.3, §1.8) is registered here; each anchor resolves to a real heading, each row names the phase that would build the feature, and the concrete, evidence-based trigger that promotes it out of deferral. None of the three is scheduled inside Phases 0–12 (## 12–## 24); all three are explicit non-goals (§1.3) gated by demonstrated downstream demand, not by default roadmap position.

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
WebAssembly DWARF / wasm source-map extension §1.6 Unscheduled — would open a Phase 13 (post-v1) A downstream consumer (the #264 Bundler/Transpiler emitting a wasm target, or a wasm-targeting debugger) needs DWARF-linked original positions, backed by profiling/demand evidence — see the parallel note under ADR-024 (§9), which gates the related but distinct SIMD-JSON/fused-compose optimizations on Phase 12 profiling rather than deferral
debug-id / build-id extensions (Sentry-style stable map identity) §1.6 Unscheduled — would open a Phase 13 (post-v1) A hosted/observability consumer requires a stable map identity across rebuilds for symbol-server lookup (the Sentry debug_id pattern), with a concrete integration requesting it
Hosted symbolication service (upload, store, server-side query) §1.6 Unscheduled — would open a Phase 13 (post-v1) An operator requests server-side symbolication beyond local smap-consumer embedding, with a defined trust boundary and multi-tenancy model — see T7 (SSRF, §25) for why the library itself never fetches a sourceMappingURL

Two related items are noted for completeness but are not register entries: they carry no PhaseDeferred(...) call of their own and are optimization variants of already-in-scope phases, not deferred features. SIMD JSON for the streaming path (an alternate smap-json parse strategy) and a fused single-pass compose (an alternate smap-compose implementation, see Alt-2/ADR-013) are revisited only with Phase 12 (## 24) profiling evidence, per the "Deferred" note beneath the ADR table (§9) — they extend Phase 4's and Phase 7's existing scope rather than opening a new phase.

39. Glossary

Terms are defined precisely and implementation-relevantly; each entry ties to a spine type, file, or invariant. Cross-references use italics.

Source Map v3 — the "Source Map Revision 3 Proposal" / TC39 source-map spec; the JSON envelope (version:3 + file/sourceRoot/sources/sourcesContent/names/mappings/ignoreList) modeled by smap-model::SourceMap. The only version this toolkit emits; from_json rejects others via JsonError::BadVersion.

VLQ — Variable-Length Quantity; signed integer encoded in a variable number of Base64 VLQ groups. Used for every field in the mappings string. Sign-bit + magnitude, not two's complement. Owned by smap-vlq.

Base64 VLQ — one VLQ group serialized as one char of the 64-char alphabet A-Za-z0-9+/ (smap-vlq::B64). Each group carries 5 payload bits + the continuation bit. Reverse table maps char → 6-bit value; unknown char → VlqError::InvalidBase64Char.

continuation bit — bit 5 (value 32) of each Base64 VLQ group; 1 = another group follows, 0 = last group. Encoder sets it while value remains after shifting right 5; decoder loops until clear, accumulating into i64 (VlqError::Overflow on >32 magnitude bits).

sign bit — the least-significant bit of the value (before splitting into 5-bit groups), 1 = negative. Distinct from the continuation bit. encode_vlq folds sign in as (v<<1)|sign; decode_vlq extracts it from the final accumulated quantity.

mappings string — the encoded VLQ blob (SourceMap::mappings). Grammar: mappings = group (";" group)* ; group = [segment ("," segment)*] ; segment = field{1}|field{4}|field{5}. Encoded/decoded by smap-codec encode_mappings/decode_mappings.

segment — one mapping entry on a generated line; smap-model::Segment{ gen_column, source:Option<u32>, orig_line, orig_column, name:Option<u32> }. In the wire format a tuple of 1, 4, or 5 VLQ fields (1 = generated-only/unmapped; 4 = mapped no name; 5 = mapped + name). Field count validated; bad count → CodecError::BadFieldCount.

field — one VLQ-encoded integer inside a segment; positionally typed (1=gen col, 2=source, 3=orig line, 4=orig col, 5=name). Each holds a relative delta, not an absolute.

generated position — location in the output (post-transform) file; smap-model::Position{ line, column }, 0-based. The query key for original_position_for.

original position — location in a source input file; carried by OriginalLocation{ source, line, column, name }. The result of original_position_for, the query key for generated_position_for.

generated line/column — the generated position axes. gen_column is Segment field 1; the generated line is implicit = outer DecodedMap index (incremented per ";").

original line/column — Segment's orig_line/orig_column (fields 3/4); index into a source file's text. 0-based internally.

source index — Segment::source (field 2); index into SourceMap::sources / sourcesContent. Interned by SourceMapBuilder::add_source (returns u32); out-of-range → CodecError::SourceIndexOob.

name index — Segment::name (field 5); index into SourceMap::names. Interned by add_name. None ⇒ segment has no field 5.

sources — SourceMap::sources: Vec<Option<String>>; input file paths/URLs, resolved against sourceRoot. None ⇒ anonymous source. Indexed by source index.

sourcesContent — SourceMap::sources_content: Vec<Option<String>>; optional inline copy of each source's text, parallel to sources. Served by source_content_for; embedding governed by Phase 10 policy.

sourceRoot — SourceMap::source_root: Option<String>; prefix prepended to each sources entry to form the resolved URL. Normalization is an API/boundary concern, never inside the codec.

sourceMappingURL — the //# sourceMappingURL=<url> (or legacy //@) trailer comment linking output code to its map. Parsed/emitted by smap-json; extract_source_mapping_url → SourceMapRef.

inline / data: URL map — a sourceMappingURL of form data:application/json;base64,<b64> embedding the whole map. extract_source_mapping_url decodes it to SourceMapRef::Inline(SourceMap) vs SourceMapRef::Url(String).

XSSI prefix — the )]}' cross-site-script-inclusion guard some tools prepend to map JSON. from_json strips a leading )]}'(+optional newline) before parsing; to_json does not emit it by default.

ignoreList — SourceMap::ignore_list: Vec<u32> (serialized as ignoreList, legacy x_google_ignoreList); source indexes of vendored/third-party files devtools should skip when stepping. Populated per Phase 10.

DecodedMap — smap-model::DecodedMap(Vec<Vec<Segment>>); the in-memory dual of the mappings string. Outer index = generated line (0-based), inner Vec = that line's segments in column order. Decoder/encoder pivot type.

SourceMap — the v3 JSON-shaped struct (smap-model); the canonical artifact built by smap-builder/smap-edit, (de)serialized by smap-json, queried by smap-consumer, produced by compose/flatten.

index map / sectioned map — smap-index::IndexedSourceMap{ version, file, sections:[Section] }; a map composed of offset-placed sub-maps instead of a single mappings. Built from concatenated chunk outputs (e.g. one section per bundled module).

section offset — Section{ offset:Position, map:SourceMap }; the generated position at which a sub-map begins. flatten adds offset.line to every section line and offset.column only to the section's first generated line.

composition / chaining — smap-compose::compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen, mid_names): for each segment in mid_to_gen, look its intermediate original position up in orig_to_mid; map gen → the found deepest source/line/col(+name); unmapped intermediate ⇒ emit a 1-field segment. Associative.

remap — smap-compose::remap(chain:&[SourceMap]); folds compose left-to-right over a chain (orig → … → gen) into one SourceMap. Errors surface as CodecError.

flatten — IndexedSourceMap::flatten() -> SourceMap; merges all sections into one flat map applying section offset arithmetic; requires sorted, non-overlapping sections.

bias — smap-consumer::Bias; tie/gap policy for binary search when the query has no exact segment. GreatestLowerBound (default) vs LeastUpperBound.

greatest-lower-bound — GLB; pick the segment with the greatest generated position <= the query. Default bias for original_position_for.

least-upper-bound — LUB; pick the segment with the least generated position >= the query.

SourceMapGenerator — the producer contract realized by smap-builder::SourceMapBuilder: add_source/add_name interning, set_source_content, add_mapping, then into_sourcemap (sorts by (gen_line, gen_column), dedups, encodes mappings). Mirrors Mozilla SourceMapGenerator / gen-mapping.

SourceMapConsumer — the query contract (smap-consumer::SourceMapConsumer): builds sorted by-generated + by-original indexes; original_position_for, generated_position_for, source_content_for, all_generated_positions_for. Mirrors Mozilla SourceMapConsumer / trace-mapping.

MagicString — smap-edit::MagicString; a source editor over an original buffer (overwrite/append/prepend/remove/move/indent) that emits transformed code (to_string) + a correct map (generate_map) via interval bookkeeping + column tracking, without re-parsing. Modeled on Rich Harris's magic-string.

gen-mapping — @jridgewell/gen-mapping; reference modern generator the SourceMapBuilder API mirrors (interning, sort-on-finalize).

trace-mapping — @jridgewell/trace-mapping; reference modern consumer the SourceMapConsumer index + bias search mirror.

relative delta — each numeric field stores the difference from the prior segment's same field, not an absolute. Reset rules (the #1 bug source): gen_column resets to 0 at every ";"; source index, original line/column, name index carry across lines. EncoderState/DecoderState hold prev_* accumulators.

0-based vs 1-based — lines and columns are 0-based everywhere inside the map and codec. Most tools present 1-based lines to users; the ±1 conversion happens only at the API/CLI boundary, never inside smap-codec/smap-consumer.

byte vs UTF-16 column — columns in Source Map v3 count UTF-16 code units (JS string semantics), not bytes or Unicode scalars. Rust String is UTF-8, so smap-edit/builder must convert byte offsets ↔ UTF-16 column counts at the boundary; an unconverted byte column mismaps any non-ASCII line.

41. Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Single-table view of Phases 0–12 (doc headings ## 12–## 24). Durations sum to the Section 1 timeline (24 weeks, single owner: Mujir Qureshi). Risks are the load-bearing failure modes; exit criteria are measurable gates that promote a phase.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
0 ##12 Skeleton Cargo workspace, 10 smap-* crate shells, model types (SourceMap/DecodedMap/Segment/Position), error enums, smap CLI scaffold, CI/Makefile/Dockerfile 1 wk Over-scoped shells → premature API lock; dep-graph cycles between crates cargo build --workspace + cargo test green; "encode one Segment → decode → identical Segment" smoke test passes; CI runs fmt+clippy+test
1 ##13 smap-vlq encode_vlq/decode_vlq, B64 + reverse table, continuation bit (32) + sign bit (LSB), VlqError 1 wk Sign-bit vs two's-complement confusion; i64 overflow on malformed input; panic on bad Base64 char Round-trip prop ∀ i64 in ±2^53; Overflow/InvalidBase64Char(u8)/UnexpectedEof returned not panicked; cargo-fuzz 1h zero panics; no_std+alloc builds
2 ##14 Model + grammar Segment 1/4/5-field shapes; ;/, separators; relative-delta reset rules; DecodedMap; 0-based convention 1 wk Wrong reset semantics (gen_column reset vs carried fields) — the #1 bug class EBNF documented + matches spec; reset table (field1 reset@;, fields2–5 carry) reviewed; DecodedMap invariants stated; 0-based-internal / 1-based-boundary documented
3 ##15 smap-codec encode_mappings/decode_mappings via EncoderState/DecoderState; gen_column reset per ;; field-count validation; CodecError 2 wk Delta-state desync across lines; BadFieldCount/NegativeColumn/SourceIndexOob misclassified; intolerance to real-world maps Round-trip DecodedMap↔mappings on corpus; field-count {1,4,5} enforced; tolerant decode of jridgewell/Sentry samples; differential vs reference codec byte-identical or semantically equal
4 ##16 smap-json v3 envelope (version…ignoreList); from_json/to_json; XSSI )]}' strip; //# sourceMappingURL=; inline data: base64 2 wk version!=3 not rejected; XSSI prefix mishandled; data: URL base64 padding; sources null entries to_json→from_json round-trip; BadVersion/MissingField/Parse surfaced; extract_source_mapping_url handles URL+inline data:; sourcesContent/sourceRoot/ignoreList preserved
5 ##17 smap-builder SourceMapBuilder: add_source/add_name interning, set_source_content, add_mapping; sort by (gen_line,gen_column); dedup; into_sourcemap 2 wk Interner index drift; unsorted/duplicate segments emitted; out-of-order add_mapping Interning returns stable u32 (no dupes); output sorted+deduped; into_sourcemap mappings decode-equal to input; ordering validated; SourceMapGenerator contract documented
6 ##18 smap-consumer SourceMapConsumer: by-gen + by-orig indexes; original_position_for/generated_position_for w/ Bias; source_content_for; all-generated-positions 2 wk GLB/LUB off-by-one; missing-segment → wrong (not None); by-orig index inconsistency Binary-search GLB (default) + LUB verified vs reference consumer; queries O(log n); None on unmapped; differential original_position_for matches Mozilla source-map on corpus
7 ##19 smap-compose compose(orig_to_mid, mid_to_gen, mid_names); remap chain fold; null-segment propagation; names + sourcesContent preservation 2 wk Unmapped mid-position → wrong segment (must emit 1-field); name loss; non-associativity Round-trip-through-chain prop holds; unmapped mid → generated-only segment; remap associative over ≥3-map chains; names follow deepest mapping; differential vs reference compose
8 ##20 smap-index IndexedSourceMap{sections:[Section{offset,map}]}; build from concatenated chunks; flatten(); offset arithmetic 2 wk Overlapping/unsorted sections; column-offset applied to wrong (non-first) line Sections sorted+non-overlapping (invariant enforced); flatten() shifts gen-line by offset.line, gen-col by offset.column only on section's first line; flatten round-trips vs per-section query
9 ##21 smap-edit MagicString overwrite/append/prepend/remove/move/indent; transformed code + correct map w/o re-parse via interval bookkeeping + column tracking 2 wk Interval-overlap corruption; UTF-8/column miscount after edits; map drift vs emitted text to_string() matches edit semantics; generate_map() consumer-verified: every output position maps back to correct original; overlapping-edit errors; no re-parse path
10 ##22 Bundler integration API #264 uses to emit composed maps for concat+minified output; sourcesContent policy; sourceRoot + relative-path normalization; ignoreList for vendored code 2 wk Path normalization breaks sources resolution; sourcesContent bloat; ignoreList indices stale after compose End-to-end: #264-style concat+minify → single valid map; paths normalized vs sourceRoot; ignoreList survives compose/flatten; embedding policy documented + size-bounded
11 ##23 Validation/conformance/fuzz Validate vs v3 spec + source-map/@jridgewell/sourcemap-codec corpora; differential decode/encode/query; fuzz VLQ+JSON+mappings; round-trip + composition props 2 wk Silent divergence from reference; fuzz panic/OOM; non-canonical-but-valid maps rejected Differential parity on full corpus; cargo-fuzz ≥24h zero panics/OOM on VLQ+JSON+mappings targets; round-trip + compose property tests in CI; conformance report published
12 ##24 Performance/scale Encode/decode millions of segments; zero-copy JSON; table-driven Base64 + byte-at-a-time decode + pre-sized buffers; memory ceilings; streaming; criterion benches 1 wk Allocation blowup on large maps; throughput regressions; GC-free but cache-unfriendly layout Decode ≥1M segments within target (e.g. ≥50 MB/s mappings, <2× JSON size in RAM); zero-copy JSON paths confirmed; criterion benches vs locked targets in CI w/ regression gate

Total: 24 weeks (1+1+1+2+2+2+2+2+2+2+2+2+1), single owner.

"Phase-0 done" vs "full done." Phase-0 done means the workspace compiles: all 10 smap-* crates exist as shells with the locked model types (SourceMap/DecodedMap/Segment/Position), error enums, the smap CLI scaffold, and CI/Makefile/Dockerfile in place — and the single round-trip smoke test (encode one Segment → decode → identical Segment) passes. It is a skeleton: no real VLQ codec, JSON, builder, consumer, composition, index, or editor behavior yet. Full done means every phase's exit criteria are met and the toolkit delivers its locked identity — Base64 VLQ codec; the v3 model + mappings state machine; v3 JSON envelope + sourceMappingURL/inline data: handling; the SourceMapBuilder generator and SourceMapConsumer query (Bias GLB/LUB) APIs; correct compose/remap chaining with null-segment propagation; sectioned IndexedSourceMap+flatten; the MagicString editor; #264 bundler integration — all validated differentially against the reference corpora, fuzzed panic-free for ≥24h, and meeting the Phase-12 million-segment performance targets. Non-goals (JS parsing/transform, minification, AST, devtools UI, the deferred wasm DWARF extension) remain explicitly out of scope at full done.
